```go
package security

import (
	"bytes"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

// writeFile creates path (and its parents) with content.
func writeFile(t *testing.T, path, content string) {
	t.Helper()
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(path), 0o755); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
}

// newTestPolicy returns a policy over fresh project and home dirs whose
// warnings go to the returned buffer.
func newTestPolicy(t *testing.T) (p *Policy, project, home string, warn *bytes.Buffer) {
	t.Helper()
	project, home = t.TempDir(), t.TempDir()
	warn = &bytes.Buffer{}
	p = NewPolicy(project, home)
	p.warn = warn
	return p, project, home, warn
}

func localSettings(project string) string {
	return filepath.Join(project, ".claude", "settings.local.json")
}
func sharedSettings(project string) string {
	return filepath.Join(project, ".claude", "settings.json")
}
func homeSettings(home string) string { return filepath.Join(home, ".claude", "settings.json") }

func TestGlobToRegex(t *testing.T) {
	// Cases from tests/security.test.ts:49-114.
	cases := []struct {
		glob, cmd  string
		fold, want bool
	}{
		{"ls *", "ls -la", false, true},
		{"ls *", "lsof -i", false, false},
		{"ls*", "lsof -i", false, true},
		{"ls*", "ls -la", false, true},
		{"git *", `git commit -m "msg"`, false, true},
		{"* commit *", `git commit -m "msg"`, false, true},
		{"tree:*", "tree", false, true},
		{"tree:*", "tree -a", false, true},
		{"tree:*", "treemap", false, false},
		{"sudo *", "sudo apt install", false, true},
		{"sudo *", "sudoedit", false, false},
		{"rm -rf /*", "rm -rf /etc", false, true},
		{"chmod -R 777 *", "chmod -R 777 /tmp", false, true},
		{"dir *", "DIR /W", true, true},
		{"dir *", "DIR /W", false, false},
		{"echo (x)", "echo (x)", false, true},
		{"a.b", "axb", false, false},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		if got := globToRegex(c.glob, c.fold).MatchString(c.cmd); got != c.want {
			t.Errorf("globToRegex(%q, %v).Match(%q) = %v, want %v", c.glob, c.fold, c.cmd, got, c.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestFileGlobToRegex(t *testing.T) {
	// Cases from tests/security.test.ts:522-546.
	cases := []struct {

	}{

		{".env", "src/.env", false},
		{"**/.env", "deep/nested/.env", true},
		{"**/.env", ".env", true},
		{"**/*credentials*", "secrets/credentials.json", true},
		{"**/*credentials*", "readme.md", false},
		{"/home/u/.ssh/**", "/home/u/.ssh/id_rsa", true},
		{"src/*.go", "src/a/b.go", false},
		{"file?.txt", "file1.txt", true},
		{"file?.txt", "file/.txt", false},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		if got := fileGlobToRegex(c.glob, false).MatchString(c.path); got != c.want {
			t.Errorf("fileGlobToRegex(%q).Match(%q) = %v, want %v", c.glob, c.path, got, c.want)

func TestRulesOrderAndFiltering(t *testing.T) {

		t.Errorf("shared rules = %+v", rs[1])

	if len(rs[2].bashDeny) != 1 || rs[2].bashDeny[0].pattern != "Bash(sudo *)" || rs[2].readDeny[0].glob != "**/*.pem" {
		t.Errorf("home rules = %+v", rs[2])

func TestRulesMissingFilesAreSilent(t *testing.T) {

	if rs := p.rules(); len(rs) != 0 {

func TestRulesMalformedJSONSkipped(t *testing.T) {
	p, project, home, warn := newTestPolicy(t)
	writeFile(t, localSettings(project), `{"permissions":{"deny":["Bash(sudo *)"`)
	writeFile(t, homeSettings(home), `{"permissions":{"deny":["Bash(rm *)"]}}`)

	for range 2 {
		rs := p.rules()
		if len(rs) != 1 || rs[0].bashDeny[0].pattern != "Bash(rm *)" {
			t.Fatalf("rules = %+v, want only the home file", rs)
		}
	}
	if n := strings.Count(warn.String(), "\n"); n != 1 || !strings.Contains(warn.String(), localSettings(project)) {
		t.Errorf("want one warning naming the bad file, got %q", warn.String())
	}
}

func TestRulesCacheInvalidation(t *testing.T) {
	p, _, home, _ := newTestPolicy(t)
	path := homeSettings(home)
	old := time.Now().Add(-time.Hour).Truncate(time.Second)
	writeFile(t, path, `{"permissions":{"deny":["Bash(aaaa *)"]}}`)
	if err := os.Chtimes(path, old, old); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if got := p.rules()[0].bashDeny[0].pattern; got != "Bash(aaaa *)" {
		t.Fatalf("first read = %q", got)
	}

	// Same size and mtime: the cached rules are served without re-reading.
	writeFile(t, path, `{"permissions":{"deny":["Bash(bbbb *)"]}}`)
	if err := os.Chtimes(path, old, old); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if got := p.rules()[0].bashDeny[0].pattern; got != "Bash(aaaa *)" {
		t.Fatalf("cache hit = %q, want stale Bash(aaaa *)", got)
	}

	// New mtime: re-parsed.
	newer := old.Add(time.Minute)
	if err := os.Chtimes(path, newer, newer); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if got := p.rules()[0].bashDeny[0].pattern; got != "Bash(bbbb *)" {
		t.Fatalf("after mtime change = %q, want Bash(bbbb *)", got)
	}

	// Deleted: dropped.
	if err := os.Remove(path); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if rs := p.rules(); len(rs) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("after delete got %d rule sets", len(rs))
	}
}

`internal/security/settings.go`:

```go
// Package security ports context-mode's server-side deny firewall
// (src/security.ts and its wiring in src/server.ts).
package security

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"regexp"
	"runtime"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"time"
)

// caseInsensitive mirrors TS `process.platform === "darwin"` (win32 dropped).
// It applies to Bash deny, the project boundary, Read allow and Read deny.
var caseInsensitive = runtime.GOOS == "darwin"

// bashRule is one `Bash(glob)` deny entry; pattern is the full entry text.
type bashRule struct {
	pattern string
	re      *regexp.Regexp
}

// fileRule is one `Read(glob)` entry; glob is the text inside the parens.
type fileRule struct {
	glob string
	re   *regexp.Regexp
}

// fileRules holds the rules parsed from one settings file.
type fileRules struct {
	bashDeny  []bashRule
	readDeny  []fileRule
	readAllow []fileRule
}

type cacheEntry struct {
	mtime time.Time
	size  int64
	rules *fileRules // nil when the file could not be read or parsed
}

// Policy evaluates commands and paths against Claude Code settings files.
type Policy struct {
	projectDir string
	home       string
	warn       io.Writer

	mu    sync.Mutex
	cache map[string]cacheEntry
}

// NewPolicy returns a Policy reading settings under projectDir and home.
// An empty projectDir or home skips the files rooted there.
func NewPolicy(projectDir, home string) *Policy {
	return &Policy{projectDir: projectDir, home: home, warn: os.Stderr, cache: map[string]cacheEntry{}}
}

// settingsPaths ports readBashPolicies / readToolPermissionPatterns file order
// (security.ts:363-394, 426-490). Only the claude global is read: adapter
// globals and CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR are dropped (spec §4.5).
func (p *Policy) settingsPaths() []string {
	var paths []string
	if p.projectDir != "" {
		paths = append(paths,
			filepath.Join(p.projectDir, ".claude", "settings.local.json"),
			filepath.Join(p.projectDir, ".claude", "settings.json"))
	}
	if p.home != "" {
		paths = append(paths, filepath.Join(p.home, ".claude", "settings.json"))
	}
	return paths
}

// rules returns the parsed rules of every readable settings file in
// precedence order. Each file costs one stat; it is re-parsed only when its
// mtime or size changes.
func (p *Policy) rules() []*fileRules {
	p.mu.Lock()
	defer p.mu.Unlock()
	var out []*fileRules
	for _, path := range p.settingsPaths() {
		fi, err := os.Stat(path)
		if err != nil {
			delete(p.cache, path)
			continue // missing file: silently skipped, as in TS
		}
		e, ok := p.cache[path]
		if !ok || !e.mtime.Equal(fi.ModTime()) || e.size != fi.Size() {
			e = cacheEntry{mtime: fi.ModTime(), size: fi.Size()}
			r, err := parseSettingsFile(path)
			if err != nil {
				// Fail-open like TS; warn once per file version, not per call.
				fmt.Fprintf(p.warn, "context-mode: ignoring settings file %s: %v\n", path, err)
			} else {
				e.rules = r
			}
			p.cache[path] = e
		}
		if e.rules != nil {
			out = append(out, e.rules)
		}
	}
	return out
}

// toolPattern ports parseToolPattern (security.ts:37-44).
var toolPattern = regexp.MustCompile(`^(\w+)\((.+)\)$`)

// parseSettingsFile ports readSingleSettings + readToolPermissionPatterns
// extractGlobs (security.ts:321-351, 434-461): only permissions.deny
// Bash()/Read() and permissions.allow Read() entries are kept.
func parseSettingsFile(path string) (*fileRules, error) {
	raw, err := os.ReadFile(path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	var doc any
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &doc); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	root, _ := doc.(map[string]any)
	perms, _ := root["permissions"].(map[string]any)
	r := &fileRules{}
	for _, entry := range stringsOf(perms["deny"]) {
		m := toolPattern.FindStringSubmatch(entry)
		if m == nil {
			continue
		}
		switch m[1] {
		case "Bash":
			r.bashDeny = append(r.bashDeny, bashRule{pattern: entry, re: globToRegex(m[2], caseInsensitive)})
		case "Read":
			// Intentional difference from TS (server.ts:1222 folds only on win32): fold on darwin so .ENV cannot bypass Read(.env) on APFS.
			r.readDeny = append(r.readDeny, fileRule{glob: m[2], re: fileGlobToRegex(m[2], caseInsensitive)})
		}
	}
	for _, entry := range stringsOf(perms["allow"]) {
		if m := toolPattern.FindStringSubmatch(entry); m != nil && m[1] == "Read" {
			r.readAllow = append(r.readAllow, fileRule{glob: m[2], re: fileGlobToRegex(m[2], caseInsensitive)})
		}
	}
	return r, nil
}

// stringsOf returns the string elements of a JSON array, skipping the rest.
func stringsOf(v any) []string {
	arr, _ := v.([]any)
	var out []string
	for _, x := range arr {
		if s, ok := x.(string); ok {
			out = append(out, s)
		}
	}
	return out
}

// globToRegex ports security.ts:69-90. "cmd:args" becomes ^cmd(\s args)?$;
// otherwise * becomes .* and everything else is literal.
func globToRegex(glob string, fold bool) *regexp.Regexp {
	var expr string
	if cmd, args, ok := strings.Cut(glob, ":"); ok {
		expr = `^` + regexp.QuoteMeta(cmd) + `(\s` + convertGlobPart(args) + `)?$`
	} else {
		expr = `^` + convertGlobPart(glob) + `$`
	}
	if fold {
		expr = `(?i)` + expr
	}
	return regexp.MustCompile(expr)
}

// convertGlobPart ports security.ts:56-60.
func convertGlobPart(glob string) string {
	return strings.ReplaceAll(regexp.QuoteMeta(glob), `\*`, `.*`)
}

// fileGlobToRegex ports security.ts:101-135: ** spans directories, * and ?
// stay within one path segment.
func fileGlobToRegex(glob string, fold bool) *regexp.Regexp {
	var b strings.Builder
	for i := 0; i < len(glob); {
		switch {
		case glob[i] == '*' && i+1 < len(glob) && glob[i+1] == '*':
			if i+2 < len(glob) && glob[i+2] == '/' {
				b.WriteString(`(.*/)?`)
				i += 3
			} else {
				b.WriteString(`.*`)
				i += 2
			}
		case glob[i] == '*':
			b.WriteString(`[^/]*`)
			i++
		case glob[i] == '?':
			b.WriteString(`[^/]`)
			i++
		default:
			b.WriteString(regexp.QuoteMeta(glob[i : i+1]))
			i++
		}
	}
	expr := `^` + b.String() + `$`
	if fold {
		expr = `(?i)` + expr
	}
	return regexp.MustCompile(expr)
}
```

```go
package security

import "strings"

// BlockedMessage is the TS deny text for a shell command (server.ts:1122).
// pattern is the full settings entry, e.g. "Bash(sudo *)".
func BlockedMessage(pattern string) string {
	return "Command blocked by security policy: matches deny pattern " + pattern
}

// CheckCommand ports evaluateCommandDenyOnly (security.ts:573-588): every
// chained segment and every nested subshell is matched against the Bash deny
// rules of each settings file in precedence order; the first match wins.
func (p *Policy) CheckCommand(cmd string) (blocked bool, pattern string) {
	return matchCommand(p.rules(), cmd)
}

// matchCommand is CheckCommand over already-loaded rules.
func matchCommand(rules []*fileRules, cmd string) (blocked bool, pattern string) {
	for _, el := range collectCommandElements(cmd) {
		for _, r := range rules {
			for _, d := range r.bashDeny {
				if d.re.MatchString(el) {
					return true, d.pattern
				}
			}
		}
	}
	return false, ""
}

// isEscaped ports security.ts:158-164: an odd run of backslashes before i.
func isEscaped(s string, i int) bool {
	n := 0
	for j := i - 1; j >= 0 && s[j] == '\\'; j-- {
		n++
	}
	return n%2 == 1
}

// splitChainedCommands ports security.ts:174-237. It splits on && || ; | & and
// newlines outside quotes, backticks and $(...). Branch order is kept verbatim:
// && and || ignore escaping, as in TS.
func splitChainedCommands(command string) []string {
	var parts []string
	var cur strings.Builder
	inSingle, inDouble, inBacktick := false, false, false
	depth := 0
	flush := func() {
		parts = append(parts, strings.TrimSpace(cur.String()))
		cur.Reset()
	}
	next := func(i int) byte {
		if i+1 < len(command) {
			return command[i+1]
		}
		return 0
	}
	for i := 0; i < len(command); i++ {
		ch := command[i]
		escaped := isEscaped(command, i)
		switch {
		case ch == '\'' && !inDouble && !inBacktick && !escaped:
			inSingle = !inSingle
			cur.WriteByte(ch)
		case ch == '"' && !inSingle && !inBacktick && !escaped:
			inDouble = !inDouble
			cur.WriteByte(ch)
		case ch == '`' && !inSingle && !inDouble && !escaped:
			inBacktick = !inBacktick
			cur.WriteByte(ch)
		case !inSingle && !inDouble && !inBacktick:
			switch {
			case ch == '$' && next(i) == '(' && !escaped:
				depth++
				cur.WriteString("$(")
				i++
			case depth > 0 && ch == '(' && !escaped:
				depth++
				cur.WriteByte(ch)
			case ch == ')' && depth > 0 && !escaped:
				depth--
				cur.WriteByte(ch)
			case depth == 0 && (ch == ';' || ch == '\n' || ch == '\r') && !escaped:
				flush()
			case depth == 0 && ch == '|' && next(i) == '|':
				flush()
				i++
			case depth == 0 && ch == '&' && next(i) == '&':
				flush()
				i++
			case depth == 0 && ch == '&' && !escaped:
				flush()
			case depth == 0 && ch == '|':
				flush()
			default:
				cur.WriteByte(ch)
			}
		default:
			cur.WriteByte(ch)
		}
	}
	flush()
	out := parts[:0]
	for _, s := range parts {
		if s != "" {
			out = append(out, s)
		}
	}
	return out
}

// extractSubshellCommands ports security.ts:243-302: the bodies of $(...) and
// backtick substitutions outside single quotes. Backtick bodies are expanded
// recursively here; $(...) bodies are recursed by collectCommandElements.
// Arithmetic $(( is not a substitution, but substitutions inside it are found.
func extractSubshellCommands(command string) []string {
	var subs []string
	inSingle, inDouble := false, false
	backtickStart := -1
	var starts, depths []int
	parenDepth := 0
	at := func(i int) byte {
		if i < len(command) {
			return command[i]
		}
		return 0
	}
	for i := 0; i < len(command); i++ {
		ch := command[i]
		escaped := isEscaped(command, i)
		switch {
		case ch == '\'' && !inDouble && backtickStart == -1 && !escaped:
			inSingle = !inSingle
		case ch == '"' && !inSingle && backtickStart == -1 && !escaped:
			inDouble = !inDouble
		case ch == '`' && !inSingle && !inDouble && !escaped:
			if backtickStart == -1 {
				backtickStart = i + 1
			} else {
				sub := command[backtickStart:i]
				subs = append(subs, sub)
				subs = append(subs, extractSubshellCommands(sub)...)
				backtickStart = -1
			}
		case !inSingle && backtickStart == -1:
			switch {
			case ch == '$' && at(i+1) == '(' && !escaped:
				if at(i+2) == '(' {
					parenDepth += 2
					i += 2
				} else {
					starts = append(starts, i+2)
					depths = append(depths, parenDepth)
					parenDepth++
					i++
				}
			case ch == '(' && !escaped:
				parenDepth++
			case ch == ')' && !escaped:
				if parenDepth > 0 {
					parenDepth--
				}
				if n := len(depths); n > 0 && parenDepth == depths[n-1] {
					start := starts[n-1]
					depths, starts = depths[:n-1], starts[:n-1]
					subs = append(subs, command[start:i])
				}
			}
		}
	}
	return subs
}

// collectCommandElements ports security.ts:304-314.
func collectCommandElements(command string) []string {
	var els []string
	for _, seg := range splitChainedCommands(command) {
		els = append(els, seg)
		for _, sub := range extractSubshellCommands(seg) {
			els = append(els, collectCommandElements(sub)...)
		}
	}
	return els
}
```

```go
package security

import (
	"regexp"
	"strings"
)

// Quote alternations replace the JS backreference form (['"])(.*?)\1, which
// RE2 lacks. Leftmost-first submatching makes the lazy bodies equivalent.
const (
	q2 = `(?:'(.*?)'|"(.*?)")`
	q3 = `(?:'(.*?)'|"(.*?)"|` + "`(.*?)`" + `)`
)

var jsShellEscapes = []*regexp.Regexp{
	regexp.MustCompile(`exec(?:Sync|File|FileSync)?\(\s*` + q3),
	regexp.MustCompile(`spawn(?:Sync)?\(\s*` + q3),
}

// shellEscapePatterns ports SHELL_ESCAPE_PATTERNS (security.ts:795-825).
var shellEscapePatterns = map[string][]*regexp.Regexp{
	"python": {
		regexp.MustCompile(`os\.system\(\s*` + q2 + `\s*\)`),
		regexp.MustCompile(`subprocess\.(?:run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)\(\s*` + q2),
	},
	"javascript": jsShellEscapes,
	"typescript": jsShellEscapes,
	"ruby": {
		regexp.MustCompile(`system\(\s*` + q2),
		regexp.MustCompile("`(.*?)`"),
	},
	"go": {
		regexp.MustCompile(`exec\.Command\(\s*` + q3),
	},
	"php": {
		regexp.MustCompile(`shell_exec\(\s*` + q3),
		regexp.MustCompile(`(?:^|[^.])exec\(\s*` + q3),
		regexp.MustCompile(`(?:^|[^.])system\(\s*` + q3),
		regexp.MustCompile(`passthru\(\s*` + q3),
		regexp.MustCompile(`proc_open\(\s*` + q3),
	},
	"rust": {
		regexp.MustCompile(`Command::new\(\s*` + q3),
	},
}

var (
	pySubprocessList = regexp.MustCompile(`subprocess\.(?:run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)\(\s*\[([^\]]+)\]`)
	quotedString     = regexp.MustCompile(q2)
)

// firstGroup returns the first non-empty capture group (the command body).
func firstGroup(m []string) string {
	for _, g := range m[1:] {
		if g != "" {
			return g
		}
	}
	return ""
}

// extractShellCommands ports security.ts:858-889 (with the Python list form,
// security.ts:834-849): subprocess.run(["rm", "-rf", "/"]) gives "rm -rf /".
func extractShellCommands(code, language string) []string {
	var cmds []string
	for _, re := range shellEscapePatterns[language] {
		for _, m := range re.FindAllStringSubmatch(code, -1) {
			if c := firstGroup(m); c != "" {
				cmds = append(cmds, c)
			}
		}
	}
	if language == "python" {
		for _, m := range pySubprocessList.FindAllStringSubmatch(code, -1) {
			var args []string
			for _, a := range quotedString.FindAllStringSubmatch(m[1], -1) {
				args = append(args, a[1]+a[2])
			}
			if len(args) > 0 {
				cmds = append(cmds, strings.Join(args, " "))
			}
		}
	}
	return cmds
}

// EmbeddedBlockedMessage is the TS deny text for a shell command found inside
// non-shell code (server.ts:1151).
func EmbeddedBlockedMessage(cmd, pattern string) string {
	return `Command blocked by security policy: embedded shell command "` + cmd + `" matches deny pattern ` + pattern
}

// CheckCode ports the language dispatch at server.ts:1745-1750: shell code is
// checked as a command; other languages are scanned for embedded commands.
// Use CheckCodeMatch when the message must name the embedded command.
func (p *Policy) CheckCode(language, code string) (blocked bool, pattern string) {
	blocked, _, pattern = p.CheckCodeMatch(language, code)
	return blocked, pattern
}

// CheckCodeMatch is CheckCode that also returns the embedded command that
// matched ("" for shell). Callers build the text with BlockedMessage when cmd
// is "" and EmbeddedBlockedMessage otherwise (checkNonShellDenyPolicy,
// server.ts:1137-1162).
func (p *Policy) CheckCodeMatch(language, code string) (blocked bool, cmd, pattern string) {
	if language == "shell" {
		blocked, pattern = p.CheckCommand(code)
		return blocked, "", pattern
	}
	cmds := extractShellCommands(code, language)
	if len(cmds) == 0 {
		return false, "", ""
	}
	rules := p.rules() // one stat per settings file per call
	for _, c := range cmds {
		if blocked, pattern = matchCommand(rules, c); blocked {
			return true, c, pattern
		}
	}
	return false, "", ""
}
```

package security

import (
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"runtime"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

func realpath(t *testing.T, p string) string {
	t.Helper()
	r, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(p)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return r
}

func symlink(t *testing.T, target, link string) {
	t.Helper()
	if err := os.Symlink(target, link); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
}

func boundaryMessage(path, project string) string {
	return `File access blocked: "` + path + `" resolves outside the project root (` + project +
		`). context-mode confines ctx_execute_file to the workspace so it cannot be used to bypass ` +
		`the host's sandbox/permission controls (issue #852). To intentionally process a file outside ` +
		`the project, add a host allow rule, e.g. "permissions": { "allow": ["Read(` + path + `)"] } in your settings.`
}

func denyMessage(glob string) string {
	return "File access blocked by security policy: path matches Read deny pattern " + glob
}

// checkErr asserts CheckReadPath(path) returns nil (want == "") or exactly want.
func checkErr(t *testing.T, p *Policy, path, want string) {
	t.Helper()
	err := p.CheckReadPath(path)
	switch {
	case want == "" && err != nil:
		t.Errorf("CheckReadPath(%q) = %q, want nil", path, err)
	case want != "" && (err == nil || err.Error() != want):
		t.Errorf("CheckReadPath(%q) = %v,\nwant %q", path, err, want)
	}
}

func TestCheckReadPathBoundary(t *testing.T) {
	p, project, _, _ := newTestPolicy(t)
	outside := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(project), "outside-"+filepath.Base(project))
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(project, "data", "in.txt"), "x")
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(outside, "secret.txt"), "s")
	symlink(t, filepath.Join(outside, "secret.txt"), filepath.Join(project, "escape.txt"))
	symlink(t, filepath.Join(project, "data", "in.txt"), filepath.Join(project, "alias.txt"))

	checkErr(t, p, "data/in.txt", "")
	checkErr(t, p, filepath.Join(project, "data", "in.txt"), "")
	checkErr(t, p, ".", "")
	checkErr(t, p, "not/created/yet.txt", "")
	checkErr(t, p, "alias.txt", "")
	checkErr(t, p, "data/../data/in.txt", "")

	up := "../" + filepath.Base(outside) + "/secret.txt"
	checkErr(t, p, up, boundaryMessage(up, project))
	abs := filepath.Join(outside, "secret.txt")
	checkErr(t, p, abs, boundaryMessage(abs, project))
	checkErr(t, p, "/etc/passwd", boundaryMessage("/etc/passwd", project))
	checkErr(t, p, "escape.txt", boundaryMessage("escape.txt", project))
}

func TestCheckReadPathAllowRule(t *testing.T) {
	p, project, home, _ := newTestPolicy(t)
	outside := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(project), "outside-"+filepath.Base(project))
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(outside, "log.txt"), "l")
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(outside, "other", "x.txt"), "x")
	symlink(t, filepath.Join(outside, "log.txt"), filepath.Join(project, "log-link.txt"))
	writeFile(t, homeSettings(home), `{"permissions":{"allow":["Read(`+outside+`/*.txt)"]}}`)

	checkErr(t, p, filepath.Join(outside, "log.txt"), "")
	checkErr(t, p, "../"+filepath.Base(outside)+"/log.txt", "")
	// The symlink's candidates are "log-link.txt", <project>/log-link.txt and the
	// target's realpath. The allow glob names the unresolved dir, so on macOS
	// (/var -> /private/var) only an allow rule on the realpath would admit it.
	if realpath(t, outside) == outside {
		checkErr(t, p, "log-link.txt", "")
	} else {
		checkErr(t, p, "log-link.txt", boundaryMessage("log-link.txt", project))
	}
	// * does not cross "/", so a deeper file is still outside and not allowed.
	deep := filepath.Join(outside, "other", "x.txt")
	checkErr(t, p, deep, boundaryMessage(deep, project))
}

func TestCheckReadPathDenyCandidates(t *testing.T) {
	p, project, home, _ := newTestPolicy(t)
	realProject := realpath(t, project)
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(project, ".env"), "e")
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(project, "secrets", "key"), "k")
	writeFile(t, filepath.Join(project, "secrets", "real-only"), "r")
	symlink(t, filepath.Join(project, "secrets", "real-only"), filepath.Join(project, "innocent.txt"))

	writeFile(t, homeSettings(home), `{"permissions":{"deny":["Read(`+realProject+`/secrets/real-only)"]}}`)

	checkErr(t, p, ".env", denyMessage(".env"))                                   // raw
	checkErr(t, p, "secrets/../secrets/key", denyMessage(project+"/secrets/key")) // resolved
	checkErr(t, p, "innocent.txt", denyMessage(realProject+"/secrets/real-only")) // realpath
	checkErr(t, p, filepath.Join(project, ".env"), "")                            // ".env" glob is anchored
}

func TestCheckReadPathDenyDarwinCaseFolding(t *testing.T) {
	if runtime.GOOS != "darwin" {
		t.Skip("case folding applies on darwin only")
	}
	p, project, _, _ := newTestPolicy(t)
	writeFile(t, sharedSettings(project), `{"permissions":{"deny":["Read(.env)","Read(**/.env)"]}}`)

	// ".ENV" resolves to <project>/.ENV; the anchored ".env" glob matches its raw form.
	checkErr(t, p, ".ENV", denyMessage(".env"))

	checkErr(t, p, filepath.Join(project, ".ENV"), denyMessage("**/.env"))

	checkErr(t, p, "notes.txt", denyMessage(realOutside+"/id_rsa"))

func TestCheckReadPathNoProjectDir(t *testing.T) {
	home := t.TempDir()
	p := NewPolicy("", home)
	writeFile(t, homeSettings(home), `{"permissions":{"deny":["Read(/etc/passwd)"]}}`)

	if runtime.GOOS != "darwin" {
		t.Skip("case folding applies on darwin only")
	}
	p, project, home, _ := newTestPolicy(t)

	checkErr(t, p, filepath.Join(outside, "b.txt"), "")   // allow folds case

`internal/security/readpath.go`:

```go
package security

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
)

// CheckReadPath ports the ctx_execute_file path checks (server.ts:2118-2123):
// first the project boundary with its Read() allow exception
// (checkProjectBoundary, server.ts:1184-1209), then the Read() deny rules
// (checkFilePathDenyPolicy, server.ts:1215-1241). The error text is the TS
// message. projectDir is expected to be absolute (config.Load: config.go:46);
// an empty projectDir disables the boundary, as TS does for an unknown root.
func (p *Policy) CheckReadPath(path string) error {
	rules := p.rules()
	cands := pathCandidates(path, p.projectDir)
	if !isPathInsideProject(path, p.projectDir, caseInsensitive) {
		if _, ok := matchFileRules(cands, rules, func(r *fileRules) []fileRule { return r.readAllow }); !ok {
			return fmt.Errorf(`File access blocked: "%s" resolves outside the project root `+
				`(%s). context-mode confines ctx_execute_file to the workspace so it `+
				`cannot be used to bypass the host's sandbox/permission controls (issue #852). `+
				`To intentionally process a file outside the project, add a host allow rule, `+
				`e.g. "permissions": { "allow": ["Read(%s)"] } in your settings.`, path, p.projectDir, path)
		}
	}
	if glob, ok := matchFileRules(cands, rules, func(r *fileRules) []fileRule { return r.readDeny }); ok {
		return errors.New("File access blocked by security policy: path matches Read deny pattern " + glob)
	}
	return nil
}

// resolveIn mirrors node's path.resolve(root, p) for an absolute root.
func resolveIn(root, p string) string {
	if filepath.IsAbs(p) {
		return filepath.Clean(p)
	}
	return filepath.Join(root, p)
}

// pathCandidates ports the candidate set of evaluateFilePath
// (security.ts:628-638): the raw input, the path resolved against the project
// root, and its realpath when it exists. Backslash normalisation is dropped
// with Windows support.
func pathCandidates(path, root string) []string {
	cands := []string{path}
	if root == "" {
		return cands
	}
	lexical := resolveIn(root, path)
	cands = append(cands, lexical)
	if real, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(lexical); err == nil {
		cands = append(cands, real)
	}
	return cands
}

// matchFileRules ports the matching loop of evaluateFilePath
// (security.ts:640-655): settings files in order, then rules, then candidates.
// It returns the first matching glob.
func matchFileRules(cands []string, rules []*fileRules, pick func(*fileRules) []fileRule) (string, bool) {
	for _, r := range rules {
		for _, fr := range pick(r) {
			for _, c := range cands {
				if fr.re.MatchString(c) {
					return fr.glob, true
				}
			}
		}
	}
	return "", false
}

// isPathInsideProject ports security.ts:686-730: the resolved path must be
// inside root, and when both exist their realpaths must be too (symlink escape).
func isPathInsideProject(path, root string, fold bool) bool {
	if root == "" {
		return true
	}
	root = filepath.Clean(root)
	lexical := resolveIn(root, path)
	if !within(root, lexical, fold) {
		return false
	}
	realRoot, err1 := filepath.EvalSymlinks(root)
	real, err2 := filepath.EvalSymlinks(lexical)
	if err1 == nil && err2 == nil && !within(realRoot, real, fold) {
		return false
	}
	return true
}

// within ports the inner helper of security.ts:696-713.
func within(root, cand string, fold bool) bool {
	if fold {
		root, cand = strings.ToLower(root), strings.ToLower(cand)
	}
	if root == cand {
		return true
	}
	rel, err := filepath.Rel(root, cand)
	if err != nil {
		return false
	}
	return rel != ".." && !strings.HasPrefix(rel, ".."+string(filepath.Separator))
}
```

```go
package executor

import (
	"errors"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"reflect"
	"testing"
)

// fakeExec returns an Executor whose PATH contains exactly the given names (resolved to /bin/<name>).
func fakeExec(env map[string]string, onPath ...string) *Executor {
	e := New("/proj")
	have := map[string]bool{}
	for _, n := range onPath {
		have[n] = true
	}
	e.lookPath = func(n string) (string, error) {
		if have[n] {
			return "/fake/" + n, nil
		}
		return "", errors.New("not found")
	}
	e.getenv = func(k string) string { return env[k] }
	return e
}

func TestLanguagesEnum(t *testing.T) {
	want := []Language{"javascript", "typescript", "python", "shell", "ruby", "go", "rust", "php", "perl", "r", "elixir", "csharp"}
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(Languages, want) {
		t.Fatalf("Languages = %v", Languages)
	}
}

func TestCommandArgv(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		name   string

		lang   Language

		{"js bun", []string{"bun", "node"}, JavaScript, []string{"/fake/bun", "run", "f"}},
		{"js node", []string{"node"}, JavaScript, []string{"/fake/node", "f"}},
		{"ts bun", []string{"bun", "tsx"}, TypeScript, []string{"/fake/bun", "run", "f"}},
		{"ts tsx", []string{"tsx", "ts-node"}, TypeScript, []string{"/fake/tsx", "f"}},
		{"ts ts-node", []string{"ts-node"}, TypeScript, []string{"/fake/ts-node", "f"}},
		{"python3", []string{"python3", "python"}, Python, []string{"/fake/python3", "f"}},
		{"python", []string{"python"}, Python, []string{"/fake/python", "f"}},
		{"shell bash", []string{"bash"}, Shell, []string{"/fake/bash", "f"}},
		{"shell sh", nil, Shell, []string{"sh", "f"}},
		{"go", []string{"go"}, Go, []string{"/fake/go", "run", "f"}},
		{"rust", []string{"rustc"}, Rust, []string{"/fake/rustc", "f"}},
		{"r Rscript", []string{"Rscript", "R"}, R, []string{"/fake/Rscript", "f"}},
		{"r R", []string{"R"}, R, []string{"/fake/R", "f"}},
		{"csharp", []string{"dotnet-script"}, CSharp, []string{"/fake/dotnet-script", "f"}},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got, err := fakeExec(nil, c.onPath...).command(c.lang, "f")
			if err != nil || !reflect.DeepEqual(got, c.want) {
				t.Fatalf("command = %v, %v; want %v", got, err, c.want)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestShellHonorsAllowlistedSHELL(t *testing.T) {

func TestBunFallbackPath(t *testing.T) {

func TestMissingRuntimeErrors(t *testing.T) {

func TestRuntimeCached(t *testing.T) {
	e := fakeExec(nil, "python3")
	calls := 0
	look := e.lookPath
	e.lookPath = func(n string) (string, error) { calls++; return look(n) }
	e.command(Python, "a")
	e.command(Python, "b")
	if calls != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("lookPath called %d times; want 1", calls)
	}
}

`internal/executor/runtime.go`:

```go
// Package executor runs ctx_execute / ctx_execute_file code in a sandboxed subprocess.
package executor

import (
	"errors"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"sync"
)

// Language is one of the 12 languages ctx_execute accepts.
type Language string

const (
	JavaScript Language = "javascript"
	TypeScript Language = "typescript"
	Python     Language = "python"
	Shell      Language = "shell"
	Ruby       Language = "ruby"
	Go         Language = "go"
	Rust       Language = "rust"
	PHP        Language = "php"
	Perl       Language = "perl"
	R          Language = "r"
	Elixir     Language = "elixir"
	CSharp     Language = "csharp"
)

// Languages is the ctx_execute language enum, in schema order.
var Languages = []Language{JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Shell, Ruby, Go, Rust, PHP, Perl, R, Elixir, CSharp}

// OutputHardCap is the combined stdout+stderr limit; past it the process group is killed.
const OutputHardCap = 100 << 20

// Executor runs scripts. Runtimes are detected lazily per language and cached.
type Executor struct {
	projectDir string
	hardCap    int64                        // OutputHardCap; tests lower it
	lookPath   func(string) (string, error) // exec.LookPath; tests stub it
	getenv     func(string) string          // os.Getenv; tests stub it

	mu       sync.Mutex
	runtimes map[Language]string // "" = not available
	bg       map[int]struct{}    // pgids of backgrounded runs
}

// New returns an Executor whose default cwd is projectDir.
func New(projectDir string) *Executor {
	return &Executor{
		projectDir: projectDir,
		hardCap:    OutputHardCap,
		lookPath:   exec.LookPath,
		getenv:     os.Getenv,
		runtimes:   map[Language]string{},
		bg:         map[int]struct{}{},
	}
}

// first returns the resolved path of the first name found on PATH, or "".
func (e *Executor) first(names ...string) string {
	for _, n := range names {
		if p, err := e.lookPath(n); err == nil {
			return p
		}
	}
	return ""
}

// detect resolves the runtime binary for lang. Port of runtime.ts:334-391 (POSIX branches only).
func (e *Executor) detect(lang Language) string {
	switch lang {
	case JavaScript:
		if b := e.bun(); b != "" {
			return b
		}
		return e.first("node")
	case TypeScript:
		if b := e.bun(); b != "" {
			return b
		}
		return e.first("tsx", "ts-node")
	case Python:
		return e.first("python3", "python")
	case Shell:
		// runtime.ts:342-357: honor $SHELL only when it exists and is an allowlisted shell.
		if s := e.getenv("SHELL"); s != "" {
			switch filepath.Base(s) {
			case "bash", "sh", "zsh", "dash":
				if _, err := os.Stat(s); err == nil {
					return s
				}
			}
		}
		if p := e.first("bash"); p != "" {
			return p
		}
		return "sh"
	case Ruby:
		return e.first("ruby")
	case Go:
		return e.first("go")
	case Rust:
		return e.first("rustc")
	case PHP:
		return e.first("php")
	case Perl:
		return e.first("perl")
	case R:
		return e.first("Rscript", "R")
	case Elixir:
		return e.first("elixir")
	case CSharp:
		return e.first("dotnet-script")
	}
	return ""
}

// bun finds bun on PATH, else at ~/.bun/bin/bun. Port of runtime.ts:129-152 (POSIX).
func (e *Executor) bun() string {
	if p := e.first("bun"); p != "" {
		return p
	}
	if home := e.getenv("HOME"); home != "" {
		p := filepath.Join(home, ".bun", "bin", "bun")
		if _, err := os.Stat(p); err == nil {
			return p
		}
	}
	return ""
}

// runtime returns the cached runtime for lang, detecting it on first use.
func (e *Executor) runtime(lang Language) string {
	e.mu.Lock()
	defer e.mu.Unlock()
	if p, ok := e.runtimes[lang]; ok {
		return p
	}
	p := e.detect(lang)
	e.runtimes[lang] = p
	return p
}

// missing holds the TS error text for each language whose runtime is absent (runtime.ts:650-763).
var missing = map[Language]string{
	JavaScript: "No JavaScript runtime available. Install Node.js or Bun on PATH (the host process is not itself a JS runtime).",
	TypeScript: "No TypeScript runtime available. Install one of: bun (recommended), tsx (npm i -g tsx), or ts-node.",
	Python:     "No Python runtime available. Install python3 or python.",
	Ruby:       "Ruby not available. Install ruby.",
	Go:         "Go not available. Install go.",
	Rust:       "Rust not available. Install rustc via https://rustup.rs",
	PHP:        "PHP not available. Install php.",
	Perl:       "Perl not available. Install perl.",
	R:          "R not available. Install R / Rscript.",
	Elixir:     "Elixir not available. Install elixir.",
	CSharp:     "C# not available. Install dotnet-script via `dotnet tool install -g dotnet-script`.",
}

// command builds argv for running file. Port of runtime.ts:644-764 (POSIX).
// For rust it returns [rustc, file]; Run compiles and then runs the binary.
func (e *Executor) command(lang Language, file string) ([]string, error) {
	if _, ok := missing[lang]; !ok && lang != Shell {
		return nil, errors.New("Unsupported language: " + string(lang))
	}
	bin := e.runtime(lang)
	if bin == "" {
		return nil, errors.New(missing[lang])
	}
	switch lang {
	case JavaScript, TypeScript:
		if filepath.Base(bin) == "bun" {
			return []string{bin, "run", file}, nil
		}
	case Go:
		return []string{bin, "run", file}, nil
	}
	return []string{bin, file}, nil
}
```

```go
package executor

import (
	"os"
	"strings"
)

// deniedEnv are vars that corrupt sandbox stdout, inject code, or break runtimes.
// Exact port of the DENIED set in executor.ts:578-678.
var deniedEnv = map[string]bool{
	// Shell
	"BASH_ENV": true, "ENV": true, "PROMPT_COMMAND": true, "PS4": true, "SHELLOPTS": true,
	"BASHOPTS": true, "CDPATH": true, "INPUTRC": true, "BASH_XTRACEFD": true,
	// Node.js
	"NODE_OPTIONS": true, "NODE_PATH": true,
	// Python
	"PYTHONSTARTUP": true, "PYTHONHOME": true, "PYTHONWARNINGS": true, "PYTHONBREAKPOINT": true, "PYTHONINSPECT": true,
	// Ruby
	"RUBYOPT": true, "RUBYLIB": true,
	// Perl
	"PERL5OPT": true, "PERL5LIB": true, "PERLLIB": true, "PERL5DB": true,
	// Elixir/Erlang
	"ERL_AFLAGS": true, "ERL_FLAGS": true, "ELIXIR_ERL_OPTIONS": true, "ERL_LIBS": true,
	// Go
	"GOFLAGS": true, "CGO_CFLAGS": true, "CGO_LDFLAGS": true,
	// Rust
	"RUSTC": true, "RUSTC_WRAPPER": true, "RUSTC_WORKSPACE_WRAPPER": true,
	"CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC": true, "CARGO_BUILD_RUSTC_WRAPPER": true, "RUSTFLAGS": true,
	// PHP
	"PHPRC": true, "PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR": true,
	// R
	"R_PROFILE": true, "R_PROFILE_USER": true, "R_HOME": true,
	// .NET startup hooks / deps
	"DOTNET_STARTUP_HOOKS": true, "DOTNET_ADDITIONAL_DEPS": true, "DOTNET_SHARED_STORE": true,
	"DOTNET_ROOT": true, "DOTNET_ROOT(x86)": true, "DOTNET_HOST_PATH": true,
	// .NET profiler attach / diagnostics
	"CORECLR_PROFILER": true, "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH": true, "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_32": true,
	"CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_64": true, "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_ARM32": true, "CORECLR_PROFILER_PATH_ARM64": true,
	"CORECLR_ENABLE_PROFILING": true, "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH": true, "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_32": true,
	"DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_64": true, "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_ARM32": true, "DOTNET_PROFILER_PATH_ARM64": true,
	"DOTNET_DiagnosticPorts": true, "DOTNET_BUNDLE_EXTRACT_BASE_DIR": true,
	// Dynamic linker
	"LD_PRELOAD": true, "DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES": true,
	// OpenSSL
	"OPENSSL_CONF": true, "OPENSSL_ENGINES": true,
	// Compiler
	"CC": true, "CXX": true, "AR": true,
	// Git
	"GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR": true, "GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL": true, "GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM": true, "GIT_EXEC_PATH": true,
	"GIT_SSH": true, "GIT_SSH_COMMAND": true, "GIT_ASKPASS": true,
}

// certPaths are probed in order for SSL_CERT_FILE (executor.ts:723-728).
var certPaths = []string{
	"/etc/ssl/cert.pem",
	"/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt",
	"/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt",
	"/etc/pki/ca-trust/extracted/pem/tls-ca-bundle.pem",
}

// buildEnv filters parent (KEY=VALUE entries) and applies sandbox overrides.
// Port of executor.ts:573-745 (#buildSafeEnv), POSIX branches only.
func buildEnv(parent []string, tmpDir string) []string {
	env := map[string]string{}
	for _, kv := range parent {
		k, v, ok := strings.Cut(kv, "=")
		if !ok || deniedEnv[k] || strings.HasPrefix(k, "BASH_FUNC_") ||
			strings.HasPrefix(strings.ToUpper(k), "COMPLUS_") {
			continue
		}
		env[k] = v
	}
	if env["HOME"] == "" {
		env["HOME"] = tmpDir
	}
	env["TMPDIR"] = tmpDir
	env["LANG"] = "en_US.UTF-8"
	env["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1"
	env["PYTHONUNBUFFERED"] = "1"
	env["PYTHONUTF8"] = "1"
	env["NO_COLOR"] = "1"
	if env["PATH"] == "" {
		env["PATH"] = "/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin"
	}
	if env["SSL_CERT_FILE"] == "" {
		for _, p := range certPaths {
			if _, err := os.Stat(p); err == nil {
				env["SSL_CERT_FILE"] = p
				break
			}
		}
	}
	out := make([]string, 0, len(env))
	for k, v := range env {
		out = append(out, k+"="+v)
	}
	return out
}

package executor

func TestJSONQuoteMatchesJSONStringify(t *testing.T) {
	cases := map[string]string{

		`/x<y>&z`:       `"/x<y>&z"`,

	p := "/p/it's \"f\".txt"
	q := `"/p/it's \"f\".txt"`
	sq := `'/p/it'\''s "f".txt'`
	want := map[Language]string{
		JavaScript: "const FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\nconst file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nconst FILE_CONTENT = require(\"fs\").readFileSync(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, \"utf-8\");\nCODE",
		Python:     "FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + "\nfile_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nwith open(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as _f:\n    FILE_CONTENT = _f.read()\nCODE",
		Shell:      "FILE_CONTENT_PATH=" + sq + "\nfile_path=" + sq + "\nFILE_CONTENT=$(cat " + sq + ")\nCODE",
		Ruby:       "FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + "\nfile_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nFILE_CONTENT = File.read(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, encoding: \"utf-8\")\nCODE",
		Go:         "package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n)\n\nvar FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + "\nvar file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\n\nfunc main() {\n\tb, _ := os.ReadFile(FILE_CONTENT_PATH)\n\tFILE_CONTENT := string(b)\n\t_ = FILE_CONTENT\n\t_ = fmt.Sprint()\nCODE\n}\n",
		Rust:       "#![allow(unused_variables)]\nuse std::fs;\n\nfn main() {\n    let file_content_path = " + q + ";\n    let file_path = file_content_path;\n    let file_content = fs::read_to_string(file_content_path).unwrap();\nCODE\n}\n",
		PHP:        "<?php\n$FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\n$file_path = $FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\n$FILE_CONTENT = file_get_contents($FILE_CONTENT_PATH);\nCODE",
		Perl:       "my $FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\nmy $file_path = $FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nopen(my $fh, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', $FILE_CONTENT_PATH) or die \"Cannot open: $!\";\nmy $FILE_CONTENT = do { local $/; <$fh> };\nclose($fh);\nCODE",
		R:          "FILE_CONTENT_PATH <- " + q + "\nfile_path <- FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nFILE_CONTENT <- readLines(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, warn=FALSE, encoding=\"UTF-8\")\nFILE_CONTENT <- paste(FILE_CONTENT, collapse=\"\\n\")\nCODE",
		Elixir:     "file_content_path = " + q + "\nfile_path = file_content_path\nfile_content = File.read!(file_content_path)\nCODE",
		CSharp:     "var FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\nvar file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nvar FILE_CONTENT = System.IO.File.ReadAllText(FILE_CONTENT_PATH);\nCODE",
	}
	want[TypeScript] = want[JavaScript]
	for _, lang := range Languages {
		if got := wrapFileContent(p, lang, "CODE"); got != want[lang] {
			t.Errorf("%s prefix:\n got %q\nwant %q", lang, got, want[lang])
		}
	}
}

	want := "\nasync function __cm_main(){\nawait 1\n}\n__cm_main().catch(e=>{console.error(e);process.exitCode=1});"
	if got := wrapJS("await 1", false); got != want {
		t.Errorf("wrapJS = %q", got)

}

func TestPrepareCode(t *testing.T) {
	// JS gets the wrapper only for ctx_execute (no FilePath).
	if got := prepareCode(JavaScript, "x", "", "/p", false); !strings.Contains(got, "__cm_main") {
		t.Errorf("js execute not wrapped: %q", got)
	}
	if got := prepareCode(TypeScript, "x", "/f", "/p", false); strings.Contains(got, "__cm_main") || !strings.HasPrefix(got, "const FILE_CONTENT_PATH") {
		t.Errorf("ts execute_file wrong: %q", got)
	}
	// Go without package is wrapped; with package left alone; FILE_CONTENT go not double-wrapped.
	if got := prepareCode(Go, `fmt.Println("hi")`, "", "/p", false); got != "package main\n\nimport \"fmt\"\n\nfunc main() {\nfmt.Println(\"hi\")\n}\n" {
		t.Errorf("go wrap = %q", got)
	}
	full := "package main\nfunc main(){}\n"
	if got := prepareCode(Go, full, "", "/p", false); got != full {
		t.Errorf("go with package changed: %q", got)
	}
	if got := prepareCode(Go, "x", "/f", "/p", false); strings.Count(got, "package main") != 1 {
		t.Errorf("go file_content double-wrapped: %q", got)
	}
	// PHP open tag.
	if got := prepareCode(PHP, "echo 1;", "", "/p", false); got != "<?php\necho 1;" {
		t.Errorf("php = %q", got)
	}
	if got := prepareCode(PHP, "  <?php echo 1;", "", "/p", false); got != "  <?php echo 1;" {
		t.Errorf("php with tag = %q", got)
	}
	// Elixir mix project prefix.
	proj := t.TempDir()
	if got := prepareCode(Elixir, "IO.puts 1", "", proj, false); got != "IO.puts 1" {
		t.Errorf("elixir without mix.exs = %q", got)
	}
	os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(proj, "mix.exs"), nil, 0o644)
	want := "Path.wildcard(Path.join(\"" + proj + "/_build/dev/lib\", \"*/ebin\"))\n|> Enum.each(&Code.prepend_path/1)\n\nIO.puts 1"
	if got := prepareCode(Elixir, "IO.puts 1", "", proj, false); got != want {
		t.Errorf("elixir mix = %q", got)
	}
}

func TestWriteScript(t *testing.T) {
	dir := t.TempDir()
	fp, err := writeScript(dir, Shell, "echo hi", "/a b/bin:/it's")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if fp != filepath.Join(dir, "script.sh") {
		t.Errorf("path = %s", fp)
	}
	b, _ := os.ReadFile(fp)
	if string(b) != "export PATH='/a b/bin:/it'\\''s'\necho hi" {
		t.Errorf("shell content = %q", b)
	}
	if st, _ := os.Stat(fp); st.Mode().Perm() != 0o700 {
		t.Errorf("shell mode = %v", st.Mode().Perm())
	}
	fp, _ = writeScript(dir, Shell, "echo hi", "")
	if b, _ := os.ReadFile(fp); string(b) != "echo hi" {
		t.Errorf("shell without PATH = %q", b)
	}
	for lang, ext := range map[Language]string{Python: "py", R: "R", Elixir: "exs", CSharp: "csx", Perl: "pl"} {
		fp, _ := writeScript(dir, lang, "x", "/bin")
		if filepath.Base(fp) != "script."+ext {
			t.Errorf("%s file = %s", lang, fp)
		}
		if b, _ := os.ReadFile(fp); string(b) != "x" {
			t.Errorf("%s content = %q", lang, b)
		}
	}
}

func TestOSTempDir(t *testing.T) {
	t.Setenv("TMPDIR", t.TempDir())
	d := detectOSTempDir() // osTempDir itself may already be cached by earlier tests
	if st, err := os.Stat(d); err != nil || !st.IsDir() {
		t.Fatalf("osTempDir %q: %v", d, err)
	}
	if strings.HasPrefix(d, os.Getenv("TMPDIR")) {
		t.Errorf("osTempDir followed TMPDIR override: %s", d)
	}
}

`internal/executor/script.go`:

```go
package executor

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/json"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"runtime"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"unicode"
)

// scriptExt maps a language to its temp script extension (executor.ts:23-36).
var scriptExt = map[Language]string{
	JavaScript: "js", TypeScript: "ts", Python: "py", Shell: "sh", Ruby: "rb", Go: "go",
	Rust: "rs", PHP: "php", Perl: "pl", R: "R", Elixir: "exs", CSharp: "csx",
}

// jsonQuote mirrors JSON.stringify for a string (no HTML escaping).
func jsonQuote(s string) string {
	var b bytes.Buffer
	enc := json.NewEncoder(&b)
	enc.SetEscapeHTML(false)
	enc.Encode(s)
	return strings.TrimSuffix(b.String(), "\n")
}

// shQuote single-quotes s for POSIX sh (executor.ts:63-65).
func shQuote(s string) string {
	return "'" + strings.ReplaceAll(s, "'", `'\''`) + "'"
}

// wrapFileContent prefixes code with FILE_CONTENT_PATH / file_path / FILE_CONTENT. Port of executor.ts:747-784.
func wrapFileContent(abs string, lang Language, code string) string {
	q := jsonQuote(abs)
	switch lang {
	case JavaScript, TypeScript:
		return "const FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\nconst file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nconst FILE_CONTENT = require(\"fs\").readFileSync(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, \"utf-8\");\n" + code
	case Python:
		return "FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + "\nfile_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nwith open(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as _f:\n    FILE_CONTENT = _f.read()\n" + code
	case Shell:
		sq := shQuote(abs)
		return "FILE_CONTENT_PATH=" + sq + "\nfile_path=" + sq + "\nFILE_CONTENT=$(cat " + sq + ")\n" + code
	case Ruby:
		return "FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + "\nfile_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nFILE_CONTENT = File.read(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, encoding: \"utf-8\")\n" + code
	case Go:
		return "package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"os\"\n)\n\nvar FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + "\nvar file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH\n\nfunc main() {\n\tb, _ := os.ReadFile(FILE_CONTENT_PATH)\n\tFILE_CONTENT := string(b)\n\t_ = FILE_CONTENT\n\t_ = fmt.Sprint()\n" + code + "\n}\n"
	case Rust:
		return "#![allow(unused_variables)]\nuse std::fs;\n\nfn main() {\n    let file_content_path = " + q + ";\n    let file_path = file_content_path;\n    let file_content = fs::read_to_string(file_content_path).unwrap();\n" + code + "\n}\n"
	case PHP:
		return "<?php\n$FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\n$file_path = $FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\n$FILE_CONTENT = file_get_contents($FILE_CONTENT_PATH);\n" + code
	case Perl:
		return "my $FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\nmy $file_path = $FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nopen(my $fh, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', $FILE_CONTENT_PATH) or die \"Cannot open: $!\";\nmy $FILE_CONTENT = do { local $/; <$fh> };\nclose($fh);\n" + code
	case R:
		return "FILE_CONTENT_PATH <- " + q + "\nfile_path <- FILE_CONTENT_PATH\nFILE_CONTENT <- readLines(FILE_CONTENT_PATH, warn=FALSE, encoding=\"UTF-8\")\nFILE_CONTENT <- paste(FILE_CONTENT, collapse=\"\\n\")\n" + code
	case Elixir:
		return "file_content_path = " + q + "\nfile_path = file_content_path\nfile_content = File.read!(file_content_path)\n" + code
	case CSharp:
		return "var FILE_CONTENT_PATH = " + q + ";\nvar file_path = FILE_CONTENT_PATH;\nvar FILE_CONTENT = System.IO.File.ReadAllText(FILE_CONTENT_PATH);\n" + code
	}
	return code
}

// wrapJS wraps JS/TS in an async main so top-level await works.
// Port of server.ts:1756-1820 without the __CM_FS__/__CM_NET__ counters and the
// require-shadowing IIFE that only fed them (spec §2.2).
func wrapJS(code string, background bool) string {
	s := "\nasync function __cm_main(){\n" + code + "\n}\n__cm_main().catch(e=>{console.error(e);process.exitCode=1});"
	if background {
		s += "\nsetInterval(()=>{},2147483647);"
	}
	return s
}

// prepareCode applies every source transform before the script is written:
// FILE_CONTENT prefix (execute_file) or JS wrapper (execute), then executor.ts:333-347 fixups.
func prepareCode(lang Language, code, filePath, projectDir string, background bool) string {
	if filePath != "" {
		code = wrapFileContent(filePath, lang, code)
	} else if lang == JavaScript || lang == TypeScript {
		code = wrapJS(code, background)
	}
	switch lang {
	case Go:
		if !strings.Contains(code, "package ") {
			code = "package main\n\nimport \"fmt\"\n\nfunc main() {\n" + code + "\n}\n"
		}
	case PHP:
		if !strings.HasPrefix(strings.TrimLeftFunc(code, unicode.IsSpace), "<?") {
			code = "<?php\n" + code
		}
	case Elixir:
		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(projectDir, "mix.exs")); err == nil {
			q := jsonQuote(filepath.Join(projectDir, "_build/dev/lib"))
			code = "Path.wildcard(Path.join(" + q + ", \"*/ebin\"))\n|> Enum.each(&Code.prepend_path/1)\n\n" + code
		}
	}
	return code
}

// writeScript writes code to dir/script.<ext> and returns its path. Shell scripts are
// 0700 and restore the parent PATH on line 1 (executor.ts:67-75, 349-367).
func writeScript(dir string, lang Language, code, parentPATH string) (string, error) {
	fp := filepath.Join(dir, "script."+scriptExt[lang])
	mode := os.FileMode(0o644)
	if lang == Shell {
		mode = 0o700
		if parentPATH != "" {
			code = "export PATH=" + shQuote(parentPATH) + "\n" + code
		}
	}
	return fp, os.WriteFile(fp, []byte(code), mode)
}

// osTempDir is the real per-user temp dir, ignoring a TMPDIR override that may point
// into the project. Port of executor.ts:99-111 (POSIX).
var osTempDir = sync.OnceValue(detectOSTempDir)

func detectOSTempDir() string {
	if runtime.GOOS == "darwin" {
		cmd := exec.Command("getconf", "DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR")
		for _, kv := range os.Environ() {
			if !strings.HasPrefix(kv, "TMPDIR=") {
				cmd.Env = append(cmd.Env, kv)
			}
		}
		out, err := cmd.Output()
		dir := strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
		if cwd, _ := os.Getwd(); err == nil && dir != "" && dir != cwd {
			return dir
		}
	}
	// linux: TS takes dirname(mktemp -u -d) with TMPDIR unset, which is /tmp.
	return "/tmp"
}
```

// alive reports whether any process matches pgrep's args (e.g. "-f", "sleep 7731").
func alive(args ...string) bool {
	out, _ := exec.Command("pgrep", args...).Output()
	return strings.TrimSpace(string(out)) != ""
}

}

```go
package executor

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"syscall"
	"time"
)

// Request is one ctx_execute / ctx_execute_file run.
type Request struct {
	Language   Language
	Code       string
	Timeout    time.Duration // 0 = none
	Background bool
	Cwd        string // "" = project dir
	FilePath   string // non-empty for ctx_execute_file: inject FILE_CONTENT prefix (absolute path)
}

// Result is what the run produced. ExitCode is 1 on timeout and 0 when backgrounded.
type Result struct {
	Stdout, Stderr         string
	ExitCode               int
	TimedOut, Backgrounded bool
}

// Run writes the script to a fresh temp dir, runs it, and removes the dir unless the
// run was backgrounded. The error is only for "cannot run" (missing runtime, temp dir).
// Port of executor.ts:282-330 (execute/executeFile) and 377-407 (rust compile + run).
func (e *Executor) Run(ctx context.Context, r Request) (Result, error) {
	file := r.FilePath
	if file != "" && !filepath.IsAbs(file) {
		file = filepath.Join(e.projectDir, file)
	}
	dir, err := os.MkdirTemp(osTempDir(), ".cmgo-")
	if err != nil {
		return Result{}, err
	}
	keep := false
	defer func() {
		if !keep {
			os.RemoveAll(dir)
		}
	}()
	code := prepareCode(r.Language, r.Code, file, e.projectDir, r.Background)
	script, err := writeScript(dir, r.Language, code, os.Getenv("PATH"))
	if err != nil {
		return Result{}, err
	}
	argv, err := e.command(r.Language, script)
	if err != nil {
		return Result{}, err
	}
	cwd := r.Cwd
	if cwd == "" {
		cwd = e.projectDir
	}
	env := buildEnv(os.Environ(), dir)

	if r.Language == Rust {
		bin := strings.TrimSuffix(script, ".rs")
		limit := 60 * time.Second // a hung compile must not run forever
		if r.Timeout > 0 && r.Timeout < limit {
			limit = r.Timeout
		}
		c := e.spawn(ctx, []string{argv[0], script, "-o", bin}, dir, env, limit, false)
		if c.ExitCode != 0 {
			msg := c.Stderr
			if msg == "" && c.TimedOut {
				msg = "spawnSync rustc ETIMEDOUT"
			}
			return Result{Stderr: "Compilation failed:\n" + msg, ExitCode: 1}, nil
		}
		argv = []string{bin}
	}

	res := e.spawn(ctx, argv, cwd, env, r.Timeout, r.Background)
	keep = res.Backgrounded
	return res, nil
}

// spawn runs argv in its own process group. Port of executor.ts:409-571.
// Timeout or the output cap SIGKILLs the whole group; with background the group is
// left running, its pgid recorded for Shutdown, and the partial output returned.
func (e *Executor) spawn(ctx context.Context, argv []string, cwd string, env []string, timeout time.Duration, background bool) Result {
	// Not context.WithTimeout: a backgrounded run must outlive both the timeout and ctx.
	runCtx, kill := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	detached := false
	defer func() {
		if !detached {
			kill()
		}
	}()
	stop := context.AfterFunc(ctx, kill)
	defer stop()

	out := &capture{max: e.hardCap, kill: kill}
	cmd := exec.CommandContext(runCtx, argv[0], argv[1:]...)
	cmd.Dir, cmd.Env = cwd, env
	cmd.Stdout, cmd.Stderr = stream{out, &out.stdout}, stream{out, &out.stderr}
	cmd.SysProcAttr = &syscall.SysProcAttr{Setpgid: true}
	cmd.Cancel = func() error { return syscall.Kill(-cmd.Process.Pid, syscall.SIGKILL) }
	cmd.WaitDelay = 2 * time.Second
	if background {
		cmd.WaitDelay = 0 // never close a detached child's pipes; TS drains until they close
	}
	if err := cmd.Start(); err != nil {
		return Result{Stderr: err.Error(), ExitCode: 1}
	}
	done := make(chan error, 1) // buffered: a backgrounded run's Wait finishes with no reader
	go func() { done <- cmd.Wait() }()

	var expired <-chan time.Time
	if timeout > 0 {
		t := time.NewTimer(timeout)
		defer t.Stop()
		expired = t.C
	}
	timedOut := false
	select {
	case <-done:
	case <-expired:
		timedOut = true
		if background {
			stop()
			detached = true
			e.mu.Lock()
			e.bg[cmd.Process.Pid] = struct{}{}
			e.mu.Unlock()
			stdout, stderr := out.detach()
			return Result{Stdout: stdout, Stderr: stderr, ExitCode: 0, TimedOut: true, Backgrounded: true}
		}
		kill()
		<-done
	}

	code := 1 // signaled (ExitCode -1), timed out, or no ProcessState
	if ps := cmd.ProcessState; ps != nil && ps.ExitCode() >= 0 && !timedOut {
		code = ps.ExitCode()
	}
	out.mu.Lock()
	defer out.mu.Unlock()
	stderr := out.stderr.String()
	if out.exceeded {
		stderr += fmt.Sprintf("\n[output capped at %.0fMB — process killed]", float64(e.hardCap)/1024/1024)
	}
	return Result{Stdout: out.stdout.String(), Stderr: stderr, ExitCode: code, TimedOut: timedOut}
}

// capture collects stdout and stderr under one combined byte cap.
type capture struct {
	mu             sync.Mutex
	stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
	total, max     int64
	exceeded       bool
	discard        bool // set once backgrounded: drain, keep nothing
	kill           func()
}

// detach snapshots the output and switches both streams to discard.
func (c *capture) detach() (string, string) {
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	c.discard = true
	return c.stdout.String(), c.stderr.String()
}

type stream struct {
	c   *capture
	buf *bytes.Buffer
}

// Write never fails: an error would abort Go's pipe copy and leave the child blocked.
// Like TS, the chunk that crosses the cap is dropped whole and the group killed once.
func (s stream) Write(p []byte) (int, error) {
	c := s.c
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	if c.discard {
		return len(p), nil
	}
	c.total += int64(len(p))
	if c.total <= c.max {
		s.buf.Write(p)
	} else if !c.exceeded {
		c.exceeded = true
		c.kill()
	}
	return len(p), nil
}
```

```go
package executor

import (
	"context"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

// bgPgids returns the recorded background process groups.
func bgPgids(e *Executor) []string {
	e.mu.Lock()
	defer e.mu.Unlock()
	var out []string
	for pgid := range e.bg {
		out = append(out, strconv.Itoa(pgid))
	}
	return out
}

func TestBackgroundSurvivesTimeoutAndShutdown(t *testing.T) {
	e := newTestExec(t)
	t.Cleanup(e.Shutdown)
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	res, err := e.Run(ctx, Request{
		Language:   Shell,
		Code:       `echo "$TMPDIR"; sleep 7735`,
		Timeout:    300 * time.Millisecond,
		Background: true,
	})
	if err != nil || !res.Backgrounded || !res.TimedOut || res.ExitCode != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v, %v", res, err)
	}
	dir := strings.TrimSpace(res.Stdout)
	t.Cleanup(func() { os.RemoveAll(dir) })
	if _, err := os.Stat(dir); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("backgrounded run's temp dir removed: %v", err)
	}
	cancel() // the request ending must not kill a backgrounded group
	time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
	pgids := bgPgids(e)
	if len(pgids) != 1 || !alive("-g", pgids[0]) || !alive("-f", "sleep 7735") {
		t.Fatalf("backgrounded group not running (pgids %v)", pgids)
	}
	e.Shutdown()
	time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
	if alive("-g", pgids[0]) || alive("-f", "sleep 7735") {
		t.Fatal("Shutdown left the group running")
	}
	if len(bgPgids(e)) != 0 {
		t.Fatal("Shutdown did not clear the registry")
	}
}

func TestBackgroundChildKeepsWritingAfterLeaderExits(t *testing.T) {
	e := newTestExec(t)
	t.Cleanup(e.Shutdown)
	marker := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "marker")
	// The leader exits at 0.6s; its subshell writes for ~4s more (past any 2s WaitDelay),
	// touches the marker, then idles in the same group until Shutdown.
	code := `echo start; ( sleep 1; i=0; while [ $i -lt 8 ]; do echo tick$i; echo err$i >&2; i=$((i+1)); sleep 0.5; done; touch ` +
		shQuote(marker) + `; exec sleep 7736 ) & sleep 0.6`
	res := run(t, e, Request{Language: Shell, Code: code, Timeout: 300 * time.Millisecond, Background: true})
	if !res.Backgrounded || res.Stdout != "start\n" {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", res)
	}
	deadline := time.Now().Add(10 * time.Second)
	for {
		if _, err := os.Stat(marker); err == nil {
			break
		}
		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
			t.Fatal("marker never appeared: background child was cut off (closed pipe / SIGPIPE)")
		}
		time.Sleep(100 * time.Millisecond)
	}
	pgids := bgPgids(e)
	if len(pgids) != 1 || !alive("-g", pgids[0]) {
		t.Fatalf("long-running group %v not alive", pgids)
	}
	e.Shutdown()
	time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
	if alive("-g", pgids[0]) || alive("-f", "sleep 7736") {
		t.Fatal("Shutdown left the group running")
	}
}

func TestBackgroundWithoutTimeoutRunsToCompletion(t *testing.T) {
	e := newTestExec(t)
	res := run(t, e, Request{Language: Shell, Code: "echo done", Background: true})
	if res.Backgrounded || res.TimedOut || res.Stdout != "done\n" || res.ExitCode != 0 || len(bgPgids(e)) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", res)
	}
}

func TestBackgroundJSKeepAlive(t *testing.T) {
	e := newTestExec(t)
	if e.runtime(JavaScript) == "" {
		t.Skip("no JS runtime")
	}
	t.Cleanup(e.Shutdown)
	// Without the setInterval keep-alive this would exit at once and not background.
	res := run(t, e, Request{Language: JavaScript, Code: `console.log("up")`, Timeout: 2 * time.Second, Background: true})
	if !res.Backgrounded || strings.TrimSpace(res.Stdout) != "up" {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", res)
	}
	pgids := bgPgids(e)
	e.Shutdown()
	time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
	if len(pgids) != 1 || alive("-g", pgids[0]) {
		t.Fatalf("JS group %v still running after Shutdown", pgids)
	}
}

func TestIsSoftFail(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		lang   Language
		code   int
		stdout string
		want   bool
	}{
		{Shell, 1, "match\n", true},
		{Shell, 1, " \n\t", false},
		{Shell, 1, "", false},
		{Shell, 2, "match", false},
		{Shell, 0, "match", false},
		{Python, 1, "match", false},
		{JavaScript, 1, "x", false},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		if got := IsSoftFail(c.lang, c.code, c.stdout); got != c.want {
			t.Errorf("IsSoftFail(%s, %d, %q) = %v; want %v", c.lang, c.code, c.stdout, got, c.want)
		}
	}
}

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/executor/background.go`:

```go
package executor

import (
	"strings"
	"syscall"
)

// Shutdown sends SIGTERM to every backgrounded process group and forgets them.
// Port of executor.ts:268-276 (cleanupBackgrounded).
func (e *Executor) Shutdown() {
	e.mu.Lock()
	defer e.mu.Unlock()
	for pgid := range e.bg {
		syscall.Kill(-pgid, syscall.SIGTERM) // already gone: ESRCH, ignored
	}
	clear(e.bg)
}

// IsSoftFail reports a non-zero exit that is not an error: shell, exit code 1, and
// non-blank stdout (grep with no match in one file of many). Port of exit-classify.ts:15-33.
func IsSoftFail(lang Language, exitCode int, stdout string) bool {
	return lang == Shell && exitCode == 1 && strings.TrimSpace(stdout) != ""
}
```

```go
package executor

import (
	"context"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// Review focus: the shell script's first line is `export PATH='<parent PATH>'`.
// A single quote inside a PATH entry must not break that line.
func TestShellPathWithSingleQuote(t *testing.T) {
	odd := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "it's bin")
	if err := os.MkdirAll(odd, 0o755); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	t.Setenv("PATH", odd+string(os.PathListSeparator)+os.Getenv("PATH"))
	res, err := New(t.TempDir()).Run(context.Background(), Request{Language: Shell, Code: `printf '%s' "$PATH"`})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if res.ExitCode != 0 || !strings.HasPrefix(res.Stdout, odd+":") {
		t.Fatalf("exit %d stdout %q stderr %q", res.ExitCode, res.Stdout, res.Stderr)
	}
}

// Review focus: project directories with spaces, quotes and non-ASCII names
// are the cwd for runs and the base for FILE_CONTENT paths.
func TestProjectDirWithSpacesQuotesUnicode(t *testing.T) {
	project := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), `my "proj" it's dự án`)
	if err := os.MkdirAll(project, 0o755); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	real, _ := filepath.EvalSymlinks(project)
	ex := New(project)
	res, err := ex.Run(context.Background(), Request{Language: Shell, Code: "pwd -P"})
	if err != nil || res.ExitCode != 0 || strings.TrimSpace(res.Stdout) != real {
		t.Fatalf("shell cwd: %q (want %q) err=%v stderr=%q", res.Stdout, real, err, res.Stderr)
	}
	file := filepath.Join(project, "a b's.txt")
	if err := os.WriteFile(file, []byte("x\ny\n"), 0o644); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	res, err = ex.Run(context.Background(), Request{Language: Shell, FilePath: file, Code: `printf '%s' "$FILE_CONTENT" | wc -l | tr -d ' '`})
	if err != nil || res.ExitCode != 0 || strings.TrimSpace(res.Stdout) != "1" {
		t.Fatalf("shell FILE_CONTENT: %q err=%v stderr=%q", res.Stdout, err, res.Stderr)
	}
	if _, lookErr := exec.LookPath("python3"); lookErr == nil {
		res, err = ex.Run(context.Background(), Request{Language: Python, FilePath: file, Code: `print(len(FILE_CONTENT.splitlines()))`})
		if err != nil || res.ExitCode != 0 || strings.TrimSpace(res.Stdout) != "2" {
			t.Fatalf("python FILE_CONTENT: %q err=%v stderr=%q", res.Stdout, err, res.Stderr)
		}
	}
}

// Review focus: binary output and one very long line come back intact in
// size; the MCP layer sanitizes UTF-8, the executor must not truncate.
func TestBinaryAndLongLineOutput(t *testing.T) {
	ex := New(t.TempDir())
	res, err := ex.Run(context.Background(), Request{Language: Shell, Code: "head -c 300000 /dev/urandom"})
	if err != nil || res.ExitCode != 0 || len(res.Stdout) != 300000 {
		t.Fatalf("binary: len %d exit %d err=%v", len(res.Stdout), res.ExitCode, err)
	}
	res, err = ex.Run(context.Background(), Request{Language: Shell, Code: "head -c 2000000 /dev/zero | tr '\\0' 'a'"})
	if err != nil || res.ExitCode != 0 || len(res.Stdout) != 2000000 || strings.Contains(res.Stdout, "\n") {
		t.Fatalf("long line: len %d exit %d err=%v", len(res.Stdout), res.ExitCode, err)
	}
}
```

package fetch

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"errors"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"net/url"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"golang.org/x/net/dns/dnsmessage"
)

func TestClassifyIP(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		ip               string
		blocked, private bool
	}{
		// IPv4 always blocked
		{"169.254.169.254", true, false}, // IMDS
		{"169.254.0.1", true, false},
		{"0.0.0.0", true, false},
		{"0.255.255.255", true, false},
		{"224.0.0.1", true, false},
		{"239.255.255.250", true, false},
		{"240.0.0.1", true, false},
		{"255.255.255.255", true, false},
		// IPv4 private
		{"127.0.0.1", false, true},
		{"127.255.255.254", false, true},
		{"10.0.0.1", false, true},
		{"10.255.255.255", false, true},
		{"172.16.0.1", false, true},
		{"172.31.255.255", false, true},
		{"192.168.1.1", false, true},
		// IPv4 public (edges just outside the ranges)
		{"8.8.8.8", false, false},
		{"172.15.255.255", false, false},
		{"172.32.0.0", false, false},
		{"169.253.255.255", false, false},
		{"192.169.0.1", false, false},
		{"223.255.255.255", false, false},
		{"1.1.1.1", false, false},
		// IPv6 always blocked
		{"::", true, false},
		{"fe80::1", true, false},
		{"febf:ffff::1", true, false},
		{"ff02::1", true, false},
		{"ff00::", true, false},
		// IPv6 private
		{"::1", false, true},
		{"fc00::1", false, true},
		{"fd12:3456::1", false, true},
		// IPv6 public
		{"2001:4860:4860::8888", false, false},
		{"fec0::1", false, false}, // just past fe80::/10
		{"fe7f::1", false, false},
		{"fe8::1", false, false}, // 0fe8:: — TS string prefix check got this wrong
		// IPv4-mapped IPv6 uses the IPv4 rules
		{"::ffff:127.0.0.1", false, true},
		{"::ffff:169.254.169.254", true, false},
		{"::ffff:10.1.2.3", false, true},
		{"::ffff:8.8.8.8", false, false},
		{"::ffff:7f00:1", false, true}, // hex form of ::ffff:127.0.0.1
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		ip := net.ParseIP(c.ip)
		if ip == nil {
			t.Fatalf("bad test IP %q", c.ip)
		}
		b, p := ClassifyIP(ip)
		if b != c.blocked || p != c.private {
			t.Errorf("ClassifyIP(%s) = (%v,%v), want (%v,%v)", c.ip, b, p, c.blocked, c.private)
		}
	}
	if b, _ := ClassifyIP(nil); !b {
		t.Error("nil IP must be blocked (malformed)")
	}
	if b, _ := ClassifyIP(net.IP{1, 2, 3}); !b {
		t.Error("3-byte IP must be blocked (malformed)")
	}
}

func dialErr(t *testing.T, strict bool, r *net.Resolver, addr string) error {
	t.Helper()
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()
	c, err := dialContext(strict, r)(ctx, "tcp", addr)
	if c != nil {
		c.Close()
	}
	return err
}

func TestDialerBlocksIMDSBeforeConnect(t *testing.T) {
	err := dialErr(t, false, nil, "169.254.169.254:80")
	var se *ssrfError
	if !errors.As(err, &se) {
		t.Fatalf("want ssrfError, got %v", err)
	}
	if se.Error() != "SSRF blocked at connect-time: 169.254.169.254 resolves to 169.254.169.254 (block)" {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", se.Error())
	}
}

func TestDialerLoopbackAllowedUnlessStrict(t *testing.T) {
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(http.ResponseWriter, *http.Request) {}))
	defer srv.Close()
	addr := srv.Listener.Addr().String()
	if err := dialErr(t, false, nil, addr); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("non-strict loopback dial failed: %v", err)
	}
	var se *ssrfError
	if err := dialErr(t, true, nil, addr); !errors.As(err, &se) || se.verdict != "private" {
		t.Fatalf("strict loopback dial: want private ssrfError, got %v", err)
	}
}

// fakeDNS is a net.Conn speaking DNS over a stream (2-byte length prefix,
// which Go's resolver uses for conns that are not net.PacketConn).
type fakeDNS struct {
	answer func(name string, t dnsmessage.Type) []net.IP
	out    bytes.Buffer
}

func (c *fakeDNS) Write(b []byte) (int, error) {
	var p dnsmessage.Parser
	h, err := p.Start(b[2:])
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	q, err := p.Question()
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	bld := dnsmessage.NewBuilder(nil, dnsmessage.Header{ID: h.ID, Response: true, Authoritative: true, RecursionDesired: h.RecursionDesired, RecursionAvailable: true})
	bld.StartQuestions()
	bld.Question(q)
	bld.StartAnswers()
	rh := dnsmessage.ResourceHeader{Name: q.Name, Type: q.Type, Class: dnsmessage.ClassINET}
	for _, ip := range c.answer(q.Name.String(), q.Type) {
		if v4 := ip.To4(); v4 != nil && q.Type == dnsmessage.TypeA {
			bld.AResource(rh, dnsmessage.AResource{A: [4]byte(v4)})
		} else if v4 == nil && q.Type == dnsmessage.TypeAAAA {
			bld.AAAAResource(rh, dnsmessage.AAAAResource{AAAA: [16]byte(ip)})
		}
	}
	msg, err := bld.Finish()
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	c.out.Write([]byte{byte(len(msg) >> 8), byte(len(msg))})
	c.out.Write(msg)
	return len(b), nil
}
func (c *fakeDNS) Read(b []byte) (int, error)       { return c.out.Read(b) }
func (c *fakeDNS) Close() error                     { return nil }
func (c *fakeDNS) LocalAddr() net.Addr              { return &net.TCPAddr{} }
func (c *fakeDNS) RemoteAddr() net.Addr             { return &net.TCPAddr{} }
func (c *fakeDNS) SetDeadline(time.Time) error      { return nil }
func (c *fakeDNS) SetReadDeadline(time.Time) error  { return nil }
func (c *fakeDNS) SetWriteDeadline(time.Time) error { return nil }

// fakeResolver answers every lookup from answer (A and AAAA queries).
func fakeResolver(answer func(name string, t dnsmessage.Type) []net.IP) *net.Resolver {
	return &net.Resolver{PreferGo: true, Dial: func(context.Context, string, string) (net.Conn, error) {
		return &fakeDNS{answer: answer}, nil
	}}
}

func TestDialerChecksResolvedIPNotHostname(t *testing.T) {
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(http.ResponseWriter, *http.Request) {}))
	defer srv.Close()
	u, _ := url.Parse(srv.URL)
	r := fakeResolver(func(name string, ty dnsmessage.Type) []net.IP {
		if name == "evil.test." && ty == dnsmessage.TypeA {
			return []net.IP{net.ParseIP("127.0.0.1")}
		}
		return nil
	})
	addr := net.JoinHostPort("evil.test", u.Port())
	if err := dialErr(t, false, r, addr); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("non-strict: evil.test -> 127.0.0.1 should connect, got %v", err)
	}
	err := dialErr(t, true, r, addr)
	var se *ssrfError
	if !errors.As(err, &se) {
		t.Fatalf("strict: want ssrfError, got %v", err)
	}
	if se.host != "evil.test" || se.ip != "127.0.0.1" || se.verdict != "private" {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", se)
	}
}

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/fetch/ip.go`:

```go
// Package fetch fetches a URL in-process with an SSRF guard at dial time and
// converts the body to indexable text (Markdown, pretty JSON or plain text).
package fetch

import (
	"context"
	"net"
	"strings"
	"syscall"
)

// ClassifyIP reports whether ip is always blocked (link-local/IMDS, 0/8,
// multicast/reserved, ::, fe80::/10, ff00::/8, malformed) or private
// (loopback, RFC1918, ::1, fc00::/7; blocked only in strict mode).
// Port of classifyIp, S:3244-3286. IPv4-mapped IPv6 goes through the IPv4 rules.
func ClassifyIP(ip net.IP) (blocked, private bool) {
	if v4 := ip.To4(); v4 != nil {
		a, b := v4[0], v4[1]
		switch {
		case a == 169 && b == 254, a == 0, a >= 224:
			return true, false
		case a == 127, a == 10, a == 172 && b >= 16 && b <= 31, a == 192 && b == 168:
			return false, true
		}
		return false, false
	}
	if len(ip) != net.IPv6len {
		return true, false // malformed
	}
	switch {
	case ip.IsUnspecified(), ip[0] == 0xfe && ip[1]&0xc0 == 0x80, ip[0] == 0xff:
		return true, false
	case ip.IsLoopback(), ip[0]&0xfe == 0xfc:
		return false, true
	}
	return false, false
}

// ssrfError is returned by the dialer's Control hook; Fetch formats it with
// the TS wording for the hop it happened on.
type ssrfError struct {
	host, ip, verdict string // verdict: "block" | "private"
}

func (e *ssrfError) Error() string {
	// TS connect-time wording (S:2915-2919).
	return "SSRF blocked at connect-time: " + e.host + " resolves to " + e.ip + " (" + e.verdict + ")"
}

// dialContext returns a DialContext that remembers the pre-resolution host and
// rejects, in Control, every address actually dialed that ClassifyIP blocks.
// resolver nil means the default resolver (tests inject a fake one).
func dialContext(strict bool, resolver *net.Resolver) func(ctx context.Context, network, addr string) (net.Conn, error) {
	return func(ctx context.Context, network, addr string) (net.Conn, error) {
		host, _, _ := net.SplitHostPort(addr)
		d := net.Dialer{
			Resolver: resolver,
			Control: func(_, address string, _ syscall.RawConn) error {
				ipStr, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(address)
				if err != nil {
					ipStr = address
				}
				ipStr, _, _ = strings.Cut(ipStr, "%") // drop IPv6 zone before classifying (TS does too)
				blocked, private := ClassifyIP(net.ParseIP(ipStr))
				if blocked {
					return &ssrfError{host, ipStr, "block"}
				}
				if private && strict {
					return &ssrfError{host, ipStr, "private"}
				}
				return nil
			},
		}
		return d.DialContext(ctx, network, addr)
	}
}
```

<table>
<thead><tr><th>Name</th><th>Value</th></tr></thead>
<tbody><tr><td>alpha</td><td>1</td></tr><tr><td>beta</td><td>2</td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<footer>SITE FOOTER</footer>
</body></html>`

func TestHTMLToMarkdown(t *testing.T) {
	md, err := HTMLToMarkdown(testPage)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for _, want := range []string{
		"# Main\n",
		"## Section\n",
		"**bold**",
		"_em_",
		"* one\n* two",
		"~~gone~~",
		"[a link](https://example.com/x)",
		"```go\nfmt.Println(\"hi\")\n```",
		"| Name | Value |\n|---|---|\n| alpha | 1 |\n| beta | 2 |",
	} {
		if !strings.Contains(md, want) {
			t.Errorf("missing %q in:\n%s", want, md)
		}
	}
	for _, gone := range []string{"SITE HEADER", "NAVLINK", "SCRIPTBODY", "NOSCRIPTBODY", "SITE FOOTER", "color:red", "Doc Title", "THE END"} {
		if strings.Contains(md, gone) {
			t.Errorf("%q should be removed, got:\n%s", gone, md)
		}
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/fetch/markdown.go`:

```go
package fetch

import (
	"github.com/JohannesKaufmann/html-to-markdown/v2/converter"
	"github.com/JohannesKaufmann/html-to-markdown/v2/plugin/base"
	"github.com/JohannesKaufmann/html-to-markdown/v2/plugin/commonmark"
	"github.com/JohannesKaufmann/html-to-markdown/v2/plugin/strikethrough"
	"github.com/JohannesKaufmann/html-to-markdown/v2/plugin/table"
)

// HTMLToMarkdown converts an HTML page to Markdown.
// Port of the Turndown setup in buildFetchCode, S:3098-3101:
// {headingStyle:'atx', codeBlockStyle:'fenced'} + gfm (tables, strikethrough),
// with script, style, nav, header, footer, noscript removed.
func HTMLToMarkdown(html string) (string, error) {
	// Built per call: cheap, and no shared mutable converter state.
	conv := converter.NewConverter(converter.WithPlugins(
		base.NewBasePlugin(), // already removes script, style, noscript, head, …
		commonmark.NewCommonmarkPlugin(
			commonmark.WithHeadingStyle(commonmark.HeadingStyleATX),
			commonmark.WithCodeBlockFence("```"),
			commonmark.WithEmDelimiter("_"),      // Turndown default
			commonmark.WithBulletListMarker("*"), // Turndown default
			commonmark.WithListEndComment(false), // no "<!--THE END-->" noise in indexed text
		),
		strikethrough.NewStrikethroughPlugin(),
		// Minimal padding like Turndown gfm; aligned padding bloats chunks when one cell is long.
		table.NewTablePlugin(table.WithCellPaddingBehavior(table.CellPaddingBehaviorMinimal)),
	))
	for _, tag := range []string{"nav", "header", "footer"} {
		conv.Register.TagType(tag, converter.TagTypeRemove, converter.PriorityStandard)
	}
	return conv.ConvertString(html)
}
```
