```go
package fetch

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"golang.org/x/net/dns/dnsmessage"
)

func testFetcher(strict bool) *Fetcher {
	f := New(strict)
	f.Timeout = 5 * time.Second
	return f
}

// serve starts a server answering every request with status, content type and body.
func serve(t *testing.T, status int, ct, body string) string {
	t.Helper()
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		if ct != "" {
			w.Header().Set("Content-Type", ct)
		}
		w.WriteHeader(status)
		fmt.Fprint(w, body)
	}))
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	return srv.URL
}

func mustFetch(t *testing.T, f *Fetcher, u string) Result {
	t.Helper()
	res, err := f.Fetch(context.Background(), u)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Fetch(%s): %v", u, err)
	}
	return res
}

func wantErr(t *testing.T, f *Fetcher, u, want string) {
	t.Helper()
	_, err := f.Fetch(context.Background(), u)
	if err == nil || err.Error() != want {
		t.Fatalf("Fetch(%s) error = %v, want %q", u, err, want)
	}
}

func TestFetchHTML(t *testing.T) {
	res := mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), serve(t, 200, "text/html; charset=utf-8", testPage))
	if res.Kind != KindHTML {
		t.Fatalf("kind = %s", res.Kind)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(res.Body, "| Name | Value |\n|---|---|\n| alpha | 1 |") {
		t.Errorf("no GFM table in:\n%s", res.Body)
	}
	for _, gone := range []string{"SCRIPTBODY", "NAVLINK"} {
		if strings.Contains(res.Body, gone) {
			t.Errorf("%q not removed:\n%s", gone, res.Body)
		}
	}
	res = mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), serve(t, 200, "application/xhtml+xml", "<p>x<b>y</b></p>"))
	if res.Kind != KindHTML || res.Body != "x**y**" {
		t.Errorf("xhtml: %+v", res)
	}
}

func TestFetchJSONPrettyPrintsKeepingKeyOrder(t *testing.T) {
	res := mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), serve(t, 200, "application/json", `{"b":1,"a":[1,"x"]}`))
	want := "{\n  \"b\": 1,\n  \"a\": [\n    1,\n    \"x\"\n  ]\n}"
	if res.Kind != KindJSON || res.Body != want {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", res)
	}
	res = mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), serve(t, 200, "application/problem+json", `{"title":"t"}`))
	if res.Kind != KindJSON || res.Body != "{\n  \"title\": \"t\"\n}" {
		t.Fatalf("+json: %+v", res)
	}
}

func TestFetchInvalidJSONIsText(t *testing.T) {
	res := mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), serve(t, 200, "application/json", "{not json"))
	if res.Kind != KindText || res.Body != "{not json" {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", res)
	}
}

func TestFetchPlainTextTrimmed(t *testing.T) {
	res := mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), serve(t, 200, "text/plain", "\uFEFF\n  line one\nline two \n\n"))
	if res.Kind != KindText || res.Body != "line one\nline two" {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", res)
	}
}

func TestFetchErrors(t *testing.T) {
	f := testFetcher(false)
	wantErr(t, f, serve(t, 404, "text/plain", "nope"), "HTTP 404")
	wantErr(t, f, serve(t, 302, "", ""), "HTTP 302") // 3xx without Location is final
	wantErr(t, f, serve(t, 200, "text/html", "<script>x()</script>  "), "empty content")
	wantErr(t, f, serve(t, 200, "text/plain", " \n "), "empty content")
}

func TestFetchRejectsBadURLs(t *testing.T) {
	f := testFetcher(false)
	wantErr(t, f, "file:///etc/passwd", `URL scheme "file:" not allowed (only http: and https:)`)
	wantErr(t, f, "javascript:alert(1)", `URL scheme "javascript:" not allowed (only http: and https:)`)
	wantErr(t, f, "not a url", "invalid URL")
	wantErr(t, f, "http://", "invalid URL")
	wantErr(t, f, "http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/",
		`URL "169.254.169.254" resolves to 169.254.169.254 — blocked (link-local / IMDS / multicast / reserved)`)
}

func TestFetchStrictBlocksLoopback(t *testing.T) {
	u := serve(t, 200, "text/plain", "ok")
	if res := mustFetch(t, testFetcher(false), u); res.Body != "ok" {
		t.Fatalf("non-strict: %+v", res)
	}
	wantErr(t, testFetcher(true), u, `URL "127.0.0.1" resolves to private IP 127.0.0.1 — blocked under CMGO_FETCH_STRICT=1`)
}

// redirector serves /r/N -> /r/N-1 ... /r/0 = "done".
func redirector(t *testing.T) string {
	t.Helper()
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		n, _ := strconv.Atoi(strings.TrimPrefix(r.URL.Path, "/r/"))
		if n == 0 {
			fmt.Fprint(w, "done")
			return
		}
		http.Redirect(w, r, "/r/"+strconv.Itoa(n-1), http.StatusFound)
	}))
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	return srv.URL
}

func TestFetchRedirectLimit(t *testing.T) {
	base := redirector(t)
	f := testFetcher(false)
	if res := mustFetch(t, f, base+"/r/5"); res.Body != "done" {
		t.Fatalf("5 hops: %+v", res)
	}
	wantErr(t, f, base+"/r/6", "SSRF blocked: redirect chain exceeded 5 hops")
}

func TestFetchRedirectTargetsRevalidated(t *testing.T) {
	to := func(loc string) string {
		srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
			w.Header().Set("Location", loc)
			w.WriteHeader(http.StatusMovedPermanently)
		}))
		t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
		return srv.URL
	}
	f := testFetcher(false)
	wantErr(t, f, to("file:///etc/passwd"), "SSRF blocked: redirect to non-http(s) scheme file:")
	wantErr(t, f, to("http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/"), "SSRF blocked: redirect to 169.254.169.254 (block)")
	wantErr(t, f, to("http://[fe80::1]/"), "SSRF blocked: redirect to fe80::1 (block)")
	wantErr(t, f, to("http://%zz/"), "SSRF blocked: invalid redirect Location: http://%zz/")
}

func TestFetchBodyCap(t *testing.T) {
	f := testFetcher(false)
	f.MaxBody = 10
	chunked := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/plain")
		w.(http.Flusher).Flush() // no Content-Length: forces the LimitReader path
		fmt.Fprint(w, strings.Repeat("x", 100))
	}))
	defer chunked.Close()
	wantErr(t, f, chunked.URL, "Response too large: 11 bytes exceeds 10")

	withCL := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "text/plain")
		w.Header().Set("Content-Length", "20")
		fmt.Fprint(w, strings.Repeat("y", 20))
	}))
	defer withCL.Close()
	wantErr(t, f, withCL.URL, "Response too large: Content-Length 20 exceeds 10")

	if res := mustFetch(t, f, serve(t, 200, "text/plain", "0123456789")); res.Body != "0123456789" {
		t.Fatalf("exactly at cap: %+v", res)
	}
}

// The name answers 127.0.0.1 on the first lookup and 169.254.169.254 after
// (DNS rebinding). The redirect back to the same name is dialed again
// (Connection: close) and the dialed IP, not the name, is rejected.
func TestFetchDNSRebindingCheckedAtDial(t *testing.T) {
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		w.Header().Set("Connection", "close")
		if r.URL.Path == "/" {
			http.Redirect(w, r, "/next", http.StatusFound)
			return
		}
		fmt.Fprint(w, "reached the rebound address")
	}))
	defer srv.Close()
	port := srv.Listener.Addr().(*net.TCPAddr).Port

	var lookups atomic.Int32
	f := testFetcher(false)
	f.resolver = fakeResolver(func(name string, ty dnsmessage.Type) []net.IP {
		if name != "rebind.test." || ty != dnsmessage.TypeA {
			return nil
		}
		if lookups.Add(1) == 1 {
			return []net.IP{net.ParseIP("127.0.0.1")}
		}
		return []net.IP{net.ParseIP("169.254.169.254")}
	})
	wantErr(t, f, fmt.Sprintf("http://rebind.test:%d/", port),
		"SSRF blocked: redirect target rebind.test resolves to 169.254.169.254 (block)")
	if n := lookups.Load(); n != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("A lookups = %d, want 2 (one per dial)", n)
	}
}

func TestFetchDNSErrors(t *testing.T) {
	f := testFetcher(false)
	f.resolver = &net.Resolver{PreferGo: true, Dial: func(context.Context, string, string) (net.Conn, error) {
		return nil, &net.OpError{Op: "dial", Net: "udp", Err: errors.New("network is down")}
	}}
	_, err := f.Fetch(context.Background(), "http://down.test/")
	if err == nil || !strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), `DNS lookup failed for "down.test": `) || !strings.HasSuffix(err.Error(), dnsRetryHint) {
		t.Fatalf("transient DNS: %v", err)
	}

	f.resolver = fakeResolver(func(string, dnsmessage.Type) []net.IP { return nil })
	_, err = f.Fetch(context.Background(), "http://nxdomain.test/")
	if err == nil || !strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), `DNS lookup failed for "nxdomain.test": `) || strings.Contains(err.Error(), "retry once") {
		t.Fatalf("not-found DNS: %v", err)
	}
}

func TestFetchTimeout(t *testing.T) {
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		<-r.Context().Done()
	}))
	defer srv.Close()
	f := testFetcher(false)
	f.Timeout = 100 * time.Millisecond
	_, err := f.Fetch(context.Background(), srv.URL)
	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "context deadline exceeded") {
		t.Fatalf("got %v", err)
	}
	if strings.Contains(err.Error(), srv.URL) {
		t.Fatalf("error should not repeat the URL: %v", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/fetch/fetch.go`:

```go
package fetch

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/url"
	"strings"
	"syscall"
	"time"
)

// Kind is the content route chosen from Content-Type (TS "__CM_CT__:<kind>").
type Kind string

const (
	KindJSON Kind = "json"
	KindHTML Kind = "html"
	KindText Kind = "text"
)

// Result is a fetched body ready for indexing: HTML already converted to
// Markdown, JSON pretty-printed (Kind text if it does not parse), trimmed.
type Result struct {
	Body string
	Kind Kind
}

type Fetcher struct {
	Strict       bool
	Timeout      time.Duration
	MaxBody      int64
	MaxRedirects int

	resolver *net.Resolver // nil = default resolver; tests inject a fake DNS
}

// ErrEmpty is returned when the fetched body is empty after trimming.
var ErrEmpty = errors.New("empty content")

func New(strict bool) *Fetcher {
	return &Fetcher{Strict: strict, Timeout: 30 * time.Second, MaxBody: 50 << 20, MaxRedirects: 5}
}

// dnsRetryHint is appended to transient DNS/network errors (S:3222-3223, S:3334-3335).
const dnsRetryHint = " — transient DNS error; retry once before falling back. If it keeps failing, the MCP host may be running under a network sandbox; restart the host with network access enabled."

// Fetch GETs rawURL and routes the body by Content-Type.
// Port of ssrfGuard (S:3168-3233), fetchOneUrl (S:3288-3370) and the
// subprocess main() (S:3079-3112), in-process.
func (f *Fetcher) Fetch(ctx context.Context, rawURL string) (Result, error) {
	u, err := url.Parse(rawURL)
	if err != nil || u.Scheme == "" {
		return Result{}, errors.New("invalid URL")
	}
	if u.Scheme != "http" && u.Scheme != "https" {
		return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("URL scheme %q not allowed (only http: and https:)", u.Scheme+":")
	}
	if u.Host == "" {
		return Result{}, errors.New("invalid URL")
	}

	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, f.Timeout)
	defer cancel()
	tr := http.DefaultTransport.(*http.Transport).Clone()
	tr.Proxy = nil // a proxy would resolve the name itself and bypass the dial-time check
	tr.DialContext = dialContext(f.Strict, f.resolver)
	defer tr.CloseIdleConnections()

	resp, hop, err := f.follow(ctx, tr, u)
	if err != nil {
		return Result{}, describe(err, hop)
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	if resp.StatusCode < 200 || resp.StatusCode > 299 {
		return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("HTTP %d", resp.StatusCode)
	}

	// safeText, S:3067-3077.
	if resp.ContentLength > f.MaxBody {
		return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("Response too large: Content-Length %d exceeds %d", resp.ContentLength, f.MaxBody)
	}
	b, err := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, f.MaxBody+1))
	if err != nil {
		return Result{}, describe(err, hop)
	}
	if int64(len(b)) > f.MaxBody {
		return Result{}, fmt.Errorf("Response too large: %d bytes exceeds %d", len(b), f.MaxBody)
	}
	// resp.text() decodes UTF-8, dropping a BOM and replacing invalid bytes.
	text := strings.ToValidUTF8(strings.TrimPrefix(string(b), "\uFEFF"), "\uFFFD")

	ct := resp.Header.Get("Content-Type") // TS matches substrings case-sensitively; so do we
	var res Result
	switch {
	case strings.Contains(ct, "application/json") || strings.Contains(ct, "+json"):
		var buf bytes.Buffer
		if json.Indent(&buf, []byte(text), "", "  ") == nil {
			res = Result{buf.String(), KindJSON}
		} else {
			res = Result{text, KindText}
		}
	case strings.Contains(ct, "text/html") || strings.Contains(ct, "application/xhtml"):
		md, err := HTMLToMarkdown(text)
		if err != nil {
			return Result{}, err
		}
		res = Result{md, KindHTML}
	default:
		res = Result{text, KindText}
	}
	res.Body = strings.TrimSpace(res.Body)
	if res.Body == "" {
		return Result{}, ErrEmpty
	}
	return res, nil
}

// follow walks redirects by hand so every hop is dialed (and IP-checked) anew.
// Port of fetchWithManualRedirect, S:3013-3058. Returns the hop index that
// produced the response or the error. It uses RoundTrip, not http.Client,
// because the client rejects an unparseable Location before we can.
func (f *Fetcher) follow(ctx context.Context, rt http.RoundTripper, u *url.URL) (*http.Response, int, error) {
	for hop := 0; ; hop++ {
		req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodGet, u.String(), nil)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, hop, err
		}
		resp, err := rt.RoundTrip(req)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, hop, err
		}
		loc := resp.Header.Get("Location")
		if resp.StatusCode < 300 || resp.StatusCode >= 400 || loc == "" {
			return resp, hop, nil
		}
		resp.Body.Close()
		if hop == f.MaxRedirects {
			return nil, hop, fmt.Errorf("SSRF blocked: redirect chain exceeded %d hops", f.MaxRedirects)
		}
		next, err := u.Parse(loc)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, hop, errors.New("SSRF blocked: invalid redirect Location: " + loc)
		}
		if next.Scheme != "http" && next.Scheme != "https" {
			return nil, hop, errors.New("SSRF blocked: redirect to non-http(s) scheme " + next.Scheme + ":")
		}
		u = next
	}
}

// describe turns a transport error into the text TS shows the model.
func describe(err error, hop int) error {
	var se *ssrfError
	if errors.As(err, &se) {
		switch {
		case hop > 0 && net.ParseIP(se.host) != nil: // S:3038
			return fmt.Errorf("SSRF blocked: redirect to %s (%s)", se.host, se.verdict)
		case hop > 0: // S:3049-3050
			return fmt.Errorf("SSRF blocked: redirect target %s resolves to %s (%s)", se.host, se.ip, se.verdict)
		case se.verdict == "block": // S:3198
			return fmt.Errorf("URL %q resolves to %s — blocked (link-local / IMDS / multicast / reserved)", se.host, se.ip)
		default: // S:3206; names the env var cmgo actually reads
			return fmt.Errorf("URL %q resolves to private IP %s — blocked under CMGO_FETCH_STRICT=1", se.host, se.ip)
		}
	}
	var de *net.DNSError
	if errors.As(err, &de) { // S:3210-3230
		msg := fmt.Sprintf("DNS lookup failed for %q: %s", de.Name, de.Error())
		if de.IsTimeout || de.IsTemporary {
			msg += dnsRetryHint
		}
		return errors.New(msg)
	}
	msg := err.Error()
	if errors.Is(err, syscall.ETIMEDOUT) || errors.Is(err, syscall.ENETUNREACH) || errors.Is(err, syscall.EPERM) { // S:3333
		msg += dnsRetryHint
	}
	return errors.New(msg)
}
```

- Produces:
  - `type Deps struct{Cfg config.Config; Store *store.Store; Exec *executor.Executor; Policy *security.Policy; Fetch *fetch.Fetcher; Now func() time.Time}`
  - `New(d *Deps, version string) *mcp.Server`. It calls `registerExecute`, `registerExecuteFile`, `registerBatch`, `registerSearch` and `registerFetchAndIndex`, each with signature `func(s *mcp.Server, d *Deps)`.
  - `ParseArgs(raw json.RawMessage) (Args, error)`, plus the `Args` methods `Has`, `String`, `Number`, `Bool`, `Array`, `Strings`, `Commands`. Each getter returns `(value, present bool, err error)`.
  - `type Command struct{Label, Command string}`
  - `TextResult(text string, isError bool) *mcp.CallToolResult`, `ErrorResult(format string, a ...any)`, `ArgError(err error)`, `KB(n int) string`
  - Test-only: `newHarness(t, now func() time.Time) *harness`, with `.call(name, argsJSON) (string, bool)` and `.writeSettings(deny ...string)`, and fields `Deps`, `Session`, `Project`, `Home`.

Every tool is registered with the low-level `s.AddTool(&mcp.Tool{…, InputSchema: json.RawMessage(...)}, handler)`. The generic `mcp.AddTool` validates arguments strictly, so it would reject the loosely typed inputs that context-mode accepts. `args.go` ports the TS zod preprocessors: `coerceJsonArray`, `coerceBoolean`, `coerceCommandsArray` (S:2503-2552) and `z.coerce.number()`.

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"reflect"
	"testing"

	"github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp"
)

func mustArgs(t *testing.T, raw string) Args {
	t.Helper()
	a, err := ParseArgs([]byte(raw))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return a
}

func TestParseArgsRejectsNonObject(t *testing.T) {
	if _, err := ParseArgs([]byte(`[1]`)); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("expected error for array arguments")
	}
	if a, err := ParseArgs(nil); err != nil || len(a) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("empty raw: %v %v", a, err)
	}
}

func TestNumberCoercion(t *testing.T) {
	a := mustArgs(t, `{"n":1500,"s":" 2000 ","e":"","b":true,"bad":"abc","f":1.5}`)
	cases := []struct {
		key  string
		want float64
		err  bool
	}{{"n", 1500, false}, {"s", 2000, false}, {"e", 0, false}, {"b", 1, false}, {"bad", 0, true}, {"f", 1.5, false}}
	for _, c := range cases {
		got, ok, err := a.Number(c.key)
		if !ok || (err != nil) != c.err || (!c.err && got != c.want) {
			t.Errorf("%s: got %v ok=%v err=%v", c.key, got, ok, err)
		}
	}
	if _, ok, _ := a.Number("missing"); ok {
		t.Error("missing key reported present")
	}
}

func TestBoolCoercionOnlyLiterals(t *testing.T) {
	a := mustArgs(t, `{"t":true,"st":" TRUE ","sf":"false","x":"yes","n":1}`)
	for key, want := range map[string]bool{"t": true, "st": true, "sf": false} {
		got, ok, err := a.Bool(key)
		if !ok || err != nil || got != want {
			t.Errorf("%s: got %v %v %v", key, got, ok, err)
		}
	}
	for _, key := range []string{"x", "n"} {
		if _, _, err := a.Bool(key); err == nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: expected error", key)
		}
	}
}

func TestArrayCoercion(t *testing.T) {
	a := mustArgs(t, `{"arr":["a","b"],"json":"[\"x\",\"y\"]","bare":"single query","empty":"  "}`)
	for key, want := range map[string][]string{"arr": {"a", "b"}, "json": {"x", "y"}, "bare": {"single query"}} {
		got, ok, err := a.Strings(key)
		if !ok || err != nil || !reflect.DeepEqual(got, want) {
			t.Errorf("%s: got %v %v %v", key, got, ok, err)
		}
	}
	if _, _, err := a.Strings("empty"); err == nil {
		t.Error("empty string must not be lifted")
	}
}

func TestCommandsCoercion(t *testing.T) {
	a := mustArgs(t, `{"c":["ls","pwd"],"o":[{"label":"L","command":"git status"}],"j":"[{\"label\":\"A\",\"command\":\"echo hi\"}]","bad":[{"label":"x"}]}`)
	got, _, err := a.Commands("c")
	if err != nil || !reflect.DeepEqual(got, []Command{{"cmd_1", "ls"}, {"cmd_2", "pwd"}}) {
		t.Fatalf("bare strings: %v %v", got, err)
	}
	got, _, _ = a.Commands("o")
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(got, []Command{{"L", "git status"}}) {
		t.Fatalf("objects: %v", got)
	}
	got, _, _ = a.Commands("j")
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(got, []Command{{"A", "echo hi"}}) {
		t.Fatalf("json string: %v", got)
	}
	if _, _, err := a.Commands("bad"); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("missing command must error")
	}
}

func TestTextResultSanitizesUTF8(t *testing.T) {
	r := TextResult("ok \xff end", true)
	tc := r.Content[0].(*mcp.TextContent)
	if tc.Text != "ok � end" || !r.IsError {
		t.Fatalf("%q %v", tc.Text, r.IsError)
	}
}

func TestKB(t *testing.T) {
	for n, want := range map[int]string{0: "0.0KB", 1024: "1.0KB", 1536: "1.5KB", 102400: "100.0KB"} {
		if got := KB(n); got != want {
			t.Errorf("KB(%d)=%s want %s", n, got, want)
		}
	}
}
```

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/config"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/executor"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/fetch"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/security"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/store"
	"github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp"
)

// harness is a connected server+client pair over in-memory transports,
// backed by a temp project dir, temp home and temp DB.
type harness struct {
	t       *testing.T
	Deps    *Deps
	Session *mcp.ClientSession
	Project string
	Home    string
}

// newHarness builds Deps rooted in t.TempDir(). now may be nil (time.Now).
func newHarness(t *testing.T, now func() time.Time) *harness {
	t.Helper()
	root := t.TempDir()
	project := filepath.Join(root, "project")
	home := filepath.Join(root, "home")
	for _, d := range []string{project, home} {
		if err := os.MkdirAll(d, 0o755); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
	}
	cfg := config.Config{
		DataDir:    filepath.Join(root, "data"),
		DBPath:     filepath.Join(root, "data", "cmgo.db"),
		ProjectDir: project,
		ProjectKey: config.NormalizeProject(project),
		Home:       home,
	}
	st, err := store.Open(cfg.DBPath)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	ex := executor.New(project)
	t.Cleanup(func() { ex.Shutdown(); st.Close() })
	d := &Deps{Cfg: cfg, Store: st, Exec: ex, Policy: security.NewPolicy(project, home), Fetch: fetch.New(false), Now: now}

	srv := New(d, "test")
	ct, stt := mcp.NewInMemoryTransports()
	ctx := context.Background()
	ss, err := srv.Connect(ctx, stt, nil)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	cs, err := mcp.NewClient(&mcp.Implementation{Name: "test-client", Version: "0"}, nil).Connect(ctx, ct, nil)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(func() { cs.Close(); ss.Close() })
	return &harness{t: t, Deps: d, Session: cs, Project: project, Home: home}
}

// call invokes a tool with arguments given as a JSON object literal and
// returns the concatenated text content and the IsError flag.
func (h *harness) call(name, argsJSON string) (string, bool) {
	h.t.Helper()
	var args map[string]any
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(argsJSON), &args); err != nil {
		h.t.Fatalf("bad test args %s: %v", argsJSON, err)
	}
	res, err := h.Session.CallTool(context.Background(), &mcp.CallToolParams{Name: name, Arguments: args})
	if err != nil {
		h.t.Fatalf("CallTool %s: %v", name, err)
	}
	var b strings.Builder
	for _, c := range res.Content {
		if tc, ok := c.(*mcp.TextContent); ok {
			b.WriteString(tc.Text)
		}
	}
	return b.String(), res.IsError
}

// writeSettings writes <project>/.claude/settings.json with the given deny rules.
func (h *harness) writeSettings(deny ...string) {
	h.t.Helper()
	dir := filepath.Join(h.Project, ".claude")
	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
		h.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	body, _ := json.Marshal(map[string]any{"permissions": map[string]any{"deny": deny}})
	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, "settings.json"), body, 0o644); err != nil {
		h.t.Fatal(err)
	}
}

func TestHarnessConnects(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	if _, err := h.Session.ListTools(context.Background(), nil); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
}
```

`internal/mcpserver/server.go`:

```go
// Package mcpserver registers cmgo's MCP tools and formats their output.
package mcpserver

import (
	"time"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/config"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/executor"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/fetch"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/security"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/store"
	"github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp"
)

// Deps are the long-lived services every tool handler uses.
type Deps struct {
	Cfg    config.Config
	Store  *store.Store
	Exec   *executor.Executor
	Policy *security.Policy
	Fetch  *fetch.Fetcher
	Now    func() time.Time // injectable clock; defaults to time.Now
}

// New builds the MCP server with all five tools registered.
func New(d *Deps, version string) *mcp.Server {
	if d.Now == nil {
		d.Now = time.Now
	}
	s := mcp.NewServer(&mcp.Implementation{Name: "cmgo", Version: version}, nil)
	registerExecute(s, d)
	registerExecuteFile(s, d)
	registerBatch(s, d)
	registerSearch(s, d)
	registerFetchAndIndex(s, d)
	return s
}
```

`internal/mcpserver/args.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"math"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// Args is a tool's decoded arguments object. The getters reproduce the TS zod
// preprocessors, so loosely typed calls (numbers or booleans sent as strings,
// arrays sent as JSON strings) behave as they did in context-mode.
type Args map[string]any

// ParseArgs decodes the raw arguments of a tools/call request.
func ParseArgs(raw json.RawMessage) (Args, error) {
	a := Args{}
	if len(raw) == 0 || string(raw) == "null" {
		return a, nil
	}
	dec := json.NewDecoder(strings.NewReader(string(raw)))
	dec.UseNumber()
	if err := dec.Decode(&a); err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("arguments must be a JSON object: %v", err)
	}
	return a, nil
}

// Has reports whether key is present and not null.
func (a Args) Has(key string) bool {
	v, ok := a[key]
	return ok && v != nil
}

// String returns a string argument; a missing key gives "" and ok=false.
func (a Args) String(key string) (string, bool, error) {
	v, ok := a[key]
	if !ok || v == nil {
		return "", false, nil
	}
	s, isStr := v.(string)
	if !isStr {
		return "", true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected string", key)
	}
	return s, true, nil
}

// Number mirrors z.coerce.number(): numbers, numeric strings (JS Number()),
// and booleans are accepted; anything that becomes NaN is an error.
func (a Args) Number(key string) (float64, bool, error) {
	v, ok := a[key]
	if !ok || v == nil {
		return 0, false, nil
	}
	switch x := v.(type) {
	case json.Number:
		f, err := x.Float64()
		if err != nil {
			return 0, true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected number", key)
		}
		return f, true, nil
	case string:
		t := strings.TrimSpace(x)
		if t == "" {
			return 0, true, nil // JS Number("") === 0
		}
		f, err := strconv.ParseFloat(t, 64)
		if err != nil || math.IsNaN(f) {
			return 0, true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected number, received nan", key)
		}
		return f, true, nil
	case bool:
		if x {
			return 1, true, nil
		}
		return 0, true, nil
	default:
		return 0, true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected number", key)
	}
}

// Bool mirrors TS coerceBoolean: only the literals "true"/"false" (any case,
// trimmed) are lifted; other strings are an error rather than truthy.
func (a Args) Bool(key string) (bool, bool, error) {
	v, ok := a[key]
	if !ok || v == nil {
		return false, false, nil
	}
	switch x := v.(type) {
	case bool:
		return x, true, nil
	case string:
		switch strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(x)) {
		case "true":
			return true, true, nil
		case "false":
			return false, true, nil
		}
	}
	return false, true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected boolean", key)
}

// Array mirrors TS coerceJsonArray: a JSON-array string is parsed, any other
// non-empty string becomes a one-element array, an empty string is an error.
func (a Args) Array(key string) ([]any, bool, error) {
	v, ok := a[key]
	if !ok || v == nil {
		return nil, false, nil
	}
	switch x := v.(type) {
	case []any:
		return x, true, nil
	case string:
		if strings.TrimSpace(x) == "" {
			return nil, true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected non-empty array", key)
		}
		var parsed []any
		dec := json.NewDecoder(strings.NewReader(x))
		dec.UseNumber()
		if err := dec.Decode(&parsed); err == nil {
			return parsed, true, nil
		}
		return []any{x}, true, nil
	}
	return nil, true, fmt.Errorf("%s: expected array", key)
}

// Strings is Array restricted to string elements.
func (a Args) Strings(key string) ([]string, bool, error) {
	arr, ok, err := a.Array(key)
	if err != nil || !ok {
		return nil, ok, err
	}
	out := make([]string, 0, len(arr))
	for i, e := range arr {
		s, isStr := e.(string)
		if !isStr {
			return nil, true, fmt.Errorf("%s[%d]: expected string", key, i)
		}
		out = append(out, s)
	}
	return out, true, nil
}

// Command is one ctx_batch_execute entry.
type Command struct {
	Label   string
	Command string
}

// Commands mirrors TS coerceCommandsArray: Array plus bare strings becoming
// {label: "cmd_<i+1>", command: s}.
func (a Args) Commands(key string) ([]Command, bool, error) {
	arr, ok, err := a.Array(key)
	if err != nil || !ok {
		return nil, ok, err
	}
	out := make([]Command, 0, len(arr))
	for i, e := range arr {
		switch x := e.(type) {
		case string:
			out = append(out, Command{Label: fmt.Sprintf("cmd_%d", i+1), Command: x})
		case map[string]any:
			label, l := x["label"].(string)
			cmd, c := x["command"].(string)
			if !l || !c {
				return nil, true, fmt.Errorf("%s[%d]: expected {label, command} strings", key, i)
			}
			out = append(out, Command{Label: label, Command: cmd})
		default:
			return nil, true, fmt.Errorf("%s[%d]: expected object", key, i)
		}
	}
	return out, true, nil
}
```

`internal/mcpserver/result.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"fmt"
	"strings"

	"github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp"
)

// TextResult wraps text as a single text content block. Invalid UTF-8 from
// subprocess output is replaced with U+FFFD, as Node's string decoding did.
func TextResult(text string, isError bool) *mcp.CallToolResult {
	return &mcp.CallToolResult{
		Content: []mcp.Content{&mcp.TextContent{Text: strings.ToValidUTF8(text, "�")}},
		IsError: isError,
	}
}

// ErrorResult is TextResult with IsError set.
func ErrorResult(format string, a ...any) *mcp.CallToolResult {
	return TextResult(fmt.Sprintf(format, a...), true)
}

// ArgError reports invalid arguments as a tool error the model can read.
func ArgError(err error) *mcp.CallToolResult {
	return ErrorResult("Invalid arguments: %v", err)
}

// KB formats a byte count like TS `(n / 1024).toFixed(1) + "KB"`.
func KB(n int) string {
	return fmt.Sprintf("%.1fKB", float64(n)/1024)
}
```

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"fmt"
	"regexp"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

func TestBuildExecuteEcho(t *testing.T) {
	if got := buildExecuteEcho("shell", "echo hi", ""); got != "```shell\necho hi\n```\n\n" {
		t.Errorf("plain echo = %q", got)
	}
	if got := buildExecuteEcho("python", "print(1)", "data.txt"); got != "path=data.txt\n```python\nprint(1)\n```\n\n" {
		t.Errorf("path echo = %q", got)
	}
	long := strings.Repeat("a", 2001)
	if got, want := buildExecuteEcho("shell", long, ""), "```shell\n"+strings.Repeat("a", 2000)+"\n… (truncated)\n```\n\n"; got != want {
		t.Errorf("truncated echo has len %d, want %d", len(got), len(want))
	}
	if got := buildExecuteEcho("shell", strings.Repeat("a", 2000), ""); strings.Contains(got, "truncated") {
		t.Error("2000-char code must not be truncated")
	}
}

func TestUTF16Prefix(t *testing.T) {
	// "😀" is 2 UTF-16 units: JS "a😀b".slice(0, 2) keeps only "a" here.
	if got, cut := utf16Prefix("a😀b", 2); got != "a" || !cut {
		t.Errorf("got %q %v", got, cut)
	}
	if got, cut := utf16Prefix("a😀b", 3); got != "a😀" || !cut {
		t.Errorf("got %q %v", got, cut)
	}
	if got, cut := utf16Prefix("a😀b", 4); got != "a😀b" || cut {
		t.Errorf("got %q %v", got, cut)
	}
}

// intentFixture is ~200 lines of log output with a few ERROR lines, sized so
// plain-text chunking yields enough chunks for distinctive terms.
func intentFixture() string {
	var b strings.Builder
	for i := 1; i <= 200; i++ {
		switch {
		case i%30 == 0:
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "line %d ERROR disk quota exceeded on volume\n", i)
		case i%45 == 0:
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "line %d retrying upload of artifact bundle\n", i)
		default:
			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "line %d step completed without problems\n", i)
		}
	}
	return b.String()
}

func TestIntentSearchMatch(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out := intentFixture()
	if len(out) <= intentSearchThreshold {
		t.Fatalf("fixture is %d bytes, want > %d", len(out), intentSearchThreshold)
	}
	got, err := intentSearch(h.Deps, out, "disk quota", "execute:shell")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	lines := strings.Split(got, "\n")
	if !regexp.MustCompile(`^Indexed \d+ sections from "execute:shell" into knowledge base\.$`).MatchString(lines[0]) {
		t.Errorf("line 0 = %q", lines[0])
	}
	want1 := regexp.MustCompile(`^[1-5] sections matched "disk quota" \(201 lines, ` + regexp.QuoteMeta(KB(len(out))) + `\):$`)
	if !want1.MatchString(lines[1]) {
		t.Errorf("line 1 = %q", lines[1])
	}
	if lines[2] != "" || !strings.HasPrefix(lines[3], "  - ") {
		t.Errorf("result list = %q", lines[2:4])
	}
	if !strings.Contains(got, "\n\nSearchable terms: ") {
		t.Errorf("missing Searchable terms:\n%s", got)
	}
	if !strings.HasSuffix(got, "\n\nUse ctx_search(queries: [...]) to retrieve full content of any section.") {
		t.Errorf("bad tail:\n%s", got)
	}
}

func TestIntentSearchNoMatch(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out := intentFixture()
	got, err := intentSearch(h.Deps, out, "zzqx", "execute:shell")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	lines := strings.Split(got, "\n")
	if want := fmt.Sprintf(`No sections matched intent "zzqx" in 201-line output (%s).`, KB(len(out))); lines[1] != want {
		t.Errorf("line 1 = %q, want %q", lines[1], want)
	}
	if !strings.HasSuffix(got, "\n\nUse ctx_search(queries: [...]) to explore the indexed content.") {
		t.Errorf("bad tail:\n%s", got)
	}
}

func TestIndexStdoutPointer(t *testing.T) {

		t.Errorf("got %q", got)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/mcpserver/echo.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

// codeEchoMax is CODE_ECHO_MAX (S:1511), counted in UTF-16 code units like JS.
const codeEchoMax = 2000

// buildExecuteEcho is the source-code preamble before tool output; path is
// "" for ctx_execute. Port of truncateCodeForEcho + buildExecuteEcho (S:1513-1527).
func buildExecuteEcho(language, code, path string) string {
	header := ""
	if path != "" {
		header = "path=" + path + "\n"
	}
	if cut, truncated := utf16Prefix(code, codeEchoMax); truncated {
		code = cut + "\n… (truncated)"
	}
	return header + "```" + language + "\n" + code + "\n```\n\n"
}

// utf16Prefix is JS s.slice(0, n) for the first n UTF-16 code units, reporting
// whether anything was cut. A surrogate pair straddling n is dropped whole.
func utf16Prefix(s string, n int) (string, bool) {
	units := 0
	for i, r := range s {
		w := 1
		if r >= 0x10000 {
			w = 2
		}
		if units+w > n {
			return s[:i], true
		}
		units += w
	}
	return s, false
}
```

`internal/mcpserver/intent.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"fmt"
	"strings"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/store"
)

const (
	intentSearchThreshold = 5_000   // INTENT_SEARCH_THRESHOLD (S:1979)
	largeOutputThreshold  = 102_400 // LARGE_OUTPUT_THRESHOLD (S:1980)
)

// intentSearch indexes output as plain text under source, searches it for
// intent and returns section titles + first-line previews, not the content.
// Port of intentSearch (S:1982-2036).
func intentSearch(d *Deps, output, intent, source string) (string, error) {
	totalLines := strings.Count(output, "\n") + 1
	size := KB(len(output))
	indexed, err := d.Store.Index(d.Cfg.ProjectKey, source, store.ChunkPlainText(output, 20))
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	results, err := d.Store.SearchWithFallback(intent, store.SearchOpts{
		Project: d.Cfg.ProjectKey, Source: source, Limit: 5, Mode: "relevance",
	})
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	terms, err := d.Store.DistinctiveTerms(indexed.SourceID, 40)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}

	lines := []string{fmt.Sprintf(`Indexed %d sections from "%s" into knowledge base.`, indexed.TotalChunks, source)}
	if len(results) == 0 {
		lines = append(lines, fmt.Sprintf(`No sections matched intent "%s" in %d-line output (%s).`, intent, totalLines, size))
	} else {
		lines = append(lines, fmt.Sprintf(`%d sections matched "%s" (%d lines, %s):`, len(results), intent, totalLines, size), "")
		for _, r := range results {
			first, _, _ := strings.Cut(r.Content, "\n")
			preview, _ := utf16Prefix(first, 120)
			lines = append(lines, "  - "+r.Title+": "+preview)
		}
	}
	if len(terms) > 0 {
		lines = append(lines, "", "Searchable terms: "+strings.Join(terms, ", "))
	}
	if len(results) == 0 {
		lines = append(lines, "", "Use ctx_search(queries: [...]) to explore the indexed content.")
	} else {
		lines = append(lines, "", "Use ctx_search(queries: [...]) to retrieve full content of any section.")
	}
	return strings.Join(lines, "\n"), nil
}

// indexStdout indexes large output with Markdown chunking and returns the
// pointer text. Port of indexStdout (S:1958-1973).
func indexStdout(d *Deps, output, source string) (string, error) {
	res, err := d.Store.Index(d.Cfg.ProjectKey, source, store.ChunkMarkdown(output, store.ChunkMaxBytes))
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("Indexed %d sections (%d with code) from: %s\nUse ctx_search(queries: [\"...\"]) to query this content. Use source: \"%s\" to scope results.",
		res.TotalChunks, res.CodeChunks, res.Label, res.Label), nil
}
```

```go
// writeProjectFile writes body to <project>/name.
func (h *harness) writeProjectFile(name, body string) {
	h.t.Helper()
	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(h.Project, name), []byte(body), 0o644); err != nil {
		h.t.Fatal(err)
	}
}

// checkListedTool asserts tools/list carries name with the given title,
// description, hand-written schema and the TS #846 annotations.
func checkListedTool(t *testing.T, name, title, desc, schema string) {
	t.Helper()
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	res, err := h.Session.ListTools(context.Background(), nil)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var want any
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(schema), &want); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for _, tool := range res.Tools {
		if tool.Name != name {
			continue
		}
		if !reflect.DeepEqual(tool.InputSchema, want) {
			t.Errorf("%s schema = %v", name, tool.InputSchema)
		}
		if tool.Title != title || tool.Description != desc {
			t.Errorf("%s title/description mismatch: %q", name, tool.Title)
		}
		if a := tool.Annotations; a == nil || a.ReadOnlyHint || a.DestructiveHint == nil || !*a.DestructiveHint || a.OpenWorldHint == nil || !*a.OpenWorldHint {
			t.Errorf("%s annotations = %+v", name, a)
		}
		return
	}
	t.Fatalf("%s not listed", name)
}

// bigMarkdown is > 100KB of Markdown with one rare word per section.
func bigMarkdown() string {
	var b strings.Builder
	for i := 0; b.Len() <= largeOutputThreshold+1000; i++ {
		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "## Section %d\n\n%s marker%dword\n\n", i, strings.Repeat("filler text for the section body. ", 30), i)
	}
	return b.String()
}

	want := regexp.MustCompile(`^` + regexp.QuoteMeta(fmt.Sprintf(shellEcho, "cat big.md")) +
		`Indexed \d+ sections \(0 with code\) from: execute:shell\nUse ctx_search\(queries: \["\.\.\."\]\) to query this content\. Use source: "execute:shell" to scope results\.$`)
	if isErr || !want.MatchString(text) {
		t.Fatalf("got %v %q", isErr, text)
	}
	hits, err := h.Deps.Store.SearchWithFallback("marker7word", store.SearchOpts{

	})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/mcpserver/execute.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"cmp"
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/executor"
	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/security"
	"github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp"
)

// execArgs are the arguments ctx_execute and ctx_execute_file share.
type execArgs struct {
	lang         executor.Language
	code, intent string
	timeoutMS    float64 // as given, for "after Nms" texts
	timeout      time.Duration
}

// parseExecArgs validates language/code/timeout/intent like the TS zod schemas
// (S:1692-1741, S:2076-2112).
func parseExecArgs(a Args) (execArgs, error) {
	var e execArgs
	lang, ok, err := a.String("language")
	if err != nil {
		return e, err
	}
	e.lang = executor.Language(lang)
	if !ok || !slices.Contains(executor.Languages, e.lang) {
		return e, fmt.Errorf("language: invalid enum value %q, expected one of %v", lang, executor.Languages)
	}
	if e.code, ok, err = a.String("code"); err != nil || !ok {
		return e, cmp.Or(err, fmt.Errorf("code: required"))
	}
	if e.intent, _, err = a.String("intent"); err != nil {
		return e, err
	}
	ms, ok, err := a.Number("timeout")
	if err != nil {
		return e, err
	}
	// shortcut: timeout <= 0 means no timer (Go executor), where TS setTimeout(0) fires at once.
	if ok && ms > 0 {
		e.timeoutMS, e.timeout = ms, time.Duration(ms*float64(time.Millisecond))
	}
	return e, nil
}

// msText formats the timeout like JS `${n}` (2000, 1.5).
func (e execArgs) msText() string { return strconv.FormatFloat(e.timeoutMS, 'f', -1, 64) }

// denyCode ports the deny-only firewall dispatch (S:1745-1751, S:2126-2132).
func denyCode(d *Deps, e execArgs) *mcp.CallToolResult {
	blocked, cmd, pattern := d.Policy.CheckCodeMatch(string(e.lang), e.code)
	switch {
	case !blocked:
		return nil
	case cmd == "":
		return TextResult(security.BlockedMessage(pattern), true)
	default:
		return TextResult(security.EmbeddedBlockedMessage(cmd, pattern), true)
	}
}

// finishRun is the shared tail of both handlers after the timeout branch:
// exit classification, then intent search, auto-index or inline output.
// Port of S:1879-1941 and S:2159-2219.
func finishRun(d *Deps, r executor.Result, e execArgs, echo, label string) *mcp.CallToolResult {
	out, isError := r.Stdout, false
	if r.ExitCode != 0 {
		if !executor.IsSoftFail(e.lang, r.ExitCode, r.Stdout) {
			isError, label = true, label+":error"
			out = fmt.Sprintf("Exit code: %d\n\nstdout:\n%s\n\nstderr:\n%s", r.ExitCode, r.Stdout, r.Stderr)
		}
	} else if out == "" {
		out = "(no output)"
	}

	var text string
	var err error
	switch {
	case strings.TrimSpace(e.intent) != "" && len(out) > intentSearchThreshold:
		text, err = intentSearch(d, out, e.intent, label)
	case len(out) > largeOutputThreshold && r.ExitCode != 0:
		text, err = intentSearch(d, out, "errors failures exceptions", label)
	case len(out) > largeOutputThreshold:
		text, err = indexStdout(d, out, label)
	default:
		text = out
	}
	if err != nil {
		return ErrorResult("Runtime error: %v", err)
	}
	return TextResult(echo+text, isError)
}

// execAnnotations are the TS #846 hints shared by both tools.
var execAnnotations = &mcp.ToolAnnotations{ReadOnlyHint: false, DestructiveHint: new(true), IdempotentHint: false, OpenWorldHint: new(true)}

// registerExecute registers ctx_execute. Port of S:1647-1952.
func registerExecute(s *mcp.Server, d *Deps) {
	s.AddTool(&mcp.Tool{
		Name:        "ctx_execute",
		Title:       "Run code in a sandbox (executes the supplied code)",
		Description: executeDesc,
		Annotations: execAnnotations,
		InputSchema: json.RawMessage(executeSchema),
	}, func(ctx context.Context, req *mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
		a, err := ParseArgs(req.Params.Arguments)
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		e, err := parseExecArgs(a)
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		background, _, err := a.Bool("background")
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		cwd, _, err := a.String("cwd")
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		if denied := denyCode(d, e); denied != nil {
			return denied, nil
		}

		r, err := d.Exec.Run(ctx, executor.Request{Language: e.lang, Code: e.code, Timeout: e.timeout, Background: background, Cwd: cwd})
		if err != nil {
			return ErrorResult("Runtime error: %v", err), nil
		}
		echo := buildExecuteEcho(string(e.lang), e.code, "")
		if r.TimedOut {
			partial := strings.TrimSpace(r.Stdout)
			switch {
			case r.Backgrounded && partial != "":
				return TextResult(echo+partial+"\n\n_(process backgrounded after "+e.msText()+"ms — still running)_", false), nil
			case partial != "":
				return TextResult(echo+partial+"\n\n_(timed out after "+e.msText()+"ms — partial output shown above)_", false), nil
			}
			return TextResult(echo+"Execution timed out after "+e.msText()+"ms\n\nstderr:\n"+r.Stderr, true), nil
		}
		return finishRun(d, r, e, echo, "execute:"+string(e.lang)), nil
	})
}

// Description and schema below are generated from server.ts S:1660-1741
// (bunNote dropped; langList is all 12 languages).

const executeDesc = `Run code in a sandboxed subprocess. Languages: javascript, typescript, python, shell, ruby, go, rust, php, perl, r, elixir, csharp.

Think-in-Code — the core philosophy: the bytes your code processes never enter your conversation memory; only what you console.log() does. Reading a 700 KB log directly means 700 KB of your remaining reasoning capacity gets spent on raw bytes. Running code over that same log in this sandbox and printing a 3 KB summary leaves you with 697 KB of capacity for the actual work.

Concrete shape — analyze 47 source files without reading any of them:
  ctx_execute(language: "javascript", code: ` + "`" + `
    const fs = require('fs');
    const files = fs.readdirSync('src').filter(f => f.endsWith('.ts'));
    files.forEach(f => {
      const lines = fs.readFileSync('src/'+f,'utf8').split('\\n').length;
      console.log(f + ': ' + lines + ' lines');
    });
  ` + "`" + `)
  // 47 files analyzed, 15,314 LoC summarized — output ~3.6 KB instead of 47 Read() calls = ~700 KB.

WHEN:
  - You intend to derive an answer FROM data (filter, count, aggregate, parse, compare, transform) — do the derivation in code and print only the answer
  - Output shape or size cannot be predicted before execution (recursive finds, repo-wide greps, list endpoints, query results, log scans)
  - You would otherwise read raw output and then mentally compute — that compute belongs here, in code, where its inputs stay out of your conversation
  - You need to keep a long-running process alive (dev server, watcher, daemon) — pass ` + "`" + `background: true` + "`" + ` to detach on timeout instead of killing the process
  - The output may legitimately be large but you only want recall-by-topic later — pass an ` + "`" + `intent` + "`" + ` string; outputs over ~5KB are auto-indexed into the knowledge base and only the section titles + previews come back, retrievable via ctx_search

WHEN NOT:
  - Single observational command whose entire short output you intend to consume verbatim (whoami, pwd, git status on a clean tree) — Bash is simpler
  - File mutations (Edit/Write) or navigation (cd/ls) — Bash is the right surface
  - You already know the output is one short fixed line and you want to read it as-is

RETURNS:
  Only what your code prints. Wrap risky calls in try/catch — uncaught errors go to stderr and may leak more than intended. When ` + "`" + `intent` + "`" + ` is set and output exceeds the auto-index threshold, the response carries searchable section titles + previews instead of the raw stdout; use ctx_search(queries: [...]) to drill into specific sections.

EXAMPLE: ctx_execute(language: "javascript", code: "const out = require('child_process').execSync('npm test', {encoding:'utf8', stdio:['ignore','pipe','pipe']}); console.log(out.split('\\n').filter(l => /(FAIL|✗|×|Error:|Tests +.*(failed|passed))/i.test(l)).slice(0, 60).join('\\n'))")
EXAMPLE: ctx_execute(language: "javascript", code: "const out = require('child_process').execSync('gh issue list --json number,title --limit 100', {encoding:'utf8'}); const hooks = JSON.parse(out).filter(i => /hook|routing/i.test(i.title)); console.log(` + "`" + `${hooks.length} hook-related issues` + "`" + `)")`

const executeSchema = `{
	"type": "object",
	"properties": {
		"language": {
			"type": "string",
			"enum": ["javascript", "typescript", "python", "shell", "ruby", "go", "rust", "php", "perl", "r", "elixir", "csharp"],
			"description": "Runtime language"
		},
		"code": {
			"type": "string",
			"description": "Source code to execute. Use console.log (JS/TS), print (Python/Ruby/Perl/R), echo (Shell), echo (PHP), fmt.Println (Go), IO.puts (Elixir), or Console.WriteLine (C#) to output a summary to context."
		},
		"timeout": {
			"type": ["number", "string"],
			"description": "Max execution time in ms. When omitted, no server-side timer fires — the MCP host's RPC timeout governs (which is the right layer for this policy). Pass an explicit value for long-running builds (Gradle/Maven/SBT)."
		},
		"background": {
			"type": ["boolean", "string"],
			"default": false,
			"description": "Keep process running after timeout (for servers/daemons). Returns partial output without killing the process. IMPORTANT: Do NOT add setTimeout/self-close timers in background scripts — the process must stay alive until the timeout detaches it. For server+fetch patterns, prefer putting both server and fetch in ONE ctx_execute call instead of using background."
		},
		"cwd": {
			"type": "string",
			"description": "Optional working directory for shell commands. Non-shell languages still execute from their sandbox temp directory."
		},
		"intent": {
			"type": "string",
			"description": "What you're looking for in the output. When provided and output is large (>5KB), indexes output into knowledge base and returns section titles + previews — not full content. Use ctx_search(queries: [...]) to retrieve specific sections. Example: 'failing tests', 'HTTP 500 errors'.\n\nTIP: Use specific technical terms, not just concepts. Check 'Searchable terms' in the response for available vocabulary."
		}
	},
	"required": ["language", "code"]
}`
```

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"unicode/utf8"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/store"
)

// Review focus: binary output through the whole ctx_execute path (index +
// intent search) must reach the client as valid UTF-8 and stay searchable.
func TestExecuteBinaryOutputIsValidUTF8(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out, isErr := h.call("ctx_execute", `{"language": "shell", "code": "head -c 200000 /dev/urandom; echo; echo marker-zebra-42"}`)
	if isErr {
		t.Fatalf("isError:\n%.300s", out)
	}
	if !utf8.ValidString(out) {
		t.Fatal("result text is not valid UTF-8")
	}
	if !strings.Contains(out, "Indexed") {
		t.Fatalf("output over 100KB should be indexed:\n%.300s", out)
	}
	res, err := h.Deps.Store.SearchWithFallback("zebra", store.SearchOpts{Project: h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey, Limit: 3})
	if err != nil || len(res) == 0 {
		t.Fatalf("marker not searchable after binary output: %v %v", res, err)
	}
}
```

Run: `go test ./internal/mcpserver/ -run TestExecuteBinaryOutputIsValidUTF8 -v`
Expected: PASS. This pins behavior the earlier steps already implement; if it fails, fix the implementation, not the test.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/mcpserver/executefile.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/executor"
	"github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk/mcp"
)

// registerExecuteFile registers ctx_execute_file. Port of S:2042-2230.
func registerExecuteFile(s *mcp.Server, d *Deps) {
	s.AddTool(&mcp.Tool{
		Name:        "ctx_execute_file",
		Title:       "Run code over a file (executes code, reads the given path)",
		Description: executeFileDesc,
		Annotations: execAnnotations,
		InputSchema: json.RawMessage(executeFileSchema),
	}, func(ctx context.Context, req *mcp.CallToolRequest) (*mcp.CallToolResult, error) {
		a, err := ParseArgs(req.Params.Arguments)
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		path, ok, err := a.String("path")
		if err == nil && !ok {
			err = errors.New("path: required")
		}
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		e, err := parseExecArgs(a)
		if err != nil {
			return ArgError(err), nil
		}
		// Project boundary, then Read() deny, then the code deny check (S:2118-2132).
		if err := d.Policy.CheckReadPath(path); err != nil {
			return TextResult(err.Error(), true), nil
		}
		if denied := denyCode(d, e); denied != nil {
			return denied, nil
		}

		r, err := d.Exec.Run(ctx, executor.Request{Language: e.lang, Code: e.code, Timeout: e.timeout, FilePath: path})
		if err != nil {
			return ErrorResult("Runtime error: %v", err), nil
		}
		echo := buildExecuteEcho(string(e.lang), e.code, path)
		if r.TimedOut {
			return TextResult(echo+"Timed out processing "+path+" after "+e.msText()+"ms", true), nil
		}
		return finishRun(d, r, e, echo, "file:"+path), nil
	})
}

// Description and schema below are generated from server.ts S:2056-2112.

const executeFileDesc = `Read a file into a sandboxed FILE_CONTENT variable and run code over it. Only what you console.log() enters your conversation — the file bytes stay in the sandbox.

Think-in-Code applied to file-level analysis: Reading the whole file means every byte enters your conversation memory and costs reasoning capacity for the rest of the session. Running code over it here lets you keep the raw bytes out and only the derived answer in. Same principle as ctx_execute, scoped to one named file via the FILE_CONTENT variable.

WHEN:
  - You want to KNOW SOMETHING ABOUT a file (line count, matches of a pattern, parsed structure, statistical aggregate) without needing to SEE all of it
  - The file is structured (CSV, JSON, log, code) and a code-level derivation is cheaper than reading verbatim
  - The file is large enough that reading the full content would burn meaningful conversation memory you need for the actual work
  - The derivation may itself produce a large output you want recall-by-topic on later — pass an ` + "`" + `intent` + "`" + ` string; outputs over ~5KB are auto-indexed and only matching sections come back, retrievable via ctx_search

WHEN NOT:
  - You intend to EDIT the file — use Read so the subsequent Edit can match the exact text
  - You only need one specific line and you know its offset — Read with offset/limit is the simplest path
  - The file is small AND you will consume all of it for understanding/editing — Read directly

RETURNS:
  Only what your code prints. The FILE_CONTENT variable holds the raw bytes inside the sandbox; nothing else leaves. When ` + "`" + `intent` + "`" + ` is set and output exceeds the auto-index threshold, the response carries searchable section titles + previews instead of the raw stdout.

EXAMPLE: ctx_execute_file(path: "huge.log", language: "javascript", code: "const errs = FILE_CONTENT.split('\\n').filter(l => /ERROR|FATAL/.test(l)); console.log(` + "`" + `${errs.length} error lines` + "`" + `); console.log(errs.slice(-5).join('\\n'))")
EXAMPLE: ctx_execute_file(path: "data.csv", language: "javascript", code: "const rows = FILE_CONTENT.split('\\n'); console.log(` + "`" + `rows: ${rows.length - 1}, header: ${rows[0]}` + "`" + `)")`

const executeFileSchema = `{
	"type": "object",
	"properties": {
		"path": {
			"type": "string",
			"description": "Absolute file path or relative to project root"
		},
		"language": {
			"type": "string",
			"enum": ["javascript", "typescript", "python", "shell", "ruby", "go", "rust", "php", "perl", "r", "elixir", "csharp"],
			"description": "Runtime language"
		},
		"code": {
			"type": "string",
			"description": "Code to process FILE_CONTENT (file_content in Elixir). Print summary via console.log/print/echo/IO.puts/Console.WriteLine."
		},
		"timeout": {
			"type": ["number", "string"],
			"description": "Max execution time in ms. When omitted, no server-side timer fires — the MCP host's RPC timeout governs."
		},
		"intent": {
			"type": "string",
			"description": "What you're looking for in the output. When provided and output is large (>5KB), returns only matching sections via BM25 search instead of truncated output."
		}
	},
	"required": ["path", "language", "code"]
}`
```

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"testing"
	"time"
)

func TestFloodGuardThresholdsAndWindow(t *testing.T) {
	var g floodGuard
	t0 := time.Date(2026, 10, 20, 12, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	for i := 1; i <= 9; i++ {
		d := g.record(t0.Add(time.Duration(i) * time.Second))
		if d.count != i {
			t.Fatalf("call %d: count %d", i, d.count)
		}
		if d.softCapped != (i > 3) || d.blocked != (i > 8) {
			t.Fatalf("call %d: softCapped=%v blocked=%v", i, d.softCapped, d.blocked)
		}
		if !d.windowStart.Equal(t0.Add(time.Second)) {
			t.Fatalf("call %d: windowStart %v", i, d.windowStart)
		}
	}
	// Exactly 60s after the window start is still inside the window (TS uses >).
	if d := g.record(t0.Add(61 * time.Second)); d.count != 10 {
		t.Fatalf("at +60s: count %d, want 10", d.count)
	}
	d := g.record(t0.Add(62 * time.Second))
	if d.count != 1 || d.blocked || d.softCapped || !d.windowStart.Equal(t0.Add(62*time.Second)) {
		t.Fatalf("after window: %+v", d)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

`internal/mcpserver/floodguard.go`:

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"sync"
	"time"
)

// ctx_search throttle defaults (S:2457-2459); the env overrides are dropped.
const (
	searchWindow     = 60 * time.Second
	searchSoftCapAt  = 3 // after this many calls: 1 result per query
	searchBlockAfter = 8 // after this many calls: refuse
)

// floodGuard is the ctx_search rolling-window call counter. TS keys it per
// agent context; one cmgo process serves one session, so one bucket suffices.
type floodGuard struct {
	mu          sync.Mutex
	count       int
	windowStart time.Time
}

type floodDecision struct {
	count       int
	windowStart time.Time
	blocked     bool
	softCapped  bool
}

// record counts one call at now. Port of FloodGuard.record (flood-guard.ts:75-90).
func (g *floodGuard) record(now time.Time) floodDecision {
	g.mu.Lock()
	defer g.mu.Unlock()
	if g.count == 0 || now.Sub(g.windowStart) > searchWindow {
		g.count, g.windowStart = 0, now
	}
	g.count++
	return floodDecision{
		count:       g.count,
		windowStart: g.windowStart,
		blocked:     g.count > searchBlockAfter,
		softCapped:  g.count > searchSoftCapAt,
	}
}
```

```go
package mcpserver

import (
	"regexp"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/lang315/context-mode-go/internal/store"
)

// sizeRe stands in for a "(12.3KB)"-style size in a pinned output.
const sizeRe = `\d+\.\dKB`

// pinOutput turns want into an anchored regexp: literal text, except that
// every "<KB>" placeholder matches a size.
func pinOutput(t *testing.T, got, want string) {
	t.Helper()
	parts := strings.Split(want, "<KB>")
	for i, p := range parts {
		parts[i] = regexp.QuoteMeta(p)
	}
	re := regexp.MustCompile(`\A` + strings.Join(parts, sizeRe) + `\z`)
	if !re.MatchString(got) {
		t.Fatalf("output mismatch\n--- got ---\n%s\n--- want ---\n%s", got, want)
	}
}

func TestBatchOutputStructure(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out, isErr := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{
		"commands": [
			{"label": "alpha", "command": "echo   alphaword"},
			{"label": "beta", "command": "echo betaword; echo betaerr >&2"}
		],
		"queries": ["alphaword", "betaerr"]
	}`)
	if isErr {
		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %s", out)
	}
	pinOutput(t, out, "Executed 2 commands (15 lines, <KB>). Indexed 2 sections. Searched 2 queries.\n"+
		"\n"+
		"## Commands\n"+
		"\n"+
		"- alpha: `echo alphaword`\n"+
		"- beta: `echo betaword; echo betaerr >&2`\n"+
		"\n"+
		"## Indexed Sections\n"+
		"\n"+
		"- alpha (<KB>)\n"+
		"- beta (<KB>)\n"+
		"\n"+
		"## alphaword\n"+
		"\n"+
		"### alpha\n"+
		"# alpha\n\n$ echo alphaword\n\nalphaword\n"+
		"\n"+
		"## betaerr\n"+
		"\n"+
		"### beta\n"+
		"# beta\n\n$ echo betaword; echo betaerr >&2\n\nbetaword\nbetaerr\n"+
		"\n"+
		"\n> **Tip:** Results are scoped to this batch only. To search across all indexed sources, use `ctx_search(queries: [...])` or call ctx_batch_execute with `query_scope: \"global\"`.\n")

	src, err := h.Deps.Store.SourceByLabel(h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey, "batch:alpha,beta")
	if err != nil || src == nil || src.ChunkCount != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("batch source not indexed under batch:alpha,beta: %+v %v", src, err)
	}
}

func TestBatchSearchableTermsLine(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	// DistinctiveTerms needs >= 3 chunks; "sharedterm" appears in 2 of 3.
	out, _ := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{
		"commands": [
			{"label": "one", "command": "echo sharedterm"},
			{"label": "two", "command": "echo sharedterm"},
			{"label": "three", "command": "echo other"}
		],
		"queries": ["sharedterm"]
	}`)
	if !regexp.MustCompile(`\n\nSearchable terms for follow-up: [^\n]*sharedterm[^\n]*\z`).MatchString(out) {
		t.Fatalf("missing terms line:\n%s", out)
	}
}

func TestBatchBareStringCommandsAndJSONStringQueries(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out, isErr := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{"commands": ["echo barehello", "echo baretwo"], "queries": "[\"barehello\"]"}`)
	if isErr {
		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %s", out)
	}
	for _, want := range []string{"- cmd_1: `echo barehello`\n- cmd_2: `echo baretwo`\n", "## barehello\n\n### cmd_1\n", "Searched 1 queries."} {
		if !strings.Contains(out, want) {
			t.Fatalf("missing %q in:\n%s", want, out)
		}
	}
	// A whole JSON-string commands array and a bare-string query also work.
	out, isErr = h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{"commands": "[{\"label\":\"j\",\"command\":\"echo jsonform\"}]", "queries": "jsonform"}`)
	if isErr || !strings.Contains(out, "## jsonform\n\n### j\n") {
		t.Fatalf("JSON-string commands failed (isErr=%v):\n%s", isErr, out)
	}
}

func TestBatchSerialSharedBudget(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out, isErr := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{
		"commands": [
			{"label": "slow", "command": "echo started; sleep 5"},
			{"label": "later", "command": "echo never"}
		],
		"queries": ["started"], "timeout": 300
	}`)
	if isErr {
		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %s", out)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(out, "- later (") || strings.Contains(out, "never\n") {
		t.Fatalf("later command should be listed but not run:\n%s", out)
	}
	sec := sectionContent(t, h, "batch:slow,later", "later")
	if sec != "# later\n\n(skipped — batch timeout exceeded)\n" && sec != "# later\n\n(skipped — batch timeout exceeded)" {
		t.Fatalf("later section = %q", sec)
	}

	// A spent budget skips every command before it runs (S:1580).
	out, _ = h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{"commands": ["echo a", "echo b"], "queries": ["skipped"], "timeout": "0"}`)
	if strings.Count(out, "(skipped — batch timeout exceeded)") != 2 || !strings.Contains(out, "Executed 2 commands (8 lines,") {
		t.Fatalf("zero budget should skip both:\n%s", out)
	}
}

func TestBatchParallelPerCommandTimeout(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	out, isErr := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{
		"commands": [
			{"label": "slow", "command": "echo partialout; sleep 5"},
			{"label": "quick", "command": "echo quickout"},
			{"label": "fast", "command": "echo fastout"}
		],
		"queries": ["timed out"], "timeout": 300, "concurrency": 3
	}`)
	if isErr {
		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %s", out)
	}
	if strings.Contains(out, "skipped") {
		t.Fatalf("parallel mode must not skip:\n%s", out)
	}
	if got := sectionContent(t, h, "batch:slow,quick,fast", "slow"); !strings.HasSuffix(got, "partialout\n\n(timed out after 300ms)") {
		t.Fatalf("slow section = %q", got)
	}
	if got := sectionContent(t, h, "batch:slow,quick,fast", "quick"); !strings.Contains(got, "quickout") {
		t.Fatalf("quick section = %q", got)
	}
}

// sectionContent returns the indexed chunk titled title in source label.
func sectionContent(t *testing.T, h *harness, label, title string) string {
	t.Helper()
	res, err := h.Deps.Store.SearchWithFallback(title, store.SearchOpts{Project: h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey, Source: label, SourceExact: true, Limit: 10})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for _, r := range res {
		if r.Title == title {
			return r.Content
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("no chunk %q in %s (got %d results)", title, label, len(res))
	return ""
}

func TestBatchDenyPattern(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	h.writeSettings("Bash(sudo *)")
	out, isErr := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{"commands": ["echo fine", "echo ok && sudo ls"], "queries": ["x"]}`)
	if !isErr || out != "Command blocked by security policy: matches deny pattern Bash(sudo *)" {
		t.Fatalf("got isErr=%v %q", isErr, out)
	}
	if n, _ := h.Deps.Store.ChunkCount(h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey); n != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("nothing should have run or been indexed, chunks=%d", n)
	}
}

func TestBatchQueryScope(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	if _, err := h.Deps.Store.Index(h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey, "notes", []store.Chunk{{Title: "Zebra notes", Content: "zebrafish regenerate fins"}}); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	out, _ := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{"commands": ["echo unrelated"], "queries": ["zebrafish"]}`)
	if !strings.Contains(out, "## zebrafish\n\nNo matching sections found.\n\n\n> **Tip:**") {
		t.Fatalf("batch scope leaked other sources:\n%s", out)
	}
	out, _ = h.call("ctx_batch_execute", `{"commands": ["echo unrelated"], "queries": ["zebrafish"], "query_scope": "global"}`)
	want := "## zebrafish\n\n### Zebra notes\nzebrafish regenerate fins\n\n\n> **Scope:** Queries searched the entire persistent index (query_scope: \"global\").\n"
	if !strings.Contains(out, want) {
		t.Fatalf("global scope missing other source:\n%s", out)
	}
}

func TestBatchQueryOutputCap(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	if _, err := h.Deps.Store.Index(h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey, "big", []store.Chunk{{Title: "Big", Content: strings.Repeat("capword filler ", 20)}}); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	got, err := formatBatchQueryResults(h.Deps.Store, h.Deps.Cfg.ProjectKey, []string{"capword", "second"}, "big", 100, "batch")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	want := "## second\n(output cap reached — use ctx_search(queries: [\"second\"]) for details)\n"
	if len(got) != 7 || got[0] != "## capword" || got[2] != "### Big" || got[5] != want {
		t.Fatalf("got %q", got)
	}
}

func TestBatchArgErrors(t *testing.T) {
	h := newHarness(t, nil)
	for args, want := range map[string]string{
		`{"commands": ["echo a"], "queries": ["a"], "concurrency": 9}`:            "Invalid arguments: concurrency: expected an integer from 1 to 8",
		`{"commands": ["echo a"]}`:                                                "Invalid arguments: queries: expected a non-empty array",
		`{"commands": [], "queries": ["a"]}`:                                      "Invalid arguments: commands: expected a non-empty array",
		`{"commands": ["echo a"], "queries": ["a"], "query_scope": "everything"}`: `Invalid arguments: query_scope: expected "batch" or "global"`,
	} {
		if out, isErr := h.call("ctx_batch_execute", args); !isErr || out != want {
			t.Errorf("%s: got isErr=%v %q", args, isErr, out)
		}
	}
}

func TestTruncateCommandForEcho(t *testing.T) {
	if got := truncateCommandForEcho("  a \n\t b  "); got != "a b" {
		t.Fatalf("collapse: %q", got)
	}
	long := strings.Repeat("x", 501)
	if got := truncateCommandForEcho(long); got != strings.Repeat("x", 500)+"…" {
		t.Fatalf("cut: len %d", len(got))
	}
	if got := jsPrefix("a😀b", 2); got != "a" {
		t.Fatalf("jsPrefix must not split a surrogate pair: %q", got)
	}
	if jsLen("a😀") != 3 {
		t.Fatal("jsLen counts UTF-16 units")
	}
}
```
