`bench/driver.py`:

```python
"""Shared JSON-RPC driver for the cmgo acceptance scripts (macOS only).

Usage from the other scripts: start N `cmgo serve` processes against one temp
CMGO_DIR, run initialize, optionally call every tool once, and read per-process
memory and disk-write counters with proc_pid_rusage.
"""
import ctypes
import http.server
import json
import os
import subprocess
import tempfile
import threading

_libc = ctypes.CDLL("/usr/lib/libSystem.B.dylib")


def rusage(pid):
    """Return (resident MB, phys_footprint MB, diskio bytes written) or None."""
    # RUSAGE_INFO_V2: 16-byte uuid, then uint64 fields:
    # resident_size=6, phys_footprint=7, diskio_byteswritten=17
    b = (ctypes.c_uint64 * 64)()
    if _libc.proc_pid_rusage(pid, 2, ctypes.byref(b)) != 0:
        return None
    return b[2 + 6] / 2**20, b[2 + 7] / 2**20, b[2 + 17]


class Server:
    def __init__(self, binary, env):
        self.p = subprocess.Popen([binary, "serve"], stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                                  stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True, env=env)
        self.next_id = 1

    def rpc(self, method, params):
        i = self.next_id
        self.next_id += 1
        self.p.stdin.write(json.dumps({"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": i, "method": method, "params": params}) + "\n")
        self.p.stdin.flush()
        while True:
            line = self.p.stdout.readline()
            if not line:
                raise RuntimeError("server exited: " + self.p.stderr.read())
            m = json.loads(line)
            if m.get("id") == i:
                if "error" in m:
                    raise RuntimeError(f"{method}: {m['error']}")
                return m["result"]

    def initialize(self):
        self.rpc("initialize", {"protocolVersion": "2025-06-18", "capabilities": {},
                                "clientInfo": {"name": "bench", "version": "0"}})
        self.p.stdin.write(json.dumps({"jsonrpc": "2.0", "method": "notifications/initialized"}) + "\n")
        self.p.stdin.flush()

    def call(self, name, args):
        r = self.rpc("tools/call", {"name": name, "arguments": args})
        text = "".join(c.get("text", "") for c in r.get("content", []))
        if r.get("isError"):
            raise RuntimeError(f"{name} returned isError: {text[:300]}")
        return text

    def stop(self):
        self.p.stdin.close()
        self.p.wait(timeout=10)


def serve_html():
    """Serve one small HTML page on 127.0.0.1; returns its URL."""
    class H(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def do_GET(self):
            body = b"<html><body><h1>Bench</h1><p>context window protection</p></body></html>"
            self.send_response(200)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", "text/html")
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass

    srv = http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", 0), H)
    threading.Thread(target=srv.serve_forever, daemon=True).start()
    return f"http://127.0.0.1:{srv.server_address[1]}/page"


def bench_env(path=None):
    root = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="cmgo-bench-")
    project = os.path.join(root, "project")
    os.makedirs(project)
    with open(os.path.join(project, "notes.txt"), "w") as f:
        f.write("alpha\nbeta\ngamma\n")
    env = {**os.environ, "HOME": os.path.join(root, "home"), "CMGO_DIR": os.path.join(root, "data"),
           "CMGO_PROJECT_DIR": project}
    if path is not None:
        env["PATH"] = path
    return env


def call_every_tool(s, url):
    """One call per tool; uses only shell and python so Node is never needed."""
    s.call("ctx_execute", {"language": "shell", "code": "echo hello"})
    s.call("ctx_execute", {"language": "python", "code": "print(sum(range(10)))"})
    s.call("ctx_execute_file", {"path": "notes.txt", "language": "shell", "code": 'echo "$FILE_CONTENT" | wc -l'})
    s.call("ctx_batch_execute", {"commands": [{"label": "ls", "command": "ls"}], "queries": ["notes"]})
    s.call("ctx_fetch_and_index", {"url": url, "source": "bench"})
    s.call("ctx_search", {"queries": ["context window"]})
```

`bench/footprint.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3 -I
"""Criterion 1: sum of phys_footprint for 8 `cmgo serve` processes <= 100 MB.

Run: go build -o bin/cmgo ./cmd/cmgo && python3 -I bench/footprint.py bin/cmgo
"""
import os
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import driver  # noqa: E402

N, LIMIT_MB = 8, 100.0


def main(binary):
    env = driver.bench_env()
    url = driver.serve_html()
    servers = []
    for _ in range(N):
        s = driver.Server(binary, env)
        s.initialize()
        driver.call_every_tool(s, url)
        servers.append(s)
    usage = [driver.rusage(s.p.pid) for s in servers]
    for s in servers:
        s.stop()
    rss = sum(u[0] for u in usage)
    fp = sum(u[1] for u in usage)
    print(f"{N} servers: RSS sum {rss:.1f} MB | phys_footprint sum {fp:.1f} MB ({fp / N:.1f}/proc)")
    ok = fp <= LIMIT_MB
    print("PASS" if ok else f"FAIL: phys_footprint sum {fp:.1f} MB > {LIMIT_MB} MB")
    return 0 if ok else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1]))
```

`bench/idle-writes.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3 -I
"""Criterion 2: 8 idle `cmgo serve` processes write <= 0.1 MB/min over 60 s.

Run: go build -o bin/cmgo ./cmd/cmgo && python3 -I bench/idle-writes.py bin/cmgo [seconds]
"""
import os
import sys
import time

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import driver  # noqa: E402

N, LIMIT_MB_PER_MIN = 8, 0.1


def main(binary, seconds):
    env = driver.bench_env()
    url = driver.serve_html()
    servers = []
    for _ in range(N):
        s = driver.Server(binary, env)
        s.initialize()
        driver.call_every_tool(s, url)  # warm: DB open, schema, one write each
        servers.append(s)
    before = [driver.rusage(s.p.pid)[2] for s in servers]
    time.sleep(seconds)
    after = [driver.rusage(s.p.pid)[2] for s in servers]
    for s in servers:
        s.stop()
    written = sum(a - b for a, b in zip(after, before))
    rate = written / 2**20 * 60 / seconds
    print(f"{N} idle servers over {seconds}s: {written} bytes written = {rate:.3f} MB/min")
    ok = rate <= LIMIT_MB_PER_MIN
    print("PASS" if ok else f"FAIL: {rate:.3f} MB/min > {LIMIT_MB_PER_MIN}")
    return 0 if ok else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1], int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 60))
```

`bench/no-node.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3 -I
"""Criterion 4: every tool works with node and bun absent from PATH
(JavaScript/TypeScript inside ctx_execute excepted).

Run: go build -o bin/cmgo ./cmd/cmgo && python3 -I bench/no-node.py bin/cmgo
"""
import os
import shutil
import sys

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import driver  # noqa: E402


def path_without_js_runtimes():
    keep = []
    for d in os.environ.get("PATH", "").split(os.pathsep):
        if any(os.path.exists(os.path.join(d, b)) for b in ("node", "bun")):
            continue
        keep.append(d)
    return os.pathsep.join(keep)


def main(binary):
    path = path_without_js_runtimes()
    for b in ("node", "bun"):
        if shutil.which(b, path=path):
            print(f"FAIL: could not remove {b} from PATH")
            return 1
    s = driver.Server(binary, driver.bench_env(path=path))
    s.initialize()
    try:
        driver.call_every_tool(s, driver.serve_html())
    except RuntimeError as e:
        print(f"FAIL: {e}")
        return 1
    finally:
        s.stop()
    print("PASS: all 5 tools ran without node or bun on PATH")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1]))
```

`testdata/corpus/md/01-getting-started.md`:

```markdown
# Getting Started

Welcome to the connection pool library. It keeps a small set of database
connections open and hands them out to request handlers.

## Installation

Install the package with your package manager and import it in your service.
The library has no runtime dependencies beyond the database driver.

## Configuration

Set `max_connections` to the number of connections the pool may open.
The default is 10, which suits most small services.

### Timeouts

The `idle_timeout` closes connections that sit unused. The `connect_timeout`
bounds how long a request waits for a free connection before it fails.

#### Retry policy

Failed connects are retried three times with exponential backoff.

## Configuration

A second Configuration section: environment variables override the file.
Set `POOL_MAX_CONNECTIONS` to change the pool size at deploy time.

## Next steps

Read the API reference and the troubleshooting guide.
```

`testdata/corpus/md/02-api-reference.md`:

`````markdown
# API Reference

## Pool.Get

`Get` returns a connection from the pool, blocking until one is free.

```go
func (p *Pool) Get(ctx context.Context) (*Conn, error) {
	select {
	case c := <-p.idle:
		return c, nil
	case <-ctx.Done():
		return nil, ctx.Err()
	}
}
```

## Pool.Put

Return a connection with `Put`. A broken connection is closed instead.

```js
// # this comment is not a heading
pool.put(conn);
```

## Shell helpers

```bash
# Start the pool exporter
# ## also not a heading
./exporter --listen :9090
```

### Nested fences

````markdown
```sql
SELECT count(*) FROM connections;
```
````

Text after the four-backtick fence.

```
unterminated fence at end of file
# Not a heading inside an open fence
`````

`testdata/corpus/md/03-long-code.md`:

`````markdown
# Rate Limiter Source

The full token bucket implementation is kept in one fenced block so it is never split.

```go
package ratelimit

import (
	"errors"
	"sync"
	"time"
)

// ErrLimited is returned when the bucket has no tokens left.
var ErrLimited = errors.New("ratelimit: too many requests")

// Bucket is a token bucket rate limiter.
type Bucket struct {
	mu       sync.Mutex
	capacity int
	tokens   int
	refill   time.Duration
	last     time.Time
	now      func() time.Time
}

// NewBucket returns a full bucket that adds one token per refill interval.
func NewBucket(capacity int, refill time.Duration) *Bucket {
	return &Bucket{
		capacity: capacity,
		tokens:   capacity,
		refill:   refill,
		last:     time.Now(),
		now:      time.Now,
	}
}

// Take removes one token or returns ErrLimited.
func (b *Bucket) Take() error {
	b.mu.Lock()
	defer b.mu.Unlock()
	b.add()
	if b.tokens == 0 {
		return ErrLimited
	}
	b.tokens--
	return nil
}

// TakeN removes n tokens at once or none of them.
func (b *Bucket) TakeN(n int) error {
	b.mu.Lock()
	defer b.mu.Unlock()
	b.add()
	if b.tokens < n {
		return ErrLimited
	}
	b.tokens -= n
	return nil
}

// Available reports the tokens left after refilling.
func (b *Bucket) Available() int {
	b.mu.Lock()
	defer b.mu.Unlock()
	b.add()
	return b.tokens
}

// add refills tokens for the time elapsed since the last call.
func (b *Bucket) add() {
	now := b.now()
	elapsed := now.Sub(b.last)
	if elapsed < b.refill {
		return
	}
	gained := int(elapsed / b.refill)
	b.tokens += gained
	if b.tokens > b.capacity {
		b.tokens = b.capacity
	}
	b.last = b.last.Add(time.Duration(gained) * b.refill)
}

// Limiter keeps one bucket per client key.
type Limiter struct {
	mu       sync.Mutex
	buckets  map[string]*Bucket
	capacity int
	refill   time.Duration
}

// NewLimiter returns a limiter that creates buckets lazily.
func NewLimiter(capacity int, refill time.Duration) *Limiter {
	return &Limiter{
		buckets:  make(map[string]*Bucket),
		capacity: capacity,
		refill:   refill,
	}
}

// Allow takes one token from the bucket for key.
func (l *Limiter) Allow(key string) bool {
	return l.bucket(key).Take() == nil
}

// bucket returns the bucket for key, creating it on first use.
func (l *Limiter) bucket(key string) *Bucket {
	l.mu.Lock()
	defer l.mu.Unlock()
	b, ok := l.buckets[key]
	if !ok {
		b = NewBucket(l.capacity, l.refill)
		l.buckets[key] = b
	}
	return b
}

// Sweep drops buckets that are full again, so idle clients free memory.
func (l *Limiter) Sweep() int {
	l.mu.Lock()
	defer l.mu.Unlock()
	dropped := 0
	for key, b := range l.buckets {
		if b.Available() == l.capacity {
			delete(l.buckets, key)
			dropped++
		}
	}
	return dropped
}

// Len reports how many client buckets are live.
func (l *Limiter) Len() int {
	l.mu.Lock()
	defer l.mu.Unlock()
	return len(l.buckets)
}

// Middleware rejects requests over the limit with status 429.
func Middleware(l *Limiter, next http.Handler) http.Handler {
	return http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		key := clientKey(r)
		if !l.Allow(key) {
			w.Header().Set("Retry-After", "1")
			http.Error(w, ErrLimited.Error(), http.StatusTooManyRequests)
			return
		}
		next.ServeHTTP(w, r)
	})
}

// clientKey picks the forwarded address when a trusted proxy set one.
func clientKey(r *http.Request) string {
	if fwd := r.Header.Get("X-Forwarded-For"); fwd != "" {
		if i := strings.IndexByte(fwd, ','); i >= 0 {
			return strings.TrimSpace(fwd[:i])
		}
		return strings.TrimSpace(fwd)
	}
	host, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(r.RemoteAddr)
	if err != nil {
		return r.RemoteAddr
	}
	return host
}

// Stats is a snapshot of limiter counters for the metrics endpoint.
type Stats struct {
	Clients  int `json:"clients"`
	Capacity int `json:"capacity"`
	RefillMS int `json:"refill_ms"`
}

// Snapshot reports the current limiter counters.
func (l *Limiter) Snapshot() Stats {
	return Stats{
		Clients:  l.Len(),
		Capacity: l.capacity,
		RefillMS: int(l.refill / time.Millisecond),
	}
}

// StartSweeper runs Sweep on every tick until stop is closed.
func (l *Limiter) StartSweeper(every time.Duration, stop <-chan struct{}) {
	t := time.NewTicker(every)
	go func() {
		defer t.Stop()
		for {
			select {
			case <-t.C:
				l.Sweep()
			case <-stop:
				return
			}
		}
	}()
}
```

## Usage

Call `Allow` with the client address before serving each request.
`````

`testdata/corpus/md/05-oversized.md`:

```markdown
# Incident Report

## Timeline

At 09:12 UTC the primary database began rejecting new connections. The connection pool on every API node filled within forty seconds, and request latency climbed from a median of 40 ms to more than eight seconds. The on-call engineer was paged by the latency alert at 09:14 and acknowledged it at 09:16. Early dashboards showed the database CPU at normal levels, which pointed away from a load problem and toward a configuration or network fault.

At 09:20 the team noticed that the database had reached its max_connections ceiling. Each API node opened far more connections than its configured pool size, because a deploy earlier that morning had changed the pool from a shared instance to one pool per request handler. With twelve handlers per node and sixty nodes, the fleet tried to hold more than seven thousand connections against a ceiling of two thousand.

At 09:31 the deploy was rolled back on half of the fleet as a test. Connection counts on the database dropped immediately and latency on the rolled-back nodes returned to normal within two minutes. The rest of the fleet was rolled back at 09:38. Error rates fell to baseline by 09:45, and the incident was declared mitigated at 09:50 after fifteen minutes of stable metrics.

During the incident roughly four percent of checkout requests failed outright and another eleven percent completed only after client retries. Background jobs that depend on the same database were paused automatically by their circuit breakers, which kept the job queue from growing without bound. No data was lost, because every write path is idempotent and the clients retried with the same request keys.

The root cause was the per-handler pool introduced by the deploy. The change passed review because the diff looked like a small refactor, and the load test environment runs a single node, where the extra pools stayed well under the connection ceiling. Nothing in the test suite asserted the total number of connections a node may open, so the regression only appeared at fleet scale.

Contributing factors include the missing connection budget alert on the database, a dashboard that showed pool usage per node but not per fleet, and a rollout policy that allowed the deploy to reach every node in under ten minutes. A slower canary phase would have limited the blast radius to a handful of nodes before the ceiling was reached.

Follow-up actions: add a test that fails when a node opens more connections than its budget, alert when the database passes eighty percent of max_connections, add a fleet-wide pool usage panel, and slow the default rollout to a one-hour canary for changes that touch the data access layer. Owners and due dates are tracked in the reliability backlog.

## Raw notes

This section is one very long paragraph with no blank lines inside it, so the paragraph splitter has nothing to split on and must keep it as a single oversized chunk. The notes were typed live during the incident call and cover the same events as the timeline in rougher form. Primary database rejecting connections at nine twelve. Pool full on api nodes within forty seconds. Median latency forty milliseconds before, eight seconds after. Pager fired on latency alert. Database CPU normal, so not load. Checking network between api subnet and database subnet, no packet loss seen. Checking database logs: too many connections errors, thousands per second. Checking max_connections: two thousand, unchanged since last quarter. Counting connections per api node: around one hundred twenty, expected ten. Looking at this morning's deploy: pool construction moved into the handler constructor, so every handler builds its own pool. Twelve handlers per node times sixty nodes times ten connections is seven thousand two hundred, well above two thousand. Decision: roll back half the fleet to confirm. Rolled back nodes recover in two minutes, connection count on database drops by half. Decision: roll back everything. Full rollback complete, error rate back to baseline, latency normal. Circuit breakers paused background jobs, queue depth stable, resumed jobs after mitigation. Checkout failure rate peaked at four percent, retries succeeded for most of the rest. No data loss, all writes idempotent with request keys. Root cause confirmed as per-handler pool. Load test runs one node so it never hit the ceiling. Need a connection budget test, a database connection alert at eighty percent, a fleet pool panel, and a slower canary for data layer changes. Postmortem owner assigned, review scheduled for Thursday. Extra filler so this paragraph alone is larger than the four kilobyte chunk limit: the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog while the pool drains and refills. End of raw notes and the word sentinelword marks the very end.

## Summary

Per-handler pools exceeded the database connection ceiling; rollback fixed it.
```

`testdata/corpus/md/09-troubleshooting.md`:

`````markdown
# Troubleshooting

## Connection refused

If clients log `ECONNREFUSED` or "connection refused", the database is not
listening on the configured port. Check the host, the port and the firewall.

## Timeout errors

A `context deadline exceeded` error means the pool waited longer than
`connect_timeout` for a free connection. Raise `max_connections` or find the
handler that leaks connections without calling `Put`.

## Authentication

Clients see `password authentication failed` when credentials rotate.
Reload the secret and restart the workers.

## Slow queries

Enable the slow query log and look for missing indexes. Queries that scan
large tables hold a connection for the whole scan, which starves the pool.

```sql
EXPLAIN ANALYZE SELECT * FROM orders WHERE customer_id = 42;
```
`````

`testdata/corpus/md/10-changelog.md`:

```markdown
# Changelog

## v2.1.0

- Added `Pool.Stats` with idle, busy and wait counters.
- The rate limiter middleware now sets `Retry-After` on 429 responses.
- Fixed a race in `Pool.Put` when the context was cancelled during return.

## v2.0.0

Breaking: `NewPool` takes an options struct instead of positional arguments.
The `max_connections` option replaces `size`, and `idle_timeout` replaces
`ttl`. Configuration files using the old keys fail to load with a clear error
that names the new key. The migration guide lists every renamed option.

Connections are now validated with a ping before they are handed out when
they have been idle for longer than `validate_after`. This removes most of the
broken pipe errors seen after network maintenance, at the cost of one round
trip on the first request after a quiet period.

## v1.4.2

- Security: redact passwords from connection strings in error messages.
- Fixed `connect_timeout` being ignored when the DNS lookup itself hung.

## v1.4.1

- Fixed a goroutine leak in the health checker when the pool was closed twice.
```

What each file covers:
- `md/01` H1-H4 stack and a repeated `## Configuration` heading (same `source::title` key twice, merged by RRF); `md/02` fenced code with `#` lines inside, a 4-backtick fence around a 3-backtick one, an unterminated fence at EOF; `md/03` a code section over 4096 B (paragraph split `(1)`, `(2)`); `md/04` rules `---`, `***`, `___`, `-----   `, non-rule `- - -`; `md/05` an oversized section split on paragraphs and one single paragraph over 4096 B; `md/06` no heading (`Untitled`), `#NoSpace`, 7 hashes; `md/07` H5 as body text and stack pops; `md/08` multibyte titles and content (UTF-16 lengths, surrogate pairs); `md/09`, `md/10` realistic search targets.
- `logs/sections.log` 5 blank-line sections, a whitespace-only separator, an 80-char title cut; `logs/short.log` ≤ 20 lines (`Output`); `logs/long.log` 3 sections with one ≥ 5000 B, so it falls back to 20-line windows, two of which start on a blank line (`Lines 19-38`, `Lines 37-56`).
- `json/config.json` nested objects, scalars, `null`, `1e-3`, arrays of arrays and of id-less objects, integer-like keys; `json/users.json` an array with `id`/`name`; `json/large.json` an array over the byte cap, batched and titled by `path`.
- `html/table.html` a GFM table, a header-less table, an ordered list, a blockquote; `html/strip.html` header/nav/footer/script/style/noscript to remove, nested lists, `<del>`, entities, `<br>`, `<hr>`, image, titled link, fenced code with language.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the generator**

`tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs`:

```js
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Golden parity generator for cmgo (spec §5.2). Run by hand, never by go test:
//
//   TS_REPO=/path/to/context-mode node tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs
//
// Reads testdata/corpus/ and writes testdata/golden/{chunks,sanitize,search,
// snippets,tools,html}.json from the TS fork at $TS_REPO (commit a5e6dd31).
// Everything the TS code writes goes to a fresh temp dir: HOME, TMPDIR,
// CONTEXT_MODE_DIR, CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR and the server cwd all point there.
// Nothing is written to ~/.claude or $TS_REPO; start.mjs/postinstall never run.
import { spawn, spawnSync, execFileSync } from "node:child_process";
import { cpSync, mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from "node:url";

const SELF = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const ROOT = resolve(dirname(SELF), "../..");
const CORPUS = join(ROOT, "testdata/corpus");
const OUT = join(ROOT, "testdata/golden");
const TS_REPO = resolve(process.env.TS_REPO || "/Users/lang/GolandProjects/github.com/lang315/context-mode");
const TS_COMMIT = "a5e6dd31";

const writeJSON = (name, v) => writeFileSync(join(OUT, name), JSON.stringify(v, null, 2) + "\n");

// Corpus files in a fixed order; the Go tests index them in this same order.
function corpusFiles() {
  const kinds = { md: "markdown", logs: "plain", json: "json" };
  const files = [];
  for (const dir of Object.keys(kinds)) {
    for (const f of readdirSync(join(CORPUS, dir)).sort()) {
      files.push({ path: `${dir}/${f}`, kind: kinds[dir] });
    }
  }
  return files;
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Store phase (runs under tsx: imports $TS_REPO/src/store.ts)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// [id, query, opts]; opts: limit, source, contentType, mode ("like" | "exact").
const SEARCH_CASES = [
  ["q01", "connection pool"],
  ["q02", "max_connections"],
  ["q03", "idle_timeout connect_timeout"],
  ["q04", "rate limiter"],
  ["q05", "Retry-After"],
  ["q06", "heartbeat worker"],
  ["q07", "rollback deploy"],
  ["q08", "connections"],
  ["q09", "configuring"],
  ["q10", "Middlewa"],
  ["q11", "TestPoolTimeout"],
  ["q12", "nil pointer dereference"],
  ["q13", "upstream connection reset"],
  ["q14", "status=429"],
  ["q15", "café"],
  ["q16", "データベース"],
  ["q17", "😀"],
  ["q18", "Ada Lovelace"],
  ["q19", "admin"],
  ["q20", "src/pool"],
  ["q21", "the and of"],
  ["q22", "conection timout"],
  ["q23", "hearbeat"],
  ["q24", "authentcation"],
  ["q25", "zzzz qqqq"],
  ["q26", "quick brown fox"],
  ["q27", "sentinelword"],
  ["q28", "EXPLAIN ANALYZE"],
  ["q29", "pool", { source: "md/0" }],
  ["q30", "pool", { contentType: "code" }],
  ["q31", "connection", { limit: 5 }],
  ["q32", "connection", { source: "logs/sections.log", mode: "exact" }],
  ["q33", "ECONNREFUSED"],
  ["q34", "\"quoted phrase\" (parens) AND NOT pool"],
  ["q35", "a b"],
  ["q36", "webhook", { limit: 1 }],
];

const EXTRA_SANITIZE = [
  "", "   ", "foo:bar baz^2 ~fuzzy", "NEAR near Near", "Error error ERROR",
  "x y zz", "café's naïve", "path/to/file.go", "a-b_c d.e", "(nested (parens))",
];

const SNIPPET_MAXES = [200, 1500, 3000];

async function storePhase(tmp) {
  const { ContentStore, sanitizeQuery, sanitizeTrigramQuery } = await import(pathToFileURL(join(TS_REPO, "src/store.ts")).href);

  // extractSnippet lives in server.ts and is not reachable without starting
  // the server, so copy its source slice (positionsFromHighlight,
  // stripMarkers, extractSnippet) into a temp module.
  const server = readFileSync(join(TS_REPO, "src/server.ts"), "utf8");
  const start = server.indexOf('const STX = "\\x02";');
  const end = server.indexOf("export type BatchQueryScope");
  if (start < 0 || end < start) throw new Error("extractSnippet slice anchors not found in server.ts");
  const snippetFile = join(tmp, "snippet.ts");
  writeFileSync(snippetFile, server.slice(start, end));
  const { extractSnippet } = await import(pathToFileURL(snippetFile).href);

  const store = new ContentStore(join(tmp, "store.db"));
  const files = corpusFiles();
  const chunkFiles = [];
  for (const f of files) {
    const text = readFileSync(join(CORPUS, f.path), "utf8");
    const r = f.kind === "markdown" ? store.index({ content: text, source: f.path })
      : f.kind === "plain" ? store.indexPlainText(text, f.path)
      : store.indexJSON(text, f.path);
    const chunks = store.getChunksBySource(r.sourceId).map((c) => ({
      title: c.title, content: c.content, hasCode: c.contentType === "code",
    }));
    chunkFiles.push({ ...f, chunks, terms: store.getDistinctiveTerms(r.sourceId) });
  }
  writeJSON("chunks.json", { tsCommit: TS_COMMIT, files: chunkFiles });

  const sanitize = [];
  for (const q of [...SEARCH_CASES.map((c) => c[1]), ...EXTRA_SANITIZE]) {
    sanitize.push({ in: q, porter: sanitizeQuery(q, "OR"), trigram: sanitizeTrigramQuery(q, "OR") });
  }
  writeJSON("sanitize.json", { tsCommit: TS_COMMIT, cases: sanitize });

  const cases = [];
  const snippets = [];
  for (const [id, query, o = {}] of SEARCH_CASES) {
    const limit = o.limit ?? 3;
    const rs = store.searchWithFallback(query, limit, o.source, o.contentType, o.mode ?? "like");
    cases.push({
      id, query, limit, source: o.source ?? "", contentType: o.contentType ?? "", mode: o.mode ?? "like",
      results: rs.map((r) => ({ key: `${r.source}::${r.title}`, contentType: r.contentType, highlighted: r.highlighted ?? "" })),
    });
    rs.forEach((r, i) => {
      const s = {};
      for (const max of SNIPPET_MAXES) s[max] = extractSnippet(r.content, query, max, r.highlighted);
      snippets.push({ id: `${id}#${i}`, query, content: r.content, highlighted: r.highlighted ?? "", snippets: s });
    });
  }
  writeJSON("search.json", { tsCommit: TS_COMMIT, files, cases });
  writeJSON("snippets.json", { tsCommit: TS_COMMIT, cases: snippets });
  store.close();
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// Server phase: drive $TS_REPO/server.bundle.mjs over stdio JSON-RPC
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

// Masks applied to every tool text, here and in the Go test (read from tools.json).
const MASKS = [
  { re: "\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2} \\d{2}:\\d{2}", to: "<DATE>" },
  { re: "\\d+ms", to: "<N>ms" },
  { re: "\\d+\\.\\dKB", to: "<N.N>KB" },
  { re: "search calls in \\d+s", to: "search calls in <N>s" },
];
const mask = (s) => MASKS.reduce((acc, m) => acc.replace(new RegExp(m.re, "g"), m.to), s);

// Tool calls replayed in this order by internal/mcpserver/golden_test.go, one
// fresh server (and store, and search flood guard) per session.
// Batch commands read the corpus through relative paths (cwd = project dir),
// so the echoed "$ cmd" lines are identical in both implementations.
const TOOL_SESSIONS = [[
  ["t01", "ctx_search", { queries: ["pool"] }],
  ["t02", "ctx_batch_execute", {
    commands: [
      { label: "getting started", command: "cat corpus/md/01-getting-started.md" },
      { label: "troubleshooting", command: "cat corpus/md/09-troubleshooting.md" },
      { label: "unicode", command: "cat corpus/md/08-unicode.md" },
    ],
    queries: ["connection refused", "max_connections", "café", "zzzz qqqq"],
  }],
  ["t03", "ctx_batch_execute", {
    commands: [
      { label: "mixed streams", command: "echo stdout line; echo stderr line >&2" },
      { label: "silent", command: "true" },
      { label: "failing", command: "false" },
      { label: "stderr only", command: "echo only-err >&2" },
      { label: "long command", command: "echo" + " word   spaced".repeat(45) },
    ],
    queries: ["stderr line", "spaced"],
  }],
  ["t04", "ctx_batch_execute", {
    commands: [
      { label: "changelog", command: "cat corpus/md/10-changelog.md" },
      { label: "oversized", command: "cat corpus/md/05-oversized.md" },
    ],
    queries: ["pool", "connection ceiling", "rollback"],
    concurrency: 2,
  }],
  ["t05", "ctx_batch_execute", {
    commands: [{ label: "test log", command: "cat corpus/logs/sections.log" }],
    queries: ["connection refused", "TestPoolTimeout"],
    query_scope: "global",
  }],
  ["t06", "ctx_batch_execute", {
    commands: JSON.stringify([{ label: "api", command: "cat corpus/md/02-api-reference.md" }]),
    queries: "Pool.Get",
  }],
  ["t07", "ctx_search", { queries: ["connection pool", "rate limiter", "zzzz qqqq"] }],
  ["t08", "ctx_search", { queries: ["pool"], source: "changelog" }],
  ["t09", "ctx_search", { queries: ["Pool"], contentType: "code", limit: 5 }],
  ["t10", "ctx_search", { queries: [] }],
  ["t11", "ctx_search", { queries: ["deploy"], sort: "timeline" }],
  ["t12", "ctx_search", { queries: ["café", "データベース"] }],
  ["t13", "ctx_search", { queries: ["heartbeat"] }],
  ["t14", "ctx_search", { queries: ["webhook"] }],
  ["t15", "ctx_search", { queries: ["pool"] }],
  ["t16", "ctx_search", { queries: ["pool"] }],
], [
  // Own session: if the legacy `query` call is counted by the flood guard in
  // one implementation and not the other, later throttle lines would all shift.
  ["t17", "ctx_batch_execute", {
    commands: [{ label: "changelog", command: "cat corpus/md/10-changelog.md" }],
    queries: ["breaking"],
  }],
  ["t18", "ctx_search", { query: "rollback" }],
  ["t19", "ctx_search", { queries: ["migration guide"] }],
]];

function rpcClient(child) {
  let buf = "";
  let nextId = 1;
  const pending = new Map();
  child.stdout.setEncoding("utf8");
  child.stdout.on("data", (d) => {
    buf += d;
    let nl;
    while ((nl = buf.indexOf("\n")) >= 0) {
      const line = buf.slice(0, nl).trim();
      buf = buf.slice(nl + 1);
      if (!line) continue;
      let msg;
      try { msg = JSON.parse(line); } catch { continue; }
      if (msg.id !== undefined && pending.has(msg.id)) {
        pending.get(msg.id)(msg);
        pending.delete(msg.id);
      }
    }
  });
  const send = (m) => child.stdin.write(JSON.stringify(m) + "\n");
  return {
    request(method, params) {
      const id = nextId++;
      return new Promise((res, rej) => {
        const timer = setTimeout(() => rej(new Error(`timeout waiting for ${method}`)), 60_000);
        pending.set(id, (m) => { clearTimeout(timer); m.error ? rej(new Error(JSON.stringify(m.error))) : res(m.result); });
        send({ jsonrpc: "2.0", id, method, params });
      });
    },
    notify(method, params) { send({ jsonrpc: "2.0", method, params }); },
  };
}

async function serverSession(tmp, session, toolSteps) {
  const project = join(tmp, "project");
  mkdirSync(project, { recursive: true });
  cpSync(CORPUS, join(project, "corpus"), { recursive: true });
  const env = {
    PATH: process.env.PATH,
    SHELL: process.env.SHELL || "/bin/bash",
    LANG: "en_US.UTF-8",
    HOME: tmp,
    TMPDIR: tmp,
    CONTEXT_MODE_DIR: join(tmp, "context-mode"),
    CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR: project,
  };
  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [join(TS_REPO, "server.bundle.mjs")], {
    cwd: project, env, stdio: ["pipe", "pipe", "ignore"],
  });
  const rpc = rpcClient(child);
  try {
    await rpc.request("initialize", {
      protocolVersion: "2025-06-18", capabilities: {}, clientInfo: { name: "golden-gen", version: "0" },
    });
    rpc.notify("notifications/initialized", {});
    const { tools } = await rpc.request("tools/list", {});
    for (const need of ["ctx_batch_execute", "ctx_search"]) {
      if (!tools.some((t) => t.name === need)) throw new Error(`server did not list ${need}`);
    }
    const steps = [];
    for (const [id, tool, args] of toolSteps) {
      const r = await rpc.request("tools/call", { name: tool, arguments: args });
      const text = (r.content || []).filter((c) => c.type === "text").map((c) => c.text).join("");
      steps.push({ id, session, tool, args, isError: !!r.isError, text: mask(text) });
    }
    return steps;
  } finally {
    child.stdin.end();
    const exited = new Promise((r) => child.once("exit", r));
    const timer = setTimeout(() => child.kill("SIGKILL"), 5000);
    await exited;
    clearTimeout(timer);
  }
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
// HTML phase: Turndown with the options of buildFetchCode (S:3098-3101)
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

function htmlPhase() {
  const require = createRequire(join(TS_REPO, "package.json"));
  const TurndownService = require("turndown");
  const { gfm } = require("turndown-plugin-gfm");
  const cases = [];
  for (const f of readdirSync(join(CORPUS, "html")).sort()) {
    const td = new TurndownService({ headingStyle: "atx", codeBlockStyle: "fenced" });
    td.use(gfm);
    td.remove(["script", "style", "nav", "header", "footer", "noscript"]);
    cases.push({ path: `html/${f}`, markdown: td.turndown(readFileSync(join(CORPUS, "html", f), "utf8")) });
  }
  writeJSON("html.json", { tsCommit: TS_COMMIT, cases });
}

// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

async function main() {
  const phase = process.argv.find((a) => a.startsWith("--phase="))?.slice(8);
  if (phase === "store") return storePhase(process.argv[process.argv.length - 1]);

  const head = execFileSync("git", ["-C", TS_REPO, "rev-parse", "HEAD"], { encoding: "utf8" }).trim();
  if (!head.startsWith(TS_COMMIT)) throw new Error(`TS_REPO is at ${head}, want ${TS_COMMIT}`);
  mkdirSync(OUT, { recursive: true });
  const tmp = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "cmgo-golden-"));
  try {
    const env = { ...process.env, HOME: tmp, TMPDIR: tmp };
    const r = spawnSync(join(TS_REPO, "node_modules/.bin/tsx"), [SELF, "--phase=store", tmp], { env, stdio: "inherit" });
    if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`store phase failed (status ${r.status})`);
    const steps = [];
    for (const [i, toolSteps] of TOOL_SESSIONS.entries()) {
      const dir = join(tmp, `session${i + 1}`);
      mkdirSync(dir);
      steps.push(...await serverSession(dir, i + 1, toolSteps));
    }
    writeJSON("tools.json", { tsCommit: TS_COMMIT, masks: MASKS, steps });
    htmlPhase();
  } finally {
    rmSync(tmp, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
  console.log(`golden files written to ${OUT}`);
}

main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the known-differences list**

The test symbols it names (`goldenToolKnown`, `htmlNormalizers`, `htmlKnown`) arrive in Task 34 and Task 35.

`testdata/golden/KNOWN-DIFFS.md`:

```markdown
# Known differences: cmgo vs TS golden output

The golden files in this directory are TS output (`tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs`,
TS commit `a5e6dd31`). Spec success criterion 3 allows only the differences
listed here. Each one has an ID and the golden case IDs it touches. The Go
tests skip or normalize exactly these cases and fail if a listed difference
goes away (so this list cannot go stale silently).

Case ID forms: `chunks:<path>`, `terms:<path>`, `sanitize:<query>`,
`search:qNN`, `snippet:qNN#i@max` (store); `tNN` (tools.json step);
`html:<path>` (fetch).

## Bugs (parity bugs, not by design)

These cases assert the **current Go output**, pinned in the test, until the
bug is fixed or the spec is changed. When the Go code changes, update the pin
and this list together.

| ID | Cases | Test | Description |
|---|---|---|---|

## By design: Turndown vs html-to-markdown v2 (spec §5.2)

Applied by `internal/fetch/golden_test.go` (`htmlNormalizers`, `htmlKnown`)
to both outputs, in the order listed per case:
`html:html/strip.html` = title, strike, list, escape;
`html:html/table.html` = title, list, table-delim, table-headless.

| ID | Cases | Turndown (TS) | html-to-markdown (cmgo) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `html-title` | `html:html/strip.html`, `html:html/table.html` | Keeps the `<head><title>` text as the first line | Drops `<head>` entirely |
| `html-strike` | `html:html/strip.html` | `~legacy~` | `~~legacy~~` |
| `html-list` | `html:html/strip.html`, `html:html/table.html` | Markers padded to 4 columns (`*   x`, `1.  x`), nested lists indented by 4 | `* x`, `1. x`, nested by 2, plus a whitespace-only line before a nested list |
| `html-escape` | `html:html/strip.html` | Decodes `&lt;` `&gt;` `&amp;`; escapes `\*` and `\]` | Keeps `&lt;Pool&gt;`; leaves `*` and `]` unescaped (both escape `[` and `_`) |
| `html-table-delim` | `html:html/table.html` | Delimiter row `\| --- \| --- \|` | `\|---\|---\|` |
| `html-table-headless` | `html:html/table.html` | A `<table>` without `<th>` stays raw HTML | Rendered as a Markdown table with an empty header row |

Everything else in both pages matches: ATX headings, fenced code with
language, `**bold**`, `_em_`, links with titles, images, `* * *` rules,
hard line breaks, blockquotes, and removal of script/style/nav/header/footer/noscript.

## By design: spec §2.2

No golden case differs because of a §2.2 item, so none is skipped:

- Byte counters / batch `NODE_OPTIONS` preload: batch commands in `tools.json`
  never start `node`, so no `__CM_FS__` line appears on either side.
- Timeline sort (`t11`): matches, but weakly: `t11` is soft-capped to 1
  result per query, so it cannot show ordering across sources. Timeline
  ordering is not covered by the golden set.
- Fetch transport, one-DB layout, no mmap, busy handling, dropped platforms:
  not observable in these outputs.

## Not compared

- `tools.json` masks (stored in the file, applied on both sides): dates
  `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, `<n>ms`, `<n.n>KB`, and the `search calls in <n>s`
  window. KB sizes were checked once unmasked and matched.
- HTML chunks: only the HTML → Markdown text is compared; its chunks follow
  from the Markdown chunker, which `chunks.json` covers.
```

**Files:**
- Test: `internal/store/golden_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ChunkMarkdown(text string, maxBytes int) []Chunk`, `ChunkPlainText(text string, linesPerChunk int) []Chunk`, `ChunkJSON(text string, maxBytes int) ([]Chunk, error)`, `ChunkMaxBytes`, `(*Store).Index(project, label string, chunks []Chunk) (IndexResult, error)`, `(*Store).DistinctiveTerms(sourceID int64, max int) ([]string, error)`, `(*Store).SearchWithFallback(query string, o SearchOpts) ([]SearchResult, error)`, `SanitizeQuery`, `SanitizeTrigramQuery`, `ExtractSnippet(content, query string, maxLen int, highlighted string) string`, test helper `openTest(t)` (store_test.go); `chunks.json`, `sanitize.json`, `search.json`, `snippets.json` from Task 32.

`internal/store/golden_test.go`:

```go
package store

// Golden parity tests (spec §5.2). Expected values come from the TS fork via
// tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs; differences allowed by design are listed with
// their case IDs in testdata/golden/KNOWN-DIFFS.md and in goldenKnown below.

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

const goldenDir = "../../testdata/golden"
const goldenCorpus = "../../testdata/corpus"

// goldenKnown maps a KNOWN-DIFFS.md case ID to its reason. A listed case is
// skipped; every other case must match TS exactly.
var goldenKnown = map[string]string{}

func loadGolden(t *testing.T, name string, v any) {
	t.Helper()
	b, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(goldenDir, name))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, v); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("%s: %v", name, err)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(string(b), `"tsCommit": "a5e6dd31"`) {
		t.Fatalf("%s: not generated from TS commit a5e6dd31", name)
	}
}

// mustCases fails on an empty golden list, which would pass vacuously.
func mustCases(t *testing.T, name string, n int) {
	t.Helper()
	if n == 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%s: no golden cases", name)
	}
}

func skipKnown(t *testing.T, id string) bool {
	t.Helper()
	if why, ok := goldenKnown[id]; ok {
		t.Logf("%s: known diff (%s)", id, why)
		return true
	}
	return false
}

type goldenFile struct {
	Path, Kind string
	Chunks     []Chunk
	Terms      []string
}

// chunkCorpusFile runs the chunker the TS store would use for this kind.
func chunkCorpusFile(t *testing.T, path, kind string) []Chunk {
	t.Helper()
	b, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(goldenCorpus, path))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	switch kind {
	case "markdown":
		return ChunkMarkdown(string(b), ChunkMaxBytes)
	case "plain":
		return ChunkPlainText(string(b), 20)
	case "json":
		c, err := ChunkJSON(string(b), ChunkMaxBytes)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("%s: %v", path, err)
		}
		return c
	}
	t.Fatalf("%s: unknown kind %q", path, kind)
	return nil
}

// goldenStore indexes the corpus in the generator's order, labels = paths.
func goldenStore(t *testing.T, files []goldenFile) (*Store, map[string]int64) {
	t.Helper()
	s := openTest(t)
	ids := map[string]int64{}
	for _, f := range files {
		r, err := s.Index("p", f.Path, chunkCorpusFile(t, f.Path, f.Kind))
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		ids[f.Path] = r.SourceID
	}
	return s, ids
}

func TestGoldenChunks(t *testing.T) {
	var g struct{ Files []goldenFile }
	loadGolden(t, "chunks.json", &g)
	mustCases(t, "chunks.json", len(g.Files))
	for _, f := range g.Files {
		id := "chunks:" + f.Path
		if skipKnown(t, id) {
			continue
		}
		got := chunkCorpusFile(t, f.Path, f.Kind)
		if len(got) != len(f.Chunks) {
			t.Errorf("%s: %d chunks, want %d", id, len(got), len(f.Chunks))
			continue
		}
		for i, w := range f.Chunks {
			if got[i] != w {
				t.Errorf("%s[%d]:\n got %+v\nwant %+v", id, i, got[i], w)
			}
		}
	}
}

func TestGoldenDistinctiveTerms(t *testing.T) {
	var g struct{ Files []goldenFile }
	loadGolden(t, "chunks.json", &g)
	s, ids := goldenStore(t, g.Files)
	for _, f := range g.Files {
		id := "terms:" + f.Path
		if skipKnown(t, id) {
			continue
		}
		got, err := s.DistinctiveTerms(ids[f.Path], 40)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		if !slices.Equal(got, f.Terms) { // nil and [] both mean none
			t.Errorf("%s:\n got %q\nwant %q", id, got, f.Terms)
		}
	}
}

func TestGoldenSanitize(t *testing.T) {
	var g struct {
		Cases []struct{ In, Porter, Trigram string }
	}
	loadGolden(t, "sanitize.json", &g)
	mustCases(t, "sanitize.json", len(g.Cases))
	for _, c := range g.Cases {
		id := "sanitize:" + c.In
		if skipKnown(t, id) {
			continue
		}
		if got := SanitizeQuery(c.In); got != c.Porter {
			t.Errorf("%s porter = %q, want %q", id, got, c.Porter)
		}
		if got := SanitizeTrigramQuery(c.In); got != c.Trigram {
			t.Errorf("%s trigram = %q, want %q", id, got, c.Trigram)
		}
	}
}

type goldenSearchCase struct {
	ID, Query, Source, ContentType, Mode string
	Limit                                int
	Results                              []struct{ Key, ContentType, Highlighted string }
}

func TestGoldenSearch(t *testing.T) {
	var g struct {
		Files []goldenFile
		Cases []goldenSearchCase
	}
	loadGolden(t, "search.json", &g)
	mustCases(t, "search.json", len(g.Cases))
	s, _ := goldenStore(t, g.Files)
	for _, c := range g.Cases {
		id := "search:" + c.ID
		if skipKnown(t, id) {
			continue
		}
		got, err := s.SearchWithFallback(c.Query, SearchOpts{
			Project: "p", Source: c.Source, SourceExact: c.Mode == "exact",
			ContentType: ContentType(c.ContentType), Limit: c.Limit, Mode: "relevance",
		})
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("%s: %v", id, err)
		}
		var gotKeys, wantKeys []string
		for _, r := range got {
			gotKeys = append(gotKeys, r.Source+"::"+r.Title)
		}
		for _, w := range c.Results {
			wantKeys = append(wantKeys, w.Key)
		}
		if !slices.Equal(gotKeys, wantKeys) {
			t.Errorf("%s %q:\n got %q\nwant %q", id, c.Query, gotKeys, wantKeys)
			continue
		}
		for i, w := range c.Results {
			hl := got[i].Highlighted
			if hl == "" { // Go drops marker-free highlights; TS keeps the plain content
				hl = got[i].Content
			}
			if string(got[i].ContentType) != w.ContentType || hl != w.Highlighted {
				t.Errorf("%s[%d] %s: contentType %s want %s; highlighted equal=%v",
					id, i, w.Key, got[i].ContentType, w.ContentType, hl == w.Highlighted)
			}
		}
	}
}

func TestGoldenSnippets(t *testing.T) {
	var g struct {
		Cases []struct {
			ID, Query, Content, Highlighted string
			Snippets                        map[string]string
		}
	}
	loadGolden(t, "snippets.json", &g)
	mustCases(t, "snippets.json", len(g.Cases))
	for _, c := range g.Cases {
		for maxStr, want := range c.Snippets {
			id := "snippet:" + c.ID + "@" + maxStr
			if skipKnown(t, id) {
				continue
			}
			max, err := strconv.Atoi(maxStr)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
			if got := ExtractSnippet(c.Content, c.Query, max, c.Highlighted); got != want {
				t.Errorf("%s %q:\n got %q\nwant %q", id, c.Query, clip(got), clip(want))
			}
		}
	}
}

func clip(s string) string {
	if r := []rune(s); len(r) > 200 {
		return string(r[:100]) + " … " + string(r[len(r)-100:])
	}
	return strings.TrimSpace(s)
}
```

```go
package mcpserver

// Golden parity test for ctx_batch_execute and ctx_search output (spec
// §5.2). tools.json is written by tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs from the TS
// server; this test replays the same calls, in order, against one harness
// per session and compares the masked text. Differences are listed by step
// ID in testdata/golden/KNOWN-DIFFS.md and in goldenToolKnown below.

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"regexp"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

type goldenToolStep struct {
	ID      string
	Session int
	Tool    string
	Args    json.RawMessage
	IsError bool
	Text    string
}

// goldenPin pins the current Go output of a step listed in KNOWN-DIFFS.md:
// goText maps the masked TS text to the masked Go text.
type goldenPin struct {
	reason  string
	isError bool
	goText  func(ts string) string
}

// goldenToolKnown: step ID → pinned Go output for KNOWN-DIFFS.md bug entries.
// Empty: the only entry (legacy-query) was fixed by matching TS.
var goldenToolKnown = map[string]goldenPin{}

func TestGoldenTools(t *testing.T) {
	b, err := os.ReadFile("../../testdata/golden/tools.json")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var g struct {
		TSCommit string
		Masks    []struct{ Re, To string }
		Steps    []goldenToolStep
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &g); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if g.TSCommit != "a5e6dd31" || len(g.Steps) == 0 {
		t.Fatalf("tools.json: commit %q, %d steps", g.TSCommit, len(g.Steps))
	}
	mask := func(s string) string {
		for _, m := range g.Masks {
			s = regexp.MustCompile(m.Re).ReplaceAllString(s, m.To)
		}
		return s
	}

	var h *harness
	session := 0
	for _, st := range g.Steps {
		if st.Session != session { // fresh server, store and flood guard
			session = st.Session
			h = newHarness(t, nil)
			if err := os.CopyFS(filepath.Join(h.Project, "corpus"), os.DirFS("../../testdata/corpus")); err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
		}
		text, isErr := h.call(st.Tool, string(st.Args))
		text = mask(text)
		wantText, wantErr := st.Text, st.IsError
		if pin, ok := goldenToolKnown[st.ID]; ok {
			t.Logf("%s: known diff, Go output pinned (%s)", st.ID, pin.reason)
			if text == st.Text && isErr == st.IsError {
				t.Errorf("%s: now matches TS; drop it from KNOWN-DIFFS.md and goldenToolKnown", st.ID)
			}
			wantText, wantErr = pin.goText(st.Text), pin.isError
		}
		if text != wantText || isErr != wantErr {
			t.Errorf("%s %s %s: isError=%v want %v\n%s", st.ID, st.Tool, st.Args, isErr, wantErr, lineDiff(text, wantText))
		}
	}
}

// lineDiff shows the first differing line with a little context.
func lineDiff(got, want string) string {
	g, w := strings.Split(got, "\n"), strings.Split(want, "\n")
	for i := 0; i < max(len(g), len(w)); i++ {
		var gl, wl string
		if i < len(g) {
			gl = g[i]
		}
		if i < len(w) {
			wl = w[i]
		}
		if gl != wl || i >= len(g) || i >= len(w) {
			return "first diff at line " + itoa(i+1) + ":\n got: " + quoteClip(gl) + "\nwant: " + quoteClip(wl)
		}
	}
	return "(texts equal)"
}

func quoteClip(s string) string {
	if len(s) > 300 {
		s = s[:300] + "…"
	}
	b, _ := json.Marshal(s)
	return string(b)
}

func itoa(n int) string {
	b, _ := json.Marshal(n)
	return string(b)
}
```

### Task 35: HTML to Markdown golden test

**Files:**
- Test: `internal/fetch/golden_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `HTMLToMarkdown(html string) (string, error)`; `html.json` from Task 32; corpus `html/*.html`.
- Produces: `TestGoldenHTMLToMarkdown`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`internal/fetch/golden_test.go`:

```go
package fetch

// Golden parity test for HTML → Markdown (spec §5.2). html.json holds the
// Turndown output from tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs. Turndown and
// html-to-markdown v2 differ on some markup; each difference has an ID in
// testdata/golden/KNOWN-DIFFS.md and a normalizer below that is applied to
// both outputs of exactly the cases listed for it. Anything else must match.

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"os"
	"regexp"
	"slices"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

var (
	reTitle         = regexp.MustCompile(`(?s)<title>(.*?)</title>`)
	reListMarker    = regexp.MustCompile(`(?m)^[ \t]*([*]|\d+\.)[ \t]+`)
	reBlankLine     = regexp.MustCompile(`(?m)^[ \t]+\n`)
	reDelimRow      = regexp.MustCompile(`(?m)^\|(?: ?-{3,} ?\|)+$`)
	reDelimCell     = regexp.MustCompile(` ?-{3,} ?`)
	reHTMLTable     = regexp.MustCompile(`(?s)<table>.*?</table>`)
	reEmptyHeadTbl  = regexp.MustCompile(`(?m)^\|(?:  \|)+\n\|(?:---\|)+(?:\n\|.*\|)+`)
	entityUnescaper = strings.NewReplacer("&lt;", "<", "&gt;", ">", "&amp;", "&")
)

// htmlNormalizers: KNOWN-DIFFS.md ID → normalizer(html, markdown).
var htmlNormalizers = map[string]func(html, md string) string{
	// Turndown keeps the <head><title> text as a first line; html-to-markdown drops <head>.
	"html-title": func(html, md string) string {
		if m := reTitle.FindStringSubmatch(html); m != nil {
			t := strings.TrimSpace(m[1])
			md = strings.TrimLeft(md, " \t")
			if strings.HasPrefix(md, t) {
				md = strings.TrimLeft(strings.TrimPrefix(md, t), " \t\n")
			}
		}
		return md
	},
	// Turndown gfm writes ~x~, html-to-markdown writes ~~x~~.
	"html-strike": func(_, md string) string { return strings.ReplaceAll(md, "~~", "~") },
	// Turndown pads list markers to 4 columns ("*   x", "1.  x") and indents
	// nested lists by 4; html-to-markdown uses "* x", 2-space nesting and a
	// whitespace-only line before a nested list.
	"html-list": func(_, md string) string {
		return reListMarker.ReplaceAllString(reBlankLine.ReplaceAllString(md, ""), "$1 ")
	},
	// Turndown decodes entities and escapes * and ]; html-to-markdown keeps
	// &lt; &gt; &amp; and escapes only [.
	"html-escape": func(_, md string) string {
		md = entityUnescaper.Replace(md)
		return strings.NewReplacer(`\*`, `*`, `\]`, `]`).Replace(md)
	},
	// Table delimiter row: Turndown "| --- |", html-to-markdown "|---|".
	"html-table-delim": func(_, md string) string {
		return reDelimRow.ReplaceAllStringFunc(md, func(row string) string { return reDelimCell.ReplaceAllString(row, "---") })
	},
	// A table without <th>: Turndown keeps the raw HTML, html-to-markdown
	// emits a table with an empty header row. Run after html-table-delim.
	"html-table-headless": func(_, md string) string {
		return reEmptyHeadTbl.ReplaceAllString(reHTMLTable.ReplaceAllString(md, "<TABLE>"), "<TABLE>")
	},
}

// htmlKnown: case ID → KNOWN-DIFFS.md IDs, applied in this order.
var htmlKnown = map[string][]string{
	"html:html/strip.html": {"html-title", "html-strike", "html-list", "html-escape"},
	"html:html/table.html": {"html-title", "html-list", "html-table-delim", "html-table-headless"},
}

func TestGoldenHTMLToMarkdown(t *testing.T) {
	b, err := os.ReadFile("../../testdata/golden/html.json")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var g struct {
		TSCommit string
		Cases    []struct{ Path, Markdown string }
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &g); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if g.TSCommit != "a5e6dd31" || len(g.Cases) == 0 {
		t.Fatalf("html.json: commit %q, %d cases", g.TSCommit, len(g.Cases))
	}
	for _, c := range g.Cases {
		id := "html:" + c.Path
		html, err := os.ReadFile("../../testdata/corpus/" + c.Path)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		got, err := HTMLToMarkdown(string(html))
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("%s: %v", id, err)
		}
		known := htmlKnown[id]
		if slices.Contains(known, "html-table-headless") { // the normalizer hides the cells; check them raw
			for _, row := range []string{"| alpha | 1 |", "| beta | 2 |"} {
				if !strings.Contains(got, row) {
					t.Errorf("%s: header-less table row %q missing:\n%s", id, row, got)
				}
			}
		}
		normalize := func(skip string) (string, string) {
			g, w := got, c.Markdown
			for _, k := range known {
				if k != skip {
					g, w = htmlNormalizers[k](string(html), g), htmlNormalizers[k](string(html), w)
				}
			}
			return g, w
		}
		if g, w := normalize(""); g != w {
			t.Errorf("%s (normalized by %v):\n--- got\n%s\n--- want\n%s", id, known, g, w)
			continue
		}
		for _, k := range known { // every listed diff must still be needed
			if g, w := normalize(k); g == w {
				t.Errorf("%s: %s no longer differs; drop it from KNOWN-DIFFS.md and htmlKnown", id, k)
			}
		}
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

The golden files exist after Task 32, so move them aside for this step:

Run: `mv testdata/golden testdata/golden.off && go test ./internal/fetch/ -run TestGoldenHTMLToMarkdown -count=1 -v`
Expected: FAIL with
```
--- FAIL: TestGoldenHTMLToMarkdown
    golden_test.go:75: open ../../testdata/golden/html.json: no such file or directory
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

No production code: `internal/fetch` is final. The six Turndown vs html-to-markdown differences are the `html-*` IDs in KNOWN-DIFFS.md; each is a normalizer applied to both outputs of the listed cases only, and the test fails if any listed normalizer is no longer needed. Restore the golden files.

Run: `mv testdata/golden.off testdata/golden`

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**

Run: `go test ./internal/fetch/ -run TestGoldenHTMLToMarkdown -count=1 -v`
Expected: PASS

```bash

git commit -m "test(fetch): golden parity for HTML to Markdown"
```

## Appendix A: Porting notes by package

These are deliberate choices made while porting from TS `a5e6dd31`. The ones that change user-visible behavior are also rows in spec §2.2. If a review disagrees with one, change the spec first, then the code.

### store (Tasks 2-9)
- **Data layout:** one DB with a `project` column. No session or attribution columns (sub-project 3 adds them through a `user_version` step), no file-backed source refresh, no mmap.
- **Retention cleanup** is gated by `meta.last_cleanup`. It reads first and takes no write lock unless cleanup is due.
- **WAL:** `journal_mode=WAL` is set once by `ensureWAL`, retried for up to 30 s on `SQLITE_BUSY`. Normal statements rely on `busy_timeout=30000`.
- **Vocabulary** is built from chunk contents, because `Index` takes chunks, not raw text.
- **`ChunkJSON`** returns an error for invalid or empty JSON. Callers fall back to `ChunkPlainText(text, 20)`.
- **Timeline sort** runs the normal relevance pipeline, then stable-sorts by `IndexedAt` (whole seconds).
- **Known small gaps vs JS:**
  - Case mapping differs for a few code points (İ, ß).
  - Lone UTF-16 surrogates become U+FFFD.
  - `DistinctiveTerms` adds `ORDER BY rowid`.
  - Fuzzy-candidate ties follow SQLite's scan order, as in TS.
- **Test values:** tests named `*Golden` and `TestJSNumber` carry values produced by running the TS code.

### security (Tasks 10-13)
- **API addition:** `CheckCodeMatch(language, code) (blocked, cmd, pattern)` and `EmbeddedBlockedMessage(cmd, pattern)` reproduce TS's message for deny hits inside non-shell code (S:1151). `mcpserver` uses them.
- **Case on darwin:** `Read()` deny rules ignore case on darwin. TS did not, which let `.ENV` past `Read(**/.env)` on APFS.
- **Global settings:** only `<home>/.claude/settings.json`. `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and per-adapter global paths are dropped. One `projectDir` is used for both `Bash` and `Read` rules.
- **Settings errors:** unreadable or malformed files are skipped, with one stderr line per file version. The cache key is path + mtime + size.
- **Regexes:** the TS regexes with backreferences are rewritten as quote alternations, with the same test results as TS.

### executor (Tasks 14-18)
- **Env denylist:** exactly the TS name list, plus the `BASH_FUNC_*` and `COMPlus_*` sweeps. `TMPDIR` is set to the script dir.
- **Temp dir:** `os.MkdirTemp(osTempDir(), ".cmgo-")`. On darwin `osTempDir` is `getconf DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR`, on Linux `/tmp`.
- **Runtimes:**
  - Python detection skips the Windows-only `--version` probe.
  - R falls back to `R`.
  - `$SHELL` is honored only for bash, sh, zsh and dash.
- **Rust:**
  - Compiles in the temp dir with the filtered env.
  - Runs the binary in the requested cwd.
  - The compile is capped at the smaller of the timeout and 60 s.
  - A Rust run may be sent to the background.
- **JS/TS wrapper:** keeps `async function __cm_main(){…}`, the `.catch`, and the background keep-alive. The byte counters are dropped (sub-project 4).
- **Output:**
  - Bytes are returned raw; `mcpserver` sanitizes UTF-8.
  - The chunk that crosses the output cap is dropped whole.
  - A spawn failure is returned as a `Result` (exit 1, error in stderr), not as an error.
- **Process groups:**
  - Background groups stay registered until `Shutdown`; their temp dirs are kept, as in TS.
  - Cancelling the caller's context kills a foreground group.

### fetch (Tasks 19-21)
- **SSRF check:** the only check is `net.Dialer.Control`, run on the address actually dialed. Errors are reworded to TS's ssrfGuard text for the first request and to the redirect text for later hops.
- **Strict-mode message:** names `CMGO_FETCH_STRICT=1`.
- **Multiple IPs per name:** if one is blocked, Go tries the next one. Blocked IPs are never connected to.
- **`ClassifyIP`** compares bits, which fixes TS's text-prefix mistakes (`fe8::1`, `::ffff:7f00:1`).
- **Error wording:** DNS and timeout errors use Go's text. "Response too large" reports MaxBody+1. `ErrEmpty` is the sentinel for an empty body.
- **JSON** is pretty-printed with `json.Indent`, so key order and number spelling stay as sent.
- **Decoding:** UTF-8 only; the BOM is dropped and invalid bytes become U+FFFD.
- **HTML to Markdown** differs from Turndown in six documented cases: `testdata/golden/KNOWN-DIFFS.md`.

### mcpserver (Tasks 23-25, Tasks 26-28, Tasks 29-30)
- **Descriptions:** tool descriptions were produced by evaluating the TS template literals, with all 12 languages listed and no Bun note. Sentences about features cmgo lacks were removed:
  - stats;
  - `CONTEXT_MODE_*` tuning;
  - `ctx_index`;
  - session memory, which returns with sub-project 3.
- **Lengths:** the code echo, command echo, label cut and preview lengths count UTF-16 units or runes, as JS does. KB figures use bytes.
- **`ctx_search`:**
  - No legacy `query` parameter. TS strips it, so a call with only `query` gets `Error: provide query or queries.`, and that call is not counted.
  - One flood guard per process.
  - A `limit` of 0 or less gives "No results found."
- **`ctx_batch_execute`:** the TS branch "all timed out with no output" cannot fire, because every command produces a section. It is left out, with a code comment.
- **`ctx_fetch_and_index`:**
  - A cached label is served before the SSRF check runs. Cached content needs no network.
  - The `cap=` header also shows when the job count lowers concurrency, as in TS `runPool`.
  - Argument error texts are Go wording, not zod's.

### golden (Tasks 32-35)
- **Generator:** `tools/golden-gen/gen.mjs` runs by hand on Node against `$TS_REPO` at `a5e6dd31`.
  - It points `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `CONTEXT_MODE_DIR`, `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` and the cwd at a fresh temp dir. It never runs `start.mjs` or postinstall, and never writes `~/.claude` or `$TS_REPO`.
  - The store phase uses `$TS_REPO/node_modules/.bin/tsx`. `extractSnippet` is copied out of `server.ts` at generation time.
- **Cases:**

  | File | Cases |
  |---|---|
  | `chunks.json` | 85 chunks over 16 files, plus 16 term lists |
  | `sanitize.json` | 46 queries |
  | `search.json` | 36 ranked queries |
  | `snippets.json` | 243 snippets |
  | `tools.json` | 19 tool calls |
  | `html.json` | 2 pages |

- **`KNOWN-DIFFS.md`** lists only the six by-design HTML conversion differences. The tests fail if a listed difference disappears.

## Appendix C: Execution notes (2026-10-21)

The plan was executed task by task on branch `impl/core`, with a review at seven checkpoints and a whole-branch review at the end. Every file in Tasks 1-35 matched this plan byte for byte. The real `go get`/`go mod tidy` produced the same `go.mod`/`go.sum` as the draft, and the Node generator produced byte-identical golden files.

**Errata in this plan:**
- **Task 22:** the Step 2 fail-first run cannot compile until `go mod tidy` adds `github.com/google/jsonschema-go` (error: `missing go.sum entry`). Run `go mod tidy` before Step 2, not only in Step 4.
- **Task 24:** `TestExecuteBinaryOutputIsValidUTF8` cannot catch a missing UTF-8 sanitizer. Indexed output returns only titles and previews, and the JSON transport already replaces invalid bytes with U+FFFD. `TestTextResultSanitizesUTF8` (Task 22) is the real guard.
- **Task 32:** the `--stat` table says 61 lines for `testdata/golden/KNOWN-DIFFS.md`; the file has 60.
- **Tasks 34 and 35:** the expected fail-first line numbers are `golden_test.go:42` and `golden_test.go:76`, not 58 and 75.

**Fixed after the whole-branch review** (one commit each, test first):

| Finding | Fix |
|---|---|
| SIGINT/SIGTERM waited for running commands; a later SIGKILL orphaned them | The executor tracks every live process group; `serve` calls `Shutdown` as soon as its context is cancelled |
| `ctx_execute_file` `path` was interpolated as code in Ruby, Perl, PHP and Elixir (also in TS) | Single-quoted literals for Ruby, PHP and Perl; an Elixir byte literal |
| Grandchildren of a foreground run kept running after it returned | The process group is killed once the leader is reaped |
| A NUL byte in a search query caused an SQL error | Both sanitizers strip `\x00` |
| `ctx_execute_file` accepted `path: ""` | Rejected as an argument error |

Final state: 189 tests pass, 0 fail, and `go test -race ./...` is clean. Bench: `phys_footprint` 78.3 MB for 8 servers, 0 bytes written in 60 s idle, no-node PASS.

**Open follow-ups (not fixed):**
- The `vocabulary` table is never pruned by `CleanupIfDue`, and it is shared by all projects.
- Re-indexing a source deletes from FTS by an UNINDEXED column, so it scans the whole table under the write lock (about 110 ms at 100k chunks).
- `Shutdown` signals recorded background pgids that may have exited long ago; after PID reuse this could reach an unrelated group (TS behaves the same).
- `prepareCode` still double-quotes the Elixir `_build` path from the configured project dir.
